# Knowledge Base Index

One-line summaries of the evergreen docs. The documentation phase appends here.

## Decisions

- [0001](decisions/0001-stack-electron-react-typescript.md) — Stack choice: Electron, React, TypeScript, Zustand, transport in the background process.
- [0002](decisions/0002-remote-head-over-relay-shared-wire.md) — Remote head over the relay, reusing the mobile wire contract.
- [0003](decisions/0003-m3-theme-tokens-css-custom-properties.md) — M3 theme values as CSS custom properties in a single `tokens.css` (dark-only, `system-ui` fallback).
- [0004](decisions/0004-renderer-session-store-reducer-wire-types.md) — Renderer session state: one Zustand store, a pure exported reducer, sealed action union, wire types reused verbatim.
- [0005](decisions/0005-secret-at-rest-safestorage-fail-closed.md) — Secrets at rest via Electron `safeStorage`, fail-closed (no plaintext fallback), over one key-domain-blind byte primitive with injected keychain/filesystem seams.
- [0006](decisions/0006-ephemeral-screen-state-usereducer-not-store.md) — Ephemeral, screen-local state uses `useReducer` + a pure reducer (resets on remount), not the module-singleton store of 0004; the store shape is reserved for app-wide shared state.
- [0007](decisions/0007-content-free-diagnostics-by-construction.md) — Content-free diagnostics by construction: the logger accepts only a typed allowlist of *envelope* fields (event, code, status, bytes, count, host, path, the additive `hash?` a decode boundary uses — #130, the additive **branded** `safeBytes?` a pre-decryption failure boundary uses — #133, and the additive plain-string `appVersion?`/`noiseProtocol?`/`protocolVersion?` a once-per-session startup banner uses — #132), enforced by the type system at every call site — never a runtime scrubber (a denylist that fails open). Grounds Bucket 1 (#126) + the #125/#127/#128/#130/#132/#133 family; #131 extends the same fail-closed allowlist to the untrusted renderer→main IPC boundary (its pin relaxes to `Omit<DiagnosticEvent,'safeBytes'>` after #133's branded field, then extends again for #132's three main-only version fields — proof the `Omit` escape hatch isn't only for branded types); #134 is the first renderer-**state** producer through that boundary, fitting entirely inside the existing `event`/`code`/`count` fields with no allowlist growth. #132 is the last of the five #125 Bucket-1 splits to ship.
- [0008](decisions/0008-thread-timeline-model.md) — Conversation-timeline model: a heterogeneous `ThreadItem` union (`assistantText`/`toolCall`/`turnBoundary`/`userText`) + pure `reduceTimeline` reducer, introduced alongside `sessionStore`'s `MessagePayload[]` (Strangler Fig). Tail-check delta coalescing per turn; a `toolResult` merges into its `toolCall` by `toolUseId`, orphan/duplicate → same-reference no-op; `turn_state` is a scalar `phase` beside `items`, not a timeline row. Foundation for #199 (transport, shipped), #202 (store + bridge, shipped), #203 (render, shipped), #214 (`turn_state` transport, shipped), #217 (`tool_use` transport, shipped), #218 (`toolCall` pending render, shipped), #229 (`tool_result` transport, shipped), #230 (resolved-row render, shipped), and [#179](codebase/179.md) (shipped — the `interactive` flip + the Strangler-Fig cutover). [#245](codebase/245.md) added the fourth member, `userText`, dormant with a placeholder render arm; [#179](codebase/179.md) wired its producer (the composer echo, retargeted from `sessionStore` to `timelineStore`) and its real render row, and in the same commit retired the coarse `MessageThread` — `Timeline` is now the conversation's single thread surface, with `MessageThread`/`MessageBubble`/`selectMessages` kept as dead-but-tested residue. The vertical is complete. [#286](codebase/286.md) added a fifth member, `sessionBoundary` — the `/clear`/idle-eviction/workspace-change delimiter (transport widened by the split-sibling [#285](codebase/285.md)) — wired live end to end (bridge arm + `TimelineRow` render row + a new long-form-relative-time `sessionBoundaryViewModel.ts`) in one ticket, the fifth application of the "new timeline-item kind → bridge arm → render row" pattern. [#317](codebase/317.md) extended the model with a second scalar beside `phase` — `stalled: boolean`, set by a new nullary `stallDetected` arm and self-cleared by the four turn-activity arms (`assistantDelta`/`toolUse`/`toolResult`/`turnState`), carried through unchanged by the three non-activity arms — the `phase`-beside-`items` precedent generalized to a second onset-only scalar. [#493](codebase/493.md) extended the model with a third scalar, `apiRetry: ApiRetryStatus | null` (a record, not a flag, set by a data-carrying `apiRetry` arm) — the deliberate **inverse** of `stalled`'s clearing rule: the four turn-activity arms carry it through unchanged instead of clearing it, since the wire's `api_retry` frame has an explicit falling edge rather than requiring a client-side self-clear.
- [0009](decisions/0009-modal-prompt-model.md) — Modal-prompt model: an id-addressed ordered `ModalPrompt[]` + pure `reduceModal` reducer, introduced alongside `sessionStore`/`threadTimeline` (Strangler Fig, nothing cut over). `shown` is idempotent on `modalId` ([#195](codebase/195.md), shipped: replace-in-place if outstanding, no-op if already `resolved`, append if never seen); `dismissed` removes by `modalId` and records it `resolved` on a genuine removal, unknown/already-dismissed id → same-reference no-op, untouched-`resolved` ordering-edge guard. `class` is `permission | trust` only — no `destructive` wire class. The modal analog of 0008; foundation for the peeled wire/transport/render/answer follow-up. Reconnect-reconcile #196 split 2-way: [#415](codebase/415.md) (shipped) adds a `reconnected` `ModalEvent` arm clearing `outstanding` on every supervisor (re)handshake while preserving `resolved`/`rejections` by reference, letting the daemon's connect-time re-sends repopulate through the existing idempotent `shown` arm; sibling [#197](codebase/197.md) (shipped) applies the same reset contract to the separate [queue store](features/queue-store.md)'s `backlogs` map, via a `resetBacklogs` setter + a `connected`-edge branch in `subscribeQueue` rather than a translator arm, since the queue bridge has no event union to extend. **[#510](codebase/510.md) (shipped, security-sensitive) deliberately reverses #415's `resolved`-preservation**: an answer clicked while disconnected is dispatched optimistically but the send is swallowed by the transport, so the retained `resolved` id suppressed the daemon's genuine re-delivery and let an explicit Allow silently decay into a deny-on-timeout. The daemon's reconcile re-sends only still-outstanding modals, so retaining `resolved` across the reset guarded nothing; the `reconnected` arm now clears both `outstanding` and `resolved`, each behind its own no-change guard, and `resolved` is per-connection memory rather than permanent. The transport slice ([#201](codebase/201.md), shipped) wired the wire types + fail-closed decode + `DaemonEvent` arms this model's `ModalEvent` targets — both existing renderer bridges discard the arms as `null`; the [modal store + bridge](features/modal-store-bridge.md) that actually consumes them shipped in [#223](codebase/223.md). ADR amended 2026-09-01 ([#870](codebase/870.md)): `modal_shown` now carries an outbound-scoping `conversation_id` (pyrycode#1065), retiring two "wire carries none" premises in § Context without changing § Decision/Rationale/Consequences — `modalId` stays the sole correlation key for an *answer*. Amended again 2026-09-01 ([#878](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-desktop/issues/878), shipped, security-sensitive): the id no longer stops at `reduceModal` — it is copied by name onto the held `ModalPrompt` and read by `selectHasOutstandingFor(conversationId)`, a new selector factory answering whether any outstanding prompt belongs to a conversation via an `===` scan (never a keyed container, so a `'__proto__'`-named id has no prototype hazard to exploit). [#873](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-desktop/issues/873) (shipped) is the resolver branch that consumes this boolean, as `resolveConversationStatus`'s leading `inputRequired` parameter; [#874](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-desktop/issues/874) (shipped) composed `selectHasOutstandingFor` at the sidebar's one production call site.
- [0010](decisions/0010-markdown-renderer-capability-absent-html.md) — Assistant markdown rendering: `react-markdown@10` with no `rehypePlugins`/`remarkPlugins`, default `skipHtml`, and a non-spreading `img` override that suppresses image elements entirely — chosen over the far-smaller `markdown-to-jsx` because its raw-HTML support is capability-**absent** (would need a new dependency to enable) rather than merely disabled by a boolean default that could regress. `code`'s `language-*` class is deliberately kept (Figma's fenced-code label carrier). Realized dormant in [#608](codebase/608.md); consumer is #609 (container) and [#623](codebase/623.md) (fenced-code language label). The `a` override shipped deny-first alongside `img` but was narrowed to an `http:`/`https:` allowlist by [#610](codebase/610.md) — see [Assistant markdown renderer](features/assistant-markdown-renderer.md) below.

## Features

- [Conversation shell](features/conversation-shell.md) — the renderer's first screen: a scrollable message thread above a bottom-pinned composer, mirrored from the mobile Conversation Thread design (#1). The thread now renders the live session store — streamed daemon replies appear as they arrive, adapted from wire `MessagePayload` at the store-read boundary via `toMessageViewModel` (#69); the composer is now wired to send with an optimistic echo (#66, see below). Gained a slim header row above the thread — the seed of a future top app bar — holding a confirm-guarded `UnpairControl`: on `window.pyry.unpair()` → `ok` it dispatches the store's new `reset` action then fires the App-level `onUnpaired` flip back to pairing; on `error` (or a rejected invoke) it reuses the composer's existing error affordance and stays put (#166, see below). Gained a `StatusRow` trigger between the thread and the composer plus an exported pure `StatusSheet` host modal — the "Run configuration" sheet's chrome only (drag handle, title, `×` close, empty scrollable body), opened/closed by a single `useState` boolean (ADR 0006), overlaid inside `.conversation` with no portal; renders no live summary or sections yet, unblocking #72 and hosting the #181 (since split into #187/#188)/#182 (since split into #191/#192) follow-ups (#177, see below); all four read-only sections (Model/Effort/YOLO #188, Context window #192) have since landed. `StatusSheet` gained an optional `children` prop and mounted its first section, `LogDataSection` — a "Log data" header + full-width filled-tonal Download button driving the debug-bundle download via a pure, total, phase-agnostic `useReducer` state machine (idle/downloading+count/saved-path/failed-reason) over the three already-typed `debugBundle*` daemon events, closing the #71 debug-bundle family end to end; the existing shell-only sheet test needed zero edits (#72, see below). Gained a **proactive** re-pair affordance beneath the composer — an exported pure `RepairPrompt({status,onRepair})` gated on the new `shouldOfferRepair` predicate + an in-file `RepairControl` container that reuses #166's `runUnpair` verbatim (no second clear path); self-hides on both outcomes, no confirm/busy phase, closing the live 2026-07-07 dead-composer incident (#120) proactively rather than requiring the manual `Unpair` (#167, see below). Gained a second, structured-stream thread — `Timeline({items})`, `MessageThread`'s twin, mounted beside it and reading the [conversation timeline store](features/conversation-timeline-store.md)'s `selectItems` — rendering each `assistantText` `ThreadItem` as a daemon-style bubble (array index as React key, justified by the reducer's append-only/tail-mutation invariant) with a streaming cursor (`▎`) derived structurally from array position on the in-progress tail; `toolCall`/`turnBoundary` draw nothing via an exhaustive no-`assertNever` switch. Strangler-Fig beside the untouched coarse thread — returns `null` on empty items for zero layout footprint, inert until #179 flips `interactive` (which will also need to reconcile the coarse thread's now-empty region, left open for #179) (#203, see below). Is now the **thread view** of the [paired shell](features/paired-shell.md)'s `list ⇄ thread` router — the paired region enters at a list first, opening a conversation mounts this screen; gained a leading `BackControl` (optional `onBack?`, the `onUnpaired?` precedent) returning to the list (#140, see below). Gained `Timeline`'s structural twin over the store's coarse `phase` scalar, `ThinkingIndicator` — a "Thinking…" affordance mounted right after `Timeline`, covering the pre-text window the daemon opens with `turn_state{thinking}` before any assistant delta; takes a boolean `isThinking` prop (never `phase`), making "no daemon-supplied string rendered" a type-level guarantee; inert until #179 flips `interactive` (#215, see below). `TimelineRow`'s `toolCall` arm gained its pending render — a compact bordered chip carrying the tool name and its one-line input summary, replacing the earlier no-op, at 50% opacity for the unresolved (`result: null`) state, `data-thread-role="tool"` keeping it out of the daemon bubble count; inert until #179 flips `interactive` (#218, see below). Gained an interactive permission/trust modal, `PermissionModal`/`PermissionModalView` — a centered M3 dialog, mounted last child of `.conversation`, rendering the [modal store](features/modal-store-bridge.md)'s oldest `outstanding` prompt (title, prompt, ordered inert option buttons, the fail-safe `defaultOptionId` marked via a `--default` modifier class), reusing `StatusSheet`'s overlay+scrim structure but centered under a distinct class set; also mounts the previously-dormant `useModalBridge` at App level (the third independent `onDaemonEvent` subscriber, beside `useDaemonEventBridge`/`useTimelineBridge`) so the store finally populates from live events; inert until #179 flips `interactive` (#224, see below). Its option buttons and a new leading Cancel affordance became **answerable**: each dispatches `answerModalCommand`/`cancelModalCommand` (#236) through a new pure `modalResolution.ts` helper (the `composerSend.ts` precedent — guarded send, unconditional local clear, tested with plain spies since the `node` test env can't fire clicks), then clears the resolved prompt locally via the existing `dismissed` reducer arm — no new store representation, no new event arm; closes the modal vertical (only #179 remains). A code-review SHOULD-FIX flagged the Cancel button's far-left placement (`margin-right: auto`) as diverging from the Figma Dialogs reference's trailing-edge grouping — non-blocking, unresolved, flagged for confirmation before #179 (#237, see below). `TimelineRow`'s `toolCall` arm now resolves that pending chip in place once `item.result` fills — `tool-row--resolved` lifts the pending dimming (both outcomes), `tool-row--error` layers on top iff `result.isError`, tinting the chip border with a newly-introduced `--color-error` token (M3 default dark error role, tone 80 — desktop's first error-family token); `result.resultSummary` deliberately not surfaced. The vertical's last render slice — only #179 (the `interactive` flip) remains (#230, see below). [#179](codebase/179.md) shipped that flip: `loadDialConfig` now advertises `capabilities: [CAPABILITY_INTERACTIVE]`, turning on everything above in one commit. The same commit retargets the composer's echo from `sessionStore` into `timelineStore` as a `userText` event, draws it as the right-aligned user bubble in `TimelineRow` (replacing the #245 `null` placeholder), and retires the `MessageThread` mount — `Timeline` is now the conversation's **single** thread surface, with `MessageThread`/`MessageBubble`/`selectMessages` kept as dead-but-tested residue. Every "inert until #179" render slice above (`Timeline`, `ThinkingIndicator`, the tool-call rows, `PermissionModal`) is now live. `PermissionModal` then gained a client-side second-confirm gate: selecting a non-default option now holds pending a `Back`/`Confirm` sub-step (a pure `selectOption` gate on `defaultOptionId`, transient container `useState`, no wire/store change — ADR 0009 has no `destructive` class) ahead of the still-ungated default-option/Cancel send ([#226](codebase/226.md), see below); the pending marker was originally keyed by a bare option id, which [#511](codebase/511.md) (shipped, security-sensitive) later found could carry a held confirm onto a *different* same-class prompt when `outstanding[0]` swapped mid-confirm — daemon option ids are a closed per-class vocabulary, not per-prompt nonces, so a stale id was guaranteed to match — and re-keyed to `{modalId, optionId}` with a new pure `resolvePendingOption`, re-deriving every render so a stale marker is inert rather than needing a clearing effect. `PermissionModal` then gained a **rejection surface**: since the answer path clears the prompt optimistically, a new `RejectionSurfaceView` — a transient, dismissible "Your answer was rejected." banner stack, keyed by the content-free `modalId` nonce, `role="alert"` — renders whenever the [modal store](features/modal-store-bridge.md)'s new `rejections` slice is non-empty, independent of `outstanding`; not security-sensitive, no bespoke Figma design yet (#249, see below). `Timeline`'s empty branch (#203's `return null`) is now a purposeful pre-first-message empty state — an in-file `EmptyThread` (a distinct `conversation__empty` surface, never `.conversation__thread`, no `data-thread-role`, so the #179 split-brain guard stays green) rendering a decorative `aria-hidden` chat glyph plus a module-level client-owned copy constant, `EMPTY_THREAD_COPY` (the `ThinkingIndicator` label idiom, making "no daemon string" structural); visually distinct from the icon-less `.channel-list__empty` (#141) by class, glyph, and centered-fill vs top-padded layout; no Figma frame exists for this state (mobile file draws only the populated thread) so copy/glyph ship as client-owned defaults; leaves the #278 workspace-chip seam open, pinning to the top of this same surface, not built here (#277, see [codebase notes](codebase/277.md)). Gained a third, prominent read of the same `ConnectionStatus` slice: a disconnected-only banner across the top of the thread, mounted between `UnpairControl` and `Timeline` — `shouldShowBanner`/`CONNECTION_BANNER_COPY` sit in `composerSend.ts` beside `composerAvailability`/`shouldOfferRepair`; mirrors the `RepairPrompt`/`RepairControl` split; distinct from both the composer's terse hint and the separate, not-built-here #149 two-dot indicator; no daemon string reaches it (structural guarantee, tested) (#279, see [codebase notes](codebase/279.md)). Gained a fifth `ThreadItem` render row, the session-boundary delimiter — a monospace title over a full-width horizontal rule (Figma 16-35) marking where a `/clear`, an idle eviction, or a workspace change started a fresh session; `TimelineRow`'s new `sessionBoundary` case carries **no `data-thread-role`** (AC4), its title derived by a new pure `sessionBoundaryViewModel.ts` (a long-form-relative-time sibling of `formatLastActivity`, not a reuse — the Figma copy is `2 hours ago`, not `2h ago`) fed a container-captured `now` threaded through `Timeline`/`TimelineRow` (both defaulting to `Date.now()` so no existing test call site needed an edit); the explanatory sentence + `Install` affordance (Figma 16-38) are deferred with the memory-plugin subsystem (#286, see [codebase notes](codebase/286.md)). Gained a render of the [queue store](features/queue-store.md)'s held backlog — `QueuedBacklog`/`QueuedBacklogControl`, a fourth pure-view-plus-in-file-container pair (the `ThinkingIndicator`/`ConnectionBanner` shape), mounted after `ThinkingIndicator` and before `StatusRow` as the not-yet-run tail; empty → `null` (no region, no chrome); non-empty → one dimmed, right-aligned `bubble--user` row per `QueuedItem` in enqueue order, `data-thread-role="queued"` (distinct from delivered `"user"`), `text` auto-escaped never `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`; reads the milestone conversation only (`MILESTONE_CONVERSATION_ID`, no nav plumbing added); new `.conversation__queued` region rule at 50% opacity, the `.tool-row` pending-dimming precedent reused rather than a new token (#294, see [codebase notes](codebase/294.md)). Closed the #277 workspace-chip seam: a pre-first-message `WorkspaceChip` mounted as a sibling above `Timeline`, below `ConnectionBannerControl` — the exact `ThinkingIndicator` idiom (container derives, pure view self-gates to `null`) — showing the workspace `cwd` a new (unpromoted, empty-thread) discussion will run in, gone once the first message lands; a new `activeConversationStore` (the `sessionIdStore`/`conversationListStore` DI-factory→singleton→hook→selector shape, held verbatim) snapshots the `conversationCreated` payload that [paired shell](features/paired-shell.md)'s nav callback already received and previously dropped; `cwd` renders as auto-escaped React children, never split/basenamed/interpreted as a path; the "Change" affordance ships `disabled={!onChange}` as both the AC placeholder and the #157 Workspace Picker's future extension seam, the same shape #276 reserves for #155's `onChannelInfo?`; no new CSS token, not security-sensitive (#278, see [codebase notes](codebase/278.md)). `QueuedBacklog` gained a required `onDrop` prop and a per-row drop/cancel affordance — a leading-sibling icon-only button, client-owned `aria-label="Drop queued message"`, dispatching a new pure `dropQueuedMessage` helper (`cancelPrompt` minus the local dispatch, no rename) that fires `dequeueMessageCommand` (#300) and nothing else, so removal is never optimistic — the row leaves only on the next `queue_state` snapshot; the button inherits `.conversation__queued`'s 50% dimming (a child opacity cannot escape a parent's opacity compositing group), shipped dimmed by design; closes the queue-drop family end to end (#296, see [codebase notes](codebase/296.md)). Gained `ThinkingIndicator`'s twin, `StallIndicator({isStalled})`, mounted as its sibling right after it — a client-owned "The turn seems to have stalled…" bubble built from `--color-error` (a leading accent border + tinted text, distinct from `.bubble--thinking`'s muted treatment), shown on the timeline store's new `stalled` scalar and self-clearing (in the reducer, not here) on the next turn activity; boolean prop, not the store type, so "no daemon string rendered" is a type-level guarantee (#317, see [codebase notes](codebase/317.md)). Gained a second `ThinkingIndicator` twin, `ApiRetryIndicator({retry})`, mounted right after `ThinkingIndicator` (before `StallIndicator`) over a third timeline-store scalar, `apiRetry: ApiRetryStatus | null` — a client-owned "API error — retrying…" bubble, `.bubble--daemon` diverged to the error role but without `.bubble--stall`'s left accent bar, plus a client-formatted `attempt N/M` counter shown only when `total > 0` (never a computed fraction, never a literal `"0/0"`); clears only on the daemon's explicit falling edge, never on turn activity, the deliberate inverse of `stalled`. Closes the mutual-exclusion question #317 deferred: a new exported `shouldShowThinking({phase, apiRetry})` predicate narrows `ThinkingIndicator`'s own gate (`phase === 'thinking' && apiRetry === null`) so a live retry supersedes the generic thinking indicator, while stall and retry may still co-render (#493, see [codebase notes](codebase/493.md)). Gained a **second** `ThinkingIndicator` supersede peer, `CompactingIndicator({isCompacting})`, mounted right after `ApiRetryIndicator` (before `StallIndicator`) over a fourth timeline-store scalar, `compacting: boolean` — a client-owned "Compacting the conversation…" bubble that, unlike the two error-role siblings, pairs `.bubble--thinking`'s muted text with a **primary**-role left accent bar (compaction is claude working normally, not a problem), completing a four-way text-role × left-bar matrix with every cell distinct; `shouldShowThinking` extended to `({phase, apiRetry, compacting})` with one added clause (`&& !compacting`), the exact one-field/one-clause seam #493 built by name for this ticket; clears only on the daemon's explicit falling edge, surviving turn activity, `apiRetry`'s inversion again but a plain boolean (no counter on the wire to discard); may still co-render with stall and retry, only thinking is excluded (#496, see [codebase notes](codebase/496.md)). Gained the thread top app bar's trailing overflow menu (Figma 16-16) — `ThreadOverflowMenuView`/`ThreadOverflowMenu`, the `RepairPrompt`/`RepairControl` pure-view/in-file-container split, mounted right after `BackControl` and gated the same way (`onBack` presence, gated at the mount site rather than self-gated so the container's `useState`/`useRef`/`useEffect` stay unconditional); the trigger advertises `aria-haspopup="menu"`/`aria-expanded`, opening a `role="menu"` surface with one enabled, no-op-routed `role="menuitem"` ("Channel info") — the documented extension slot #155 wires to open the Channel Info sheet (node 20-48); open/closed is screen-local `useState` (ADR 0006, resets on remount, never the store); Escape and outside-click both dismiss and return focus to the trigger via a `document`-listener effect scoped to the open state; no z-index needed, since DOM order alone keeps it above the message thread and below the later-mounted status-sheet/permission-modal overlays (#276, see [codebase notes](codebase/276.md)). Gained a screen-snapshot request + display, mounted between `StatusRow` and `InterruptControl`: `ScreenSnapshotView`/`ScreenSnapshotControl`, the `InterruptButton`/`InterruptControl` triad but bundling the request button and the display region into one exported view (action + display ship as one `s`, per the ticket's sizing note); the new `requestScreenSnapshot.ts` fires the **existing** `requestSnapshot` command (no new command/IPC) with the `sendInterrupt` guarded-send posture; the display discriminates on `snapshot === null` — a placeholder `<p>` (no snapshot yet) vs. a `<pre>` holding the [screen-snapshot store](features/screen-snapshot-store.md)'s held `text` (even when `''`), auto-escaped, never `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`; always visible (never `null` at rest), CSS-bounded (`max-height`+`overflow:auto`) so a large screen dump scrolls internally; no Figma coverage (#324, see [codebase notes](codebase/324.md)) — **removed in [#618](codebase/618.md)**: the feature it exposed (photographing claude's terminal) was deleted upstream, so the button rendered enabled and did nothing; the store and bridge stay, reader-less. Closed the #149 two-dot family end to end: `StatusRow`'s previously-empty `.status-row__summary` slot (now a flex row, coexisting with #181/#182's future run-config text) gained `ConnectionStatusIndicatorControl` — two labelled, colour-coded dots, one per connection leg, mirroring mobile's `ConnectionStatusLine`; two exported pure mapping functions, `relayLeg`/`daemonLeg` (the `isTurnRunning` shape, exhaustive switch, no `default`, TS2366-guarded), turn each leg's raw status into a `{category: 'up'|'in-progress'|'down', label}` pair — `daemon-absent` reads relay-up/"Reachable" since a missing daemon behind a reachable relay is the daemon leg's story, and the daemon dot reaches up only on `connected` (never `connecting`, no false green); the two legs are never cross-referenced, so after a fatal session close the relay dot legitimately stays up while the daemon dot goes down, by design; a new `--color-warning` token (`#ffca45`, no M3 warning role exists) drives the in-progress dot, `.conn-dot`/`.conn-leg__label` land in `conversation.css` (#330, see [codebase notes](codebase/330.md)). [#719](codebase/719.md) later reversed one arm of that mapping: the relay leg's `null` sentinel ("no status has arrived yet") now reads its own `unknown`/`Relay Unknown` category — neutral `--color-outline` — instead of being collapsed into `down`/`Relay Offline`, since [#718](codebase/718.md) put these dots on the sidebar from the first frame of every launch and the old collapse made a cold start look like an outage; `daemonLeg` is untouched. Made the #276 overflow menu's "Channel info" item live: `ChannelInfoSheetView`/`ChannelInfoSheet` — a second `StatusSheet`-chrome bottom sheet (Figma 20-48), open-state a new screen-local `channelInfoOpen` `useState` in `ConversationScreen` itself, **retiring** the speculative `ConversationScreenProps.onChannelInfo?` prop #276 had reserved for a `PairedShell`-driven design that was never built; renders the `activeConversationStore` conversation's About detail (Workspace `cwd` mono + Last activity via `formatLastActivity`) and a Channel ID footer, or a graceful placeholder + no footer when the store holds `null` (a list-opened thread); ships an empty `.channel-info__actions` slot for #366/#367/#368 to fill; no transport/IPC/wire (#365, see [codebase notes](codebase/365.md)). The slot's first filler, a Rename tonal pill (Figma 20:89), landed in #368: gated on an `onRename?` callback the container supplies only for a non-null active conversation (no button on the list-opened-thread case, mirroring #365's empty-About branch); activating it seeds and opens the existing [Rename dialog](features/rename-conversation-dialog.md) (#360) via a screen-local `useState` pair the container owns, dispatching the already-shipped `renameConversation` command (#359) on Save. The one production line outside `ConversationScreen.tsx`: `requestRenameConversation`'s `row` param narrowed `ConversationSummary` → `Pick<ConversationSummary, 'id'>` (its real input) so the sheet's `ConversationCreatedPayload` passes directly, no adapter, no cast, zero fan-out at the existing `ChannelList` call site. Renderer-contained, not security-sensitive, code review PASS with no findings (#368, see [codebase notes](codebase/368.md)). The slot's second filler, an Archive tonal pill (Figma 20:94), landed in #366, one merge after #368: reuses `.channel-info__action` verbatim (no new CSS) and the same `onArchive?`-callback-gate shape as Rename, but dispatches directly instead of opening a dialog — a new exported `requestArchiveConversation` helper (a structural clone of `requestUnarchiveConversation`) fires the already-shipped, previously-dormant [`archiveConversation` command](features/conversation-archive.md) (#363) with `{conversation_id}`, fire-and-forget, then the handler calls the container's existing `onClose`; button order is Rename → Archive → the future Delete (#367), destructive-last. The archived row leaving the active list needs no new code, riding the existing #275 `conversation_updated` re-list path the restore flow (#346/#348) already proved in reverse. Renderer-contained, not security-sensitive, code review PASS with no findings (#366, see [codebase notes](codebase/366.md)). The slot's third and final filler, a Delete pill (Figma 20:96), landed in #377, completing the Rename/Archive/Delete trio: unlike its siblings, dispatch is two-step — `onDelete` opens an inline confirm (a client-owned invention, no Figma node, the #226 second-confirm posture) that swaps in for the pill in the same actions column; confirming fires a new `requestDeleteConversation` helper (an `requestArchiveConversation` clone) against the already-shipped, previously-dormant [`deleteConversation` command](features/conversation-delete.md) (#364) with `{conversation_id}`, then closes the sheet; cancelling flips back to the pill with no wire effect. A new `.channel-info__action--danger` modifier layers an error-tinted outline (`box-shadow: inset 0 0 0 1px var(--color-error)`, since desktop has no error-container fill token) onto the base pill for both the Delete pill and its confirm button. The confirm's own open state (`deleteConfirmOpen`) is screen-local `useState`, the `renameOpen` twin; `window.pyry` is dereferenced only inside the confirm handler. The deleted row leaving the list is #376's job (already merged), not this ticket's. Renderer-contained, not security-sensitive, code review PASS with one non-blocking NIT (focus drops to `document.body` on the pill→confirm swap) (#377, see [codebase notes](codebase/377.md)). The `WorkspaceChip.onChange` seam (#278) landed its consumer in #383: a new `WorkspacePickerSheet.tsx` — the `ChannelInfoSheetView`/`ChannelInfoSheet` chrome-clone + callback-gating pattern, a third `.status-sheet__*` sheet — lists the [recent-workspaces store](features/recent-workspaces-store.md) (#382), marks the row matching the active conversation's `cwd` with a "default" pill, and dispatches the existing `changeWorkspace` command (#379, [conversation workspace change](features/conversation-workspace-change.md)) on selection via a new `requestChangeWorkspace` helper that maps the row's `path` into the wire's `cwd` field; the daemon's `conversation_updated` reply reflects the change into the list for free (#273/#275), no optimistic update. Both #382's store/bridge and #379's command — each shipped dormant naming this picker as their intended caller — went live with zero changes to either. The "Other" create-folder entry ships present-but-disabled (`onCreateFolder` omitted), the extension seam #384 fills. Renderer-contained, not security-sensitive, code review PASS with two non-gating NITs (inline glyph coloring vs. Figma's accent colors; the generic create-folder label vs. Figma's parent-specific one, both deferred by design) (#383, see [codebase notes](codebase/383.md)). That seam landed in #398: a new `CreateFolderDialog.tsx` (Figma 19-44, a `RenameConversationDialog` clone retitled "Create workspace") mounts the [round-trip store](features/new-folder-store.md)'s previously-dormant bridge dialog-scoped, dispatches `createWorkspaceFolder` (#381) under the active conversation's `cwd`, and on the `created` outcome switches the conversation to the daemon's returned path verbatim (the #288 EvalSymlinks lesson) via `requestChangeWorkspace`, closing both the dialog and the picker; a `rejected` outcome shows a generic apostrophe-free line and stays open for retry. The picker's create-folder label is now parent-specific ("Create new folder under &lt;cwd&gt;"), completing the #384 split. Renderer-contained, not security-sensitive, code review PASS with one non-blocking SHOULD-FIX (the new rejected-state error line lacks `role="alert"`) and a NIT (a benign lazy-reference import cycle between the dialog and the picker) (#398, see [codebase notes](codebase/398.md)). Gained a clear path on `activeConversationStore`, `clearActiveConversation` — returns the state to `initialActiveConversationState`, a named setter beside `setActiveConversation` rather than a reducer conversion, sourced from the exported constant; shipped with no production caller (#529, see [codebase notes](codebase/529.md)); [#530](codebase/530.md) (navigation, shipped) touched the same `PairedShell.tsx` call site but deliberately left `clearActiveConversation` uncalled — `setActiveConversation` stays unconditional there, clearing the timeline and session id instead via the new `activateConversation` helper; [#531](codebase/531.md) (unpair / pair-another-server, shipped) is `clearActiveConversation`'s sole caller, wired unconditionally into the new `clearPairingScopedState` helper. Split from #568 alongside #582 (truncation/cap reports, open) and #583 (latest patch, open); visual design is #580's (open), interim chrome is the shared `.status-sheet__*` vocabulary. Security-sensitive (architect self-review PASS — untrusted, model-influenced shell-command text rendered as auto-escaped inert text only, non-interactive rows, no list-shaped derivation), code review PASS with zero findings. The Model section's `MODEL_CATALOG` (#188) gained a fourth entry — `Fable 5` / `newest in the Fable family`, family token `fable`, inserted between Sonnet and Haiku — reaching a published model family the sheet previously made unselectable; the round-trip proof reuses `entry.family`'s existing dual role (submitted token + `matchedFamily` match key) with no new export, no CSS and no JSX change, split from #561 which is blocked on `pyrycode#1646` and will delete this hardcoded catalog once daemon-derived rows land. Security-sensitive (architect self-review PASS, zero findings), code review PASS with one non-blocking NIT (a comment line-length note; this repo runs no formatter) (#590, see [codebase notes](codebase/590.md)). Gained a scroll-follow pin on `.conversation__thread`: stays pinned to the bottom on a newly arriving item only if the operator was already there, decided by a screen-local flag written from the container's own scroll events and re-asserted in a dependency-free layout effect after every render, so sibling chrome mounting/unmounting can never un-pin a thread the operator never scrolled (#601, built on the dormant `isAtBottom` helper from #600; see [codebase notes](codebase/601.md)). The assistant-text bubble gained a dedicated `.bubble--assistant-text` modifier (`white-space: pre-wrap`) so a multi-paragraph reply keeps its blank lines and space runs instead of collapsing to one run-on line — hung on its own class rather than `.bubble--daemon` so the four chrome affordances and the coarse path's `.bubble--user` are structurally unreachable by the rule, and deliberately kept once markdown lands (#608/#609/#610) since the in-progress streaming-cursor tail always renders as plain text. Not security-sensitive, no wire/store change. Code review PASS with one non-blocking NIT (e2e assertion ordering) (#607, see [codebase notes](codebase/607.md)). Sending now overrides #601's conditional pin unconditionally and leaves the thread pinned for the reply that follows: `useThreadScrollPin` widens its return to `{ scrollPin, followBottom }`, a single `following.current = true` re-arm wired as a required `onMessageSent` prop on `Composer` and invoked inside `handleSubmit`'s existing `if (sent)` branch, so a no-op submit (not connected, whitespace-only, no active conversation) never re-arms the flag and a submit whose bridge call throws still jumps because the optimistic echo still landed; because #601's re-assert has no dependency array, the jump and the pin are the same fact at two times, so an operator who scrolls up again mid-stream still wins. Not security-sensitive, no wire/store/stylesheet change. Code review PASS with one non-blocking NIT (the no-armed-pin criterion has no live test, structurally guaranteed instead) (#602, see [codebase notes](codebase/602.md)). The thinking indicator now holds for the whole running turn instead of vanishing once `phase` leaves `thinking`: `shouldShowThinking` broadens its phase clause to the already-exported `isTurnRunning` (`thinking || responding`) with both supersede clauses (`apiRetry`, `compacting`) textually untouched, and a new `workingIndicatorState` composes on top to pick between two client-owned labels (`THINKING_COPY`/new `WORKING_COPY`); `ThinkingIndicator`'s prop widens from `isThinking: boolean` to `state: WorkingIndicatorState | null`, keeping "no daemon string reaches this view" a type-level guarantee. No CSS/store/wire change, no rename. Not security-sensitive, code review PASS with one non-blocking SHOULD FIX (a stale self-contradicting comment on `isTurnRunning`) (#648, see [codebase notes](codebase/648.md)). The working indicator now names the daemon's currently-open tool (`` `Running <name>… ` ``) instead of the generic `WORKING_COPY`, derived by a new pure `openToolName(items)` — the last `toolCall` item in array order whose `result` is still `null` — over the same `items` slice `Timeline` already reads; reverts the moment that tool's `toolResult` fills the item, no further daemon event needed. Deliberately reverses #648's "no daemon string reaches this view" type-level guarantee, narrowly: `ThinkingIndicator` gains a second, required, separately-typed `toolName: string | null` prop rather than a widened `WorkingIndicatorState`, so the label *choice* stays a closed client-owned union and the name renders only as an auto-escaped text child, exactly like the tool row's own `name` two rows above. A new `.bubble--tool-label` CSS modifier (`.tool-row__summary`'s one-line-ellipsis bound, not `.tool-row__name`'s never-truncates one) keeps a long name from wrapping or moving the composer. Not security-sensitive, no wire/store change. Code review PASS with three non-blocking NITs and one flagged-not-gated risk: a stale open `toolCall` from an interrupted turn could make the next turn's indicator name it, corroborated by pyrycode #1243 (interrupt → `turn_end{cancelled}`, not a `tool_result`) but left unaddressed per the ticket's own deferred-until-observed posture (#649, see [codebase notes](codebase/649.md)). The indicator now opens the moment the composer accepts a submit, closing the blank window in *front* of a turn (#648 closed the one behind it): a new renderer-sourced `TimelineState` scalar, `localSendPending`, is set by the existing `userText` echo dispatch (no new event — that dispatch already *is* the composer's accept signal) and closed by any daemon `turn_state`, a reconnect reconcile, or a timeline reset; `phase` itself stays daemon-only (a wire mirror), so the local open cannot arm `InterruptControl`. The view composes a new `workingIndicatorStateWithLocalSend` *on top of* the frozen `workingIndicatorState` gate — re-calling it with `phase: 'thinking'` substituted rather than widening `ThreadStatus` — so #493's/#496's supersede clauses are inherited for free and all 19 existing gate-test literals stand untouched. Code review round 1 FAILed on e2e blast radius the change's own PR body wrongly claimed didn't exist: two Playwright specs' fakes silently lost their intended mount-transition coverage because the new local-send path pre-opened the indicator ahead of their pushed `turn_state` frames; round 2 fixed both by making the fakes end turns with a real daemon's trailing `turn_state{idle}` and PASSed (#650, see [codebase notes](codebase/650.md)). `TimelineRow`'s `toolCall` arm was extracted into an exported `ToolRow({item, expanded?})` — the `UnrecognizedRow` shape, exported so the branch is reachable from this repo's DOM-less server-render tier — and reverses #230's "resultSummary not surfaced" call: expanded with a result present, a body stacks under the chip (a sibling, not a child, since the chip is a single-line `overflow: hidden` pill) drawing the result text in a bounded, scrolling `<pre>` (`.unrecognized-row__raw`'s treatment at the chip's `--radius-sm`), a client-owned `TOOL_RESULT_EMPTY_COPY` ("No output") for an exact-`''` empty result, and a `tool-row__body--error` modifier (kept structurally separate from the row's own `tool-row--error`) for a failed tool. One derived `body = expanded ? result : null` gates both the wrapper modifier and the body element, so a pending row draws nothing regardless of the flag. `Timeline`/`TimelineRow` signatures are untouched and nothing passes `expanded` yet — the toggle, the container state and the e2e are #697's, and the per-input-field list this same body will hold is #645's. Not security-sensitive by itself but security-reviewed (PASS): the result text's only sink is React text children, explicitly never through `AssistantMarkdown` (imported two arms up in this file — markdown would let a daemon-relayed result emit an `<img src>` beacon from a privileged renderer) and never an attribute/log/lookup-key. Code review PASS with one non-blocking SHOULD FIX (a CSS comment on `.tool-row__body--error .tool-row__result` overstates its own reach — it does not yet style `.tool-row__empty`, left for #645/#697) and two NITs (#696, see [codebase notes](codebase/696.md)). #697 then supplied the missing control: the chip on a resolved row forks into a real `<button aria-expanded>` (pending rows keep the original `<div>` chip byte-for-byte and offer no affordance), backed by one component-local `useState` — the same shape `UnrecognizedRow` already shipped, per ADR 0006's "lowest scope that resets correctly" — so no row can inherit another's expansion and none survive a thread reset without any explicit teardown, riding `Timeline`'s array-index key stability and `fillResult`'s in-place replacement. `expanded` renamed to `defaultExpanded` since it stopped being a controlled value. One additive `.tool-row__chip--toggle` CSS rule, silent on `border` so the pre-existing error-accent selector keeps winning. Code review PASS, 0 MUST FIX, 2 NIT (#697, see [codebase notes](codebase/697.md)). The chip's second run then stopped drawing raw `inputSummary`: `toolHeadline()`, a new pure module beside `shortenPath`, picks a headline from the tool's own input fields as one fallback chain (a `Bash` description-then-command override, the eight-name preferred order, the first line-break-free entry — what makes MCP tools work — then `inputSummary` verbatim), shortened through #644's helper iff the picked key is `file_path`/`path`/`notebook_path`; the swap is one run inside `chipRuns`, reaching both the resolved button and the pending div. Central trap handled: `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` is off in `tsconfig.web.json`, so a `Record<string, string>` index read needs an explicit `string | undefined` annotation to avoid re-opening a blank-headline hole through the type system. `shortenPath.ts`'s doc comment, which misdirected this ticket's own first caller toward a `kind`/`subject` shape that never shipped, was corrected comment-only in the same PR. Code review PASS, 0 MUST FIX, 2 NIT (#705, see [codebase notes](codebase/705.md)). The body then gained the full picture beside that headline: every entry of the `input` map, name above value, in arrival order, as the first children of the same body, above the unchanged result — literal where the headline is selective, no shortening, no salience pick, no re-ordering, no skipping the field the headline already promoted. `Object.entries(item.input ?? NO_INPUT_FIELDS)`, inline and never `for...in`/keyed, where `NO_INPUT_FIELDS` is a named `{}` constant rather than a bare literal because the bare form widens the union `Object.entries` resolves against and silently types every value `any`. No list-wrapper element and no length guard — an empty map renders nothing, so absent-vs-`{}` collapsing into "draw nothing" is structural. `.tool-row__input-value` joins the existing `.tool-row__result` selector rather than copying its declarations, so "the field list takes the result block's visual language" is a fact by construction; the failed-tool accent's descendant selector gains nothing from that join, so the field list stays untinted on a failed tool, deliberately. First ticket to draw the input **key** in the DOM — extended #697's SAFETY block on the basis that a name is exactly as daemon-controlled as its value. Corrected the one stale `#645` CSS comment that falsely claimed the error accent reached `.tool-row__empty` and a future field list. Code review PASS, 0 MUST FIX, 2 NIT (#706, see [codebase notes](codebase/706.md)). [#796](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-desktop/issues/796) built the desktop layout's own fixed-height status row above the composer (Figma `111:3525`): `ComposerStatusArea({ isRunning, children })`, a new in-file function that — unlike every sibling indicator — never returns `null`, so its height is reserved whether or not the working indicator has a label and the composer stops moving under the operator's cursor. `ThinkingIndicator`'s markup moved out of the daemon-bubble surface into this row as its sole child (a single `<span>` inheriting `--color-primary`; `.bubble--thinking`/`.bubble--tool-label` retired), gaining a turning `PyryMark` icon gated on the raw `isTurnRunning(phase)` reading, independent of the label's own api-retry/compaction supersede rules — so a turning icon beside no text is a legal render, AC2's held height is what makes it read as intentional. `ApiRetryIndicator`/`CompactingIndicator`/`StallIndicator` keep their pre-#796 mount site, bubble treatment and precedence rule untouched. The snowflake mark itself moved out of `WelcomeScreen.tsx`'s module-private `PyrycodeMark` into a shared `theme/PyryMark.tsx` (see [Welcome screen](features/welcome-screen.md) below), verified to be the same glyph the welcome hero draws by an exact 6.5× coordinate ratio rather than assumed. The retired `.bubble`-ancestry truncation backstop for a long daemon tool name was re-derived as an explicit three-link `min-width: 0` flex chain, measured against a 3000-char name rather than assumed; `thread-scroll-pin.spec.ts`'s viewport-shrink criterion was repointed from the now non-shrinking working indicator onto the stall indicator; and the repo's first `prefers-reduced-motion` coverage landed as its own Playwright spec, with a `matchMedia` self-verify gate and a control arm proving the spec can fail rather than pass vacuously. Code review PASS, one non-blocking SHOULD FIX (a stale comment clause) and a batch of stale cross-file prose comparisons to the retired `.bubble--thinking`, folded into the docs by this pass rather than gated on. See [Composer status row](features/conversation-shell-composer.md#composer-status-row-796). [#811](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-desktop/issues/811) built the desktop layout's other new row, a fixed-height `.composer__footer` **below** the message box — `Composer`'s second child, after `.composer__row` (`.composer__hint`, formerly the first child, was retired by [#968](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-desktop/issues/968)) — holding a lone "Context: N%" reading (`ContextUsageReading`/`ContextUsageControl`, the `ComposerErrorChip`/`ComposerErrorChipControl` pure-view/store-bound-container shape) while the row's other four desktop-layout slots (#680 Actions/#682 permission mode/#683 model and effort/#685 attach) stay genuinely empty — no placeholder, no disabled control — since each is blocked on daemon work that doesn't exist yet. The percentage math [Run configuration Context window section](features/conversation-shell-workspace-and-run-config.md#run-configuration-context-window-section-192) used to compute inline is now one shared `contextUsagePercent(usedTokens, windowTokens): number | null` (new `contextUsage.ts`), so the sheet's gauge and this reading share one guard rather than two that could drift; the shared guard also gained `Number.isFinite(windowTokens)`, closing a `NaN`/`width: NaN%` reachable from a daemon frame whose token counts overflow to `Infinity`. Not security-sensitive by itself but security-reviewed (architect self-review, PASS after one MUST FIX on the finiteness gap, fixed in the same pass) — no daemon *string* reaches this surface at all, only a clamped integer interpolated into a client-owned literal. See [Composer footer row](features/conversation-shell-composer.md#composer-footer-row-811). **Split 2026-09-01** — the per-surface sections now live in their own documents, listed below; this entry stays the map. **Split 2026-09-01** — the routing, pairing and conversation-exit sections now live in their own documents, listed below; this entry stays the map. **Split 2026-09-01** — the store internals now live in their own document, listed below; this entry stays the map.
    - [Conversation timeline store — internals](features/conversation-timeline-store-internals.md) — The store itself, the translator and React binding that feed it from the daemon event stream, and the flow between them.
    - [Paired shell — conversation exits and stamps](features/paired-shell-conversation-exits.md) — What happens to the open thread when its conversation is deleted or archived, and the last-read and view stamps written as the user moves between conversations.
    - [Paired shell — the pair server route](features/paired-shell-pair-server-route.md) — The pairServer route: how the shell reaches the pairing surface and what it does while it is there.
    - [Paired shell — routing and layout](features/paired-shell-routing.md) — The route model and its transition, the pure view and its container, the two-pane desktop layout, the seams at either end, and the data flow between them.
    - [Conversation shell — chrome and controls](features/conversation-shell-chrome.md) — The screen's structure and the persistent controls around the thread: layout, theme, the back, unpair and re-pair controls, and the connection surfaces in the header.
    - [Conversation shell — composer options panel](features/conversation-shell-composer-options.md) — Map only. **Split 2026-09-02** into the two documents below, once the slash-command type-ahead's mount pushed the shared doc over the size cap.
    - [Conversation shell — composer options panel surface](features/conversation-shell-composer-options-panel.md) — The shared panel surface (#838), its footer placement and right-edge clamp arithmetic (#839, #847, clamp later lifted into a shared hook by #940), and its keyboard contract (#840).
    - [Conversation shell — slash command type-ahead](features/conversation-shell-composer-options-slash-type-ahead.md) — #939's pure opening/filtering/completion decisions over a published command list, plus #940's mount that renders them as a panel anchored to the message box, per #934's product decision (no descriptions, a 10-row cap, a window-relative width bound).
    - [Conversation shell — composer](features/conversation-shell-composer.md) — The composer's own surfaces: status row, error chip, message box (#951), footer row. Options panel has its own doc.
    - [Conversation shell — conversation surfaces and modals](features/conversation-shell-conversation-and-modals.md) — Surfaces that act on the conversation as a whole rather than on one turn. **Split 2026-09-02** into three further documents (below); this entry now holds only the interactive flip + thread cutover, the queued backlog, and screen-snapshot history.
    - [Conversation shell — actions menu and reader cutover](features/conversation-shell-actions-menu-and-reader-cutover.md) — The composer's Actions menu (#680: sends `/clear`/`/compact`/`/knowledge-capture` as ordinary message text via the shared options panel) and the per-conversation timeline reader cutover (#758: every render slice reads the open conversation's own holder slice instead of the flat store).
    - [Composer model menu](features/composer-model-menu.md) — #988: the footer's second live control, assembled from the shared options panel, the daemon-published rows and the exact-equality match rule. Three renderings decided by one pure function (`composerModelMenuModel`); optimistic label move on pick, reverted on rejection; triggered the `.composer__footer-button` shared-treatment extraction and a correction to three sites resting on "exactly one `.composer-options-anchor` exists."
    - [Composer effort menu](features/composer-effort-menu.md) — #989: the footer's third live control and its last before the context reading, `ComposerModelMenu`'s assembly reused with the label being the session's effort value verbatim rather than a looked-up name. Same three-reading shape gated on `effort_levels` collapsing absent/`null`/`[]` into one inert arm, no fallback to a full five-level list, a repeated published level carried rather than deduped; declined the third-glyph CSS lift, which #682 declined again rather than carrying as this ticket had assigned it.
    - [Composer permission-mode menu](features/composer-permission-mode-menu.md) — #682: the footer's second live control, between Actions and the model menu, assembled from #1020's inbound mode, #1021's outbound field and the shared options panel. The wire is asymmetric — six modes reported, five settable (`bypassPermissions` refused on write, so nothing here can escalate a session into it) — so the menu renders six labels but offers five entries. Structurally unlike its two neighbours: its entries are a client-owned constant rather than a daemon-published list, so it has no inert arm, takes no props, and is operable the instant a mode is known — moving the footer's anchor/`aria-haspopup` counts across ten shipped assertions. Its label is looked up (camelCase machine value → display name) through an own-property-guarded read, closing a `__proto__`/`constructor` crash a bare index would have let through.
    - [Conversation shell — modals](features/conversation-shell-modals.md) — Map only. **Split 2026-09-02** into the two documents below.
    - [Conversation shell — permission modal](features/conversation-shell-permission-modal.md) — The permission/trust modal (#224, answerable since #237, second-confirm since #226, rejection surface since #249, re-keyed marker since #511).
    - [Conversation shell — question panel](features/conversation-shell-question-panel.md) — The batched-question overlay in the composer's slot (#906: `useQuestionBridge` mounted at App level, `ComposerSlot` drawing `QuestionPanelView`; #907: radio/checkbox option rows keyed by array index plus the in-list Other field, drawing only; #912: those rows and the Other field made live against the #911 picks store, surviving a chat switch; #915: the title row draws one tab per question, keyed by array position, and a click jumps the box to it, clamped against a same-nonce re-delivery shortening the list; #916: the same jump added to the Actions row as a Previous button plus a trailing button that reads Next until the last question). **Split 2026-09-02**: its Cancel-button section moved to the document below to stay under the size cap.
    - [Question panel — Cancel refuses the batch](features/question-panel-cancel-refusal.md) — #921 gives the Actions row's Cancel button its first sending handler: `questionResolution.ts`'s `refuseQuestionBatch` sends `refuseQuestionsCommand({question_batch_id})` (#920's command, the `answer_token` minted main-side and never seen by the renderer) guarded in a `try`, then dispatches `dismissed` to `questionPicksStore` and `questionBatchStore` **outside** the try — picks-first, matching `subscribeQuestionBatches`' own fan-out order — so a swallowed send still clears the panel (AC3) and the composer returns. The two client-owned constants carried on the batch store's `dismissed` arm, `outcome: 'refused'`/`source: 'client'`, deliberately avoid `modalResolution.cancelPrompt`'s `'local'`: that value is a member of `WireModalSource`'s closed set and reads there as an *answered* outcome, which would let a later reader merging the two families mistake a refusal for the operator's own choice. Still no `resolved` id-memory on the batch store — the daemon re-asserts a batch only at connect time and both stores already reset on `reconnected` first, so a batch reappearing after a swallowed refusal is the honest outcome. The catch logs a static, content-free string with the error object dropped, since an `Error` from a failing bridge send can stringify the nonce it choked on. `e2e/question-cancel-refuses.spec.ts` is the first spec in this repo to assert on an outbound frame, narrowing its capture of `question_refused` to `{type, question_batch_id}` at capture time so the token can never appear in a diff. Security review PASS — see the document for the full review. Its deps interface, `QuestionRefuseDeps`, is renamed `QuestionResolveDeps` by the document below to serve a second exit.
    - [Question panel — Continue answers the batch](features/question-panel-continue-answer.md) — #922 gives the Actions row's trailing button its Continue-role handler, the panel's last inert control. `resolveQuestionAnswers(questions, selections)` is one pure function that is both the availability gate and the payload builder — a question holds a value with at least one ticked option or a ticked, non-blank Other row, and any gap returns `null` for the whole batch rather than a partial result, so the button's `disabled` state and the frame's contents can never disagree. Ticked labels resolve `option.label` in ascending display order (the one place claude-authored text leaves `questionResolution.ts`, into the entry's `values` array and nowhere else — the stale module docblock claiming otherwise is corrected in the same commit), with the trimmed Other text appended last. A stale option position left by a same-nonce re-delivery — the accepted gap #911's security review handed forward — is skipped rather than thrown out of the render, resolving it rather than merely bounding it. `answerQuestionBatch` reuses `refuseQuestionBatch`'s guarded-send-then-picks-first-clear shape verbatim over the renamed `QuestionResolveDeps`, refusing outright on a `null` payload with no send, no clear, and no log. The gate on the trailing button is a conjunction — unavailable only in the Continue role *and* only while incomplete — because gating on incompleteness alone would disable the same element's Next role and strand the operator unable to reach the question that would complete the batch. `selectBatchSelections`, a new picks-store read shape returning the whole batch's picks by reference (a hoisted shared empty `Map` when untouched, the `EMPTY_QUESTION_SELECTION` mechanism one level up), feeds the container's single call to `resolveQuestionAnswers`. CSS mutes both the content colour *and* the fill on `:disabled` — a deliberate deviation from `.composer__send:disabled`'s content-only convention, because this button's enabled fill is `--color-primary` (`#9dcbfc`, bright in the dark-only theme) rather than the neutral fills the precedent buttons use, so muting content alone would read as legible-but-still-most-prominent. `e2e/question-answer-continue.spec.ts` drives a three-question batch (single-select, multi-select, Other) with nothing answered first, to make the deadlock failure mode visible if it existed, before answering and asserting the sent frame narrows to `{type, question_batch_id, answers}` at capture time. Security review PASS, with one named-not-guarded concurrency residual (a same-nonce re-delivery landing between the render that computed `answers` and the click) ruled not exploitable since only the daemon, which already controls both the re-delivery and the resolution, could mount it.
    - [Conversation shell — session boundaries and channel info](features/conversation-shell-session-and-channel-info.md) — The session-boundary delimiter row (#286, redrawn #690) and the Channel Info sheet (#365) with its Rename/Archive/Delete actions (#368/#366/#377).
    - [Conversation shell — seams](features/conversation-shell-seams.md) — The seams this screen exposes, both the ones a later ticket bound and the ones still open.
    - [Conversation shell — tool row layout](features/conversation-shell-tool-row-layout.md) — The later redraw of the tool row: the shell command code block, the full-width bordered row, and the header's groups and run routing.
    - [Conversation shell — message bubble](features/conversation-shell-message-bubble.md) — The later redraw of the bubble itself (#969): the desktop `Message` shape, a new `title-small` token family, the meta row (`DD.MM.YYYY - HH:MM` timestamp, local getters, plus the copy control), and the clipboard-permission allowlist it needed in `src/main/index.ts`. The queued row and the unmounted `MessageBubble` residue take the CSS restyle only, no meta row.
    - [Conversation shell — tool rows](features/conversation-shell-tool-rows.md) — How a tool call is painted from the moment it appears to the moment its result can be read: the pending and resolved rows, the expandable result, the collapsed headline and the input field list.
    - [Conversation shell — turn status surfaces](features/conversation-shell-turn-status.md) — What the screen shows while a turn is running: the timeline render, the composer status row's single label (thinking/working, retry, compacting, or stalled — folded into one view by #967), and the background-task panel.
    - [Conversation shell — workspace and run configuration](features/conversation-shell-workspace-and-run-config.md) — Choosing where a session runs and how it is configured: the workspace chip and picker, the run configuration sheet and its sections, and the log data section.
- [Paired shell](features/paired-shell.md) — the second-level `list ⇄ thread ⇄ settings ⇄ pairServer ⇄ archive` router under the app shell's `conversation` route: a pure `nextPairedRoute` reducer + `PairedShellView` route→view switch, plus a `PairedShell` container owning the `useReducer` nav state (#140). `open` has three independent triggers — a Channel List row click (#448 records the clicked conversation first), a daemon-confirmed `conversationCreated` event (#242), and a clicked push notification (#393, carries no conversation payload). [#530](codebase/530.md) (navigation, security-sensitive, shipped) replaced both carrying triggers' direct `setActiveConversation` with a new `activateConversation` helper (`src/renderer/src/activateConversation.ts`) that clears [`timelineStore`](features/thread-timeline.md) ([#528](codebase/528.md)'s `reset` arm) and [`sessionIdStore`](features/session-id-store.md) ([#529](codebase/529.md)'s `clearSessionId`) before recording the new conversation, gated on the active conversation's id actually changing — fixing a bug where opening a different conversation rendered the previous one's rows and could address a Run-configuration write (including YOLO) to the previous conversation's still-running daemon session (PASS, 0 blocking + 2 NITs, see [codebase notes](codebase/530.md)). [#531](codebase/531.md) (unpair / pair-another-server, security-sensitive, shipped) added a sibling helper, `clearPairingScopedState`, wired unconditionally into `onUnpaired` and `onPairServerPaired` — the two paths that end a pairing rather than switch conversations — clearing the same timeline and session-id stores plus [`activeConversationStore`](features/conversation-shell.md) ([#529](codebase/529.md)'s `clearActiveConversation`) and [`sessionStore`](features/session-store.md) (`reset`, #166, moved out of `runUnpair`'s ok branch into this shared seam) in one step, closing the gap where the first thread opened on a freshly paired server still showed the previous daemon's rows and could address wire actions (including a Run-configuration YOLO write) to it (PASS, 0 blocking + 3 NITs, see [codebase notes](codebase/531.md)). [#593](codebase/593.md) (security-sensitive, shipped) widened `clearPairingScopedState` to a fifth store, [`announcedModelStore`](features/announced-model-store.md)'s `clearAnnouncedModel` — claude's announced running model is pairing-scoped the same way (nothing on a fresh pairing re-asserts it) and, once #560 rendered it, a stale value from the previous pairing was observable in the run-configuration sheet under no provenance marker; deliberately kept off the transport's `connected` edge, since a reconnect to the *same* daemon leaves the held announcement accurate (PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/593.md)). [#652](codebase/652.md) (shipped) added a **fourth** trigger, this time for `back` rather than `open`: deleting the discussion currently open in the thread now returns to `list` on the daemon's own `conversationDeleted` confirmation, via a new sibling helper `exitActiveConversation` (id-gated like #530's, clearing `timelineStore`/`activeConversationStore`/`sessionIdStore` — three of #531's five, deliberately excluding `sessionStore` and `announcedModelStore` since the pairing itself hasn't ended) and a new `conversationDeletedBridge` (the `conversationCreatedBridge` twin, a second independent subscriber to `conversationDeleted` beside the list-reflect's). Reuses the existing absolute `back` transition — no new nav arm, no reducer edit. Fixes the second-order bug where the deleted-but-still-active conversation kept receiving the composer send and the queued-message drop after its row had already left the Channel List. PASS, 1 SHOULD FIX (a missing regression pin on the bridge's `!== null` vs. truthiness guard) + 1 NIT (`back`'s absoluteness can navigate away from a non-thread screen on a delayed confirmation — flagged for #653, not fixed here), see [codebase notes](codebase/652.md). [#653](codebase/653.md) (shipped) added a **fifth** trigger, the archive sibling of #652: the discussion currently open in the thread returns to `list` once the daemon's authoritative conversation list shows it archived, reusing `exitActiveConversation` **unmodified**. The signal can't be a dedicated wire event the way #652's `conversationDeleted` is — decoded `conversation_updated` carries no archive flag and fires identically on rename and change-workspace, both reachable on the open discussion from inside its own thread — so a new sibling bridge, `conversationArchivedBridge` (the first of the family to own no wire arm), derives a level predicate over `conversationsReceived` instead: does the active conversation's row in the daemon's list say `is_archived`? That shape makes the AC trigger-agnostic for free, including picking up a second client's archive (eventually, via this client's own next re-list, since pyrycode#881 correlates `conversation_updated` to the requester rather than broadcasting it) and leaves no stale-row window at all, since the same list arrival that triggers the exit has already filtered the row out of `partitionActive`. PASS, 3 NITs (all doc-precision, none blocking) + the #652 absolute-`back` NIT carried forward unfixed (the window is wider here — two round trips instead of one), see [codebase notes](codebase/653.md). [#670](codebase/670.md) (shipped) turned `list` and `thread` from mutually-exclusive alternative screens into the two arms of one **two-pane desktop shell** — a fixed 400px sidebar (the Channel List, now always mounted) beside a chat pane holding `ConversationScreen` on `thread` and `null` on `list`, plus `minWidth: 800` on the `BrowserWindow`; `settings`/`archive`/`pairServer` already returned a full-screen `<section>` replacing the whole shell, so they satisfy "still open over both panes" for free. Round-1 code review **FAILED**: mounting the sidebar beside the thread made a same-route conversation switch (a different sidebar row clicked while a thread is open) reachable for the first time, and every one of `ConversationScreen`'s screen-local state owners that assumed "a remount always separates two conversations" — the composer draft sharpest among them, which would follow the operator into the switched-to conversation and be sent there — leaked across the switch, since `activateConversation`'s store-side clear covers the timeline and session id but not component state. Rework added a required `paneKey: string | null` prop applied as `ConversationScreen`'s `key`, recorded by the container at the two conversation-activating call sites (a FAB create confirmation, a sidebar row click) and left unset by the notification-triggered nullary `open`; **PASS** on round 2 after an independent re-derivation of the activation-site set and a fresh accessible-name/vacuity sweep, see [codebase notes](codebase/670.md). [#757](codebase/757.md) (shipped) widened both id-gated helpers again, this time to clear the [conversation timeline holder](features/conversation-timeline-holder.md) (#755/#756's keyed store, which had no clear path at all): `clearPairingScopedState` gained a sixth store, nullary `clearAllTimelines()`, and `exitActiveConversation` gained a fourth clear, `clearTimelineFor(conversationId)` — the pairing-boundary clear drops every retained slice because conversation ids are scoped to the issuing server, while the deletion clear drops exactly the one conversation and leaves every other slice the same held object. Both sit immediately after each helper's pre-existing flat-store `reset`, the dual-write position #756 established. PASS, 0 MUST / 2 SHOULD (both self-authored comment-cite drift, folded into the citation sweep) / 1 NIT, see [codebase notes](codebase/757.md).
- [Live window](features/live-window.md) — `createLiveWindow()` (`src/main/liveWindow.ts`), the composition root's Electron-free indirection for "which `BrowserWindow` is current." Holds two faces — a `sink: DaemonEventSink` that is *never* destroyed (recording the last connection-status event, then forwarding one hop down through [#518](codebase/518.md)'s `emitDaemonEvent` guard, so a status change during a closed-window gap is not lost) and a `window: FocusableWindow & ActivatableWindow` that answers `isDestroyed()` honestly (the opposite requirement, since its callees decide whether to act by asking first). `WindowTarget = DaemonEventSink & FocusableWindow & ActivatableWindow` answers #518's open question 3 — the three interfaces intersect here rather than collapsing into one, so zero fixture cascades. The root's `mainWindow` binding is deleted entirely; a new `openWindow()` attaches each window (first, or dock-reopened) at creation and, on `did-finish-load`, replays the recorded status then calls the connection's idempotent `start()` — never `reconnect()`, which would kill a streaming turn (AC4). Closes [#518](codebase/518.md)'s carried-forward gap: routing alone was safe but not working, since every captured-window consumer still pointed at a destroyed original after a dock reopen (#519, security-sensitive, PASS after 1 MUST FIX + rework, see [codebase notes](codebase/519.md)).
- [Session store](features/session-store.md) — the renderer's single source of truth for the active session's connection status + conversation messages, mutated through a sealed action union; the state seam #3/#19 dispatches into and the conversation thread reads (#2, read side bound in #69). `createSessionStore` now takes an optional `observe` seam invoked synchronously after every `dispatch`; the app singleton wires it to a content-free diagnostics logger so each state transition — including a same-state dedup — leaves a footprint in the debug bundle (#134, see below). Gained a `reset` action (`return initialSessionState`) — unlike `disconnected`, which deliberately preserves `messages`, `reset` clears both facets in one step; originally dispatched only from the conversation screen's unpair control (#166), moved into the shared `clearPairingScopedState` helper by [#531](codebase/531.md) so both paths that end a pairing (unpair, pair-another-server) dispatch it, and the reset's stale doc comment (claiming alone it hides a previous pairing's conversation, no longer true since #179 moved the thread onto `timelineStore`) was corrected in the same ticket.
- [Daemon-event channel](features/daemon-event-channel.md) — the typed background→window event pipe: a sealed `DaemonEvent` union in `src/shared/ipc/`, the single `emitDaemonEvent` send path, and a receive-only `onDaemonEvent` subscription on `window.pyry` (#18). Gained three no-store-action members — `debugBundleProgress{chunksReceived}` / `debugBundleSaved{path}` / `debugBundleFailed{reason}` + the coarse closed `DebugBundleFailure` enum — reporting the debug-bundle download's progress/result to the #72 download UI, not the session store (#168); now has a real emitter, the [debug-bundle orchestrator](features/debug-bundle-orchestrator.md) (#169, see below). Gained a fourth no-store-action member, `snapshotReceived{model,effort,yolo}` — a **dedicated minimal shape**, deliberately not a reuse of the wire `ScreenSnapshotPayload` it derives from, so the reply's sensitive `text` field structurally cannot reach the renderer (#180, see below). Extended with two more always-present fields, `used_tokens`/`window_tokens` (context-window usage, pyrycode/pyrycode#857) — carried faithfully as values, not interpreted (#191); the [run-config store](features/run-config-store.md) copies both out and the sheet renders them as the Context window gauge (#192). Gained a fifth and sixth no-store-action member, `assistantDelta{turnId,seq,text}` / `turnEnd{turnId,stopReason}` — the v2 interactive-stream transport slice, **deliberately NOT content-minimised** (unlike `snapshotReceived`, `text` is the render payload and crosses on purpose); consumed by the [conversation timeline store](features/conversation-timeline-store.md)'s bridge, not the session store; real emitter from the start but zero live traffic until #179 flips `interactive` (#199, store+bridge consumer landed #202). [#751](codebase/751.md) later widened `assistantDelta` to carry `conversationId` by name (security-sensitive, PASS, 2 NIT) — the same routing-key widening `turnState`/`stallDetected`/`apiRetry`/`compacting` already had, and the first on a turn-stream item rather than daemon state; the id still stops at that bridge, so `ThreadEvent.assistantDelta` and the render slice are unaffected. [#752](codebase/752.md) widened `turnEnd` next, on the same terms (security-sensitive, PASS, 1 SHOULD FIX + 2 NIT) — no decode change, since `parseTurnEndPayload` already required the field; the id stops at the same bridge, so `ThreadEvent.turnEnd` is likewise unaffected. [#763](codebase/763.md), the code child of the #753 split, widened `toolUse` next, on the same terms (security-sensitive, PASS, 2 NIT) — no decode change, since `parseToolUsePayload` already required the field; the id stops at the same bridge, so `ThreadEvent.toolUse` is likewise unaffected; borrowed citations of the old identity elsewhere retired by [#764](codebase/764.md). [#766](codebase/766.md), the code child of the #754 split, widened `toolResult` last, on the same terms (security-sensitive, PASS after one rework round, 1 NIT) — no decode change, since `parseToolResultPayload` already required the field; the id stops at the same bridge, so `ThreadEvent.toolResult` is likewise unaffected. [#784](codebase/784.md) widened `unrecognizedMessage` last, on the same terms (security-sensitive, PASS, 2 NIT) — no decode change, since `parseUnrecognizedMessagePayload` already required the field; the id stops at the same bridge, so `ThreadEvent.unrecognizedMessage` is likewise unaffected. This completes the #675 family: all nine `DaemonEvent` arms with a wire `conversation_id` now carry `conversationId`. Gained an eighth no-store-action member, `turnState{state}` — the coarse turn-lifecycle scalar of the same v2 stream, decoded via the `role`-style closed-enum idiom; consumed as the timeline bridge's third owned arm, giving `selectPhase` its first real source (#214, see below). Gained a ninth no-store-action member, `toolUse{turnId,toolUseId,name,inputSummary}` — the tool-call enrichment of the same v2 stream, decoded via five `requireString`s (no enum); consumed as the timeline bridge's fourth owned arm, the first to drive a durable `toolCall` item rather than text or a scalar (#217, see below). Widened with a sixth, optional field, `input?: Readonly<Record<string, string>>` (pyrycode#1678) — the tool's own input fields, decoded via a new `optionalStringMap` narrower that rotates `requireStringArrayOrNull`'s posture from a list to a map and drops the three prototype-reserved keys rather than throwing on them (so a model can't suppress its own tool row by naming a parameter `__proto__`); ships dormant, absent means a pre-#1678 daemon (#642, security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/642.md)).
