Gained a tenth and eleventh no-store-action member, `modalShown{modalId,class,title,prompt,options,defaultOptionId}` / `modalDismissed{modalId,outcome,source}` — the transport slice of the modal vertical (ADR 0009), decoded via two closed-enum checks (`class`/`source`) plus a per-option narrower over the ordered `options` array; unlike every prior member, consumed by **neither** existing bridge (both discard as `null`) — the real consumer is the third, independent [modal store + bridge](features/modal-store-bridge.md), shipped in #223. Neither arm drops a `conversation_id` — the wire carries none on a modal (#201, see below). Gained a twelfth no-store-action member, `toolResult{turnId,toolUseId,isError,resultSummary}` — the outcome half of `toolUse` and the vertical's last transport slice, decoded via four `requireString`s plus one `requireBoolean` (`isError`, `false` is a value); consumed as the timeline bridge's fifth owned arm, the first to **resolve** an existing `toolCall`'s `result` in place (via `fillResult`, #121) rather than append a new item or set a scalar; also the first arm to force a case in three exhaustive `DaemonEvent` switches at once, since `modalBridge.ts` (#223) now also nulls it (#229, see below). Gained a thirteenth no-store-action member, `conversationCreated{conversation}` — the write-side twin of `conversationsReceived`, reusing the wire `ConversationCreatedPayload` verbatim (its own 5-field shape, not `ConversationSummary`); consumed by neither existing bridge, real consumer is the render sibling #242 (#241, see [conversation create](features/conversation-create.md), below). Gained a fourteenth no-store-action member, `sessionTransition{newSessionId}` — the interactive session-boundary marker's addressing id, content-minimised at the emit like `snapshotReceived` (`previous_session_id`/`reason`/`occurred_at`/`workspace_cwd` decoded then dropped); consumed by none of the three existing bridges (#254, see below); its real consumer is the [session-id store](features/session-id-store.md)'s App-level headless subscriber, a fifth independent `onDaemonEvent` listener, not a change to any of the three ([#259](codebase/259.md), see below). Widened with `reason`/`occurredAt`/`workspaceCwd` — only `previous_session_id` stays dropped now; `reason` carried as the closed `WireSessionTransitionReason`, `workspaceCwd` as `string | null` (untrusted daemon filesystem path, plain-text-only warning carried forward from `conversationCreated`/`conversationUpdated`); no bridge gained a case, the existing #259 consumer reads only `newSessionId` and is unaffected (#285, see [codebase notes](codebase/285.md)). Gained a fifteenth no-store-action member, `modalAnswerRejected{modalId}` — the first built entirely from **main-side correlation memory**, not a decoded wire field: the daemon `error` it reports on (#116) carries no `modal_id` (ADR 0009), so [daemon connection](features/daemon-connection.md) attributes it via a FIFO of its own outstanding answered ids; consumed by neither existing bridge, real (still-dormant) owner is the [modal store + bridge](features/modal-store-bridge.md), render lands in #249 (#248, security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/248.md)). Gained a sixteenth no-store-action member, `sessionSettingsUpdated{sessionId}` — the `set_session_settings` (#263) confirmation reply's addressing id, minimal by construction (the reply's one field, nothing to drop, unlike `sessionTransition`'s four-field trim); consumed by none of the three existing bridges, real consumer is #261 / #256 (correlation / store, not yet built) (#264, see [session settings send](features/session-settings-send.md), [codebase notes](codebase/264.md)). That sixteenth member widened with a `changeId: string` field — the renderer-minted correlation key [daemon connection](features/daemon-connection.md)'s new `pendingSettings` map echoes back after matching the reply's `Envelope.in_reply_to`; the widen is transparent to all three exhaustive bridges (field-only change, no new arm), still consumed by none of them, real consumer is #256 (#261, security-sensitive, PASS, see [session settings send](features/session-settings-send.md), [codebase notes](codebase/261.md)). Gained a seventeenth no-store-action member, `sessionSettingsRejected{changeId}` — the rejected twin of `sessionSettingsUpdated`, a genuine new arm (forces the three exhaustive bridges' compiler-checked case, unlike the field-widen above); emitted by [daemon connection](features/daemon-connection.md)'s `pendingSettings`-lookup precedence gate on a correlated `daemon-error`, which on a match also suppresses that same case's `modalAnswerRejected` FIFO and the #116 bundle reassembler; consumed by none of the three existing bridges, real consumer is #256 (#269, security-sensitive, PASS, see [session settings send](features/session-settings-send.md), [codebase notes](codebase/269.md)). [#179](codebase/179.md) flipped `interactive` on: `assistantDelta`/`turnEnd`/`turnState`/`toolUse`/`toolResult`/`modalShown`/`modalDismissed` now carry live daemon traffic in production, not just unit-test-driven decode. Gained a further no-store-action member, `conversationUpdated{conversation}` — the write-side twin of `conversationCreated`, reusing the wire `ConversationUpdatedPayload` verbatim; unlike `conversationCreated`, its producer is an **unsolicited daemon broadcast** (no `in_reply_to` correlation, the `assistant_delta` pattern), emitted unconditionally on decode; consumed by neither existing bridge, real consumer is the list-reflect slice #275 (#273, see [conversation promote](features/conversation-promote.md), below). Gained an eighteenth no-store-action member, `queueState{conversationId,queued}` — the daemon's unsolicited queued-backlog snapshot (SSOT pyrycode #720), reusing the wire `QueuedItem` row type verbatim; the first member to **keep** `conversation_id` (as `conversationId`) where every turn-stream sibling drops it, since the snapshot is replacement-truth and the still-unbuilt #293 store keys its held backlog by it. `timelineBridge` deliberately nulls it rather than owning it as a sixth arm — `queue_state` is daemon state, not a turn-stream item; consumed by none of the three existing bridges, real consumer is #293 (#292, security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/292.md)). Gained a further no-store-action member, `stallDetected` — the daemon's onset-only `stall` liveness signal on the same v2 stream as `turnState`/`toolUse`; at ship time **nullary**, the only member whose one wire field (`conversation_id`) was dropped at the emit so nothing crossed IPC at all, the strongest content-minimisation posture in the union at the time; consumed as a no-op by all three exhaustive bridges at ship time (the fourth ticket, after #241/#229/#254, to force a case in all three at once) (#315, security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/315.md)); the [conversation timeline store](features/conversation-timeline-store.md)'s bridge has since moved it from that no-op group to a sixth owned arm, feeding a new `stalled` scalar the render slice reads (#317, see below). [#732](codebase/732.md) later widened the emit to carry `conversationId` by name (security-sensitive, PASS) — the id still stops at that bridge, so `ThreadEvent.stallDetected` and the render slice are unaffected. Gained a nineteenth no-store-action member, `screenSnapshotReceived{text,ts}` — the deliberate, security-reviewed **reversal** of `snapshotReceived`'s content-minimisation: emitted from that same `case 'snapshot'` seam (one frame, two independent emits), a fresh named-field literal bounding the widening to exactly `text`/`ts` since a real consumer, the display slice #318, now exists; `snapshotReceived` itself stays byte-for-byte unchanged; consumed as a no-op by all three exhaustive bridges (the fifth ticket, after #241/#229/#254/#315, to force a case in all three at once), real consumer is #318 (#316, security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/316.md)). Gained a twentieth no-store-action member, `relayLinkChanged{status}` — the relay-**socket** leg as its own signal, distinct from this same union's combined session `connecting`/`connected`/`failed`: a closed `RelayLinkStatus` enum (`connected`/`offline`/`daemon-absent`), fed by a new [relay supervisor](features/relay-supervisor.md) `relay-closed{code}` (a retryable close, now surfaced without changing its absorption) forwarded raw through the [noise relay driver](features/noise-relay-driver.md) and classified at [daemon connection](features/daemon-connection.md)'s single choke point — the raw close code never crosses IPC; consumed as a no-op by all three exhaustive bridges (the sixth ticket to force a case in all three at once), real consumer is the not-yet-built relay-link store #329, toward the #330 two-dot indicator (#328, security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/328.md)). Gained a twenty-first no-store-action member, `conversationDeleted{id}` — the write-side counterpart of `conversationUpdated`, but a distinct, **correlated** reply (matched by `in_reply_to`, not a broadcast) rather than an unsolicited fan-out; unlike `conversationUpdated`'s five-field-by-reference reuse, this flattens to a fresh single-field literal naming the bare routing `id` (the `turnState`/`sessionSettingsUpdated` single-field emit idiom), so `events.ts` stays free of a `ConversationDeletedPayload` import; emitted unconditionally on decode from the same choke point, no correlation state threaded (the `id` is self-sufficient); consumed as a no-op by all three exhaustive bridges at ship time, real consumer is the list-reflect slice #376 — shipped (#375, security-sensitive, PASS, see [conversation delete](features/conversation-delete.md), [codebase notes](codebase/375.md)). Gained a twenty-second no-store-action member, `recentWorkspacesReceived{recentWorkspaces}` — the recent-workspaces read reply, reusing a new wire `RecentWorkspace{path,last_used_at}` row type verbatim (the `conversationsReceived` precedent), a near-exact transpose of #139; `path` is untrusted daemon-supplied display text never resolved to a filesystem path, `last_used_at` an opaque unparsed RFC3339 string; consumed as a no-op by all three exhaustive bridges at ship time; real consumer is the [recent-workspaces store](features/recent-workspaces-store.md)'s bridge, shipped dormant in #382 (#380, security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/380.md)). Gained a twenty-third no-store-action member, `workspaceFolderCreated{path}` — the write-side counterpart of `recentWorkspacesReceived`, a **correlated direct reply** (matched by `in_reply_to`, no broadcast) to the new `createWorkspaceFolder` command (see [Command channel](features/command-channel.md), below); its one-field `WorkspaceFolderCreatedPayload` flattens to a bare `path` at the emit rather than carrying the wire type by reference — the `conversationDeleted` single-field idiom, not `conversationCreated`'s — so `events.ts` stays free of the payload-type import; consumed as a no-op by all three exhaustive bridges at ship time, real consumer is the not-yet-built Create-folder dialog #157 (#381, security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/381.md)). Gained a twenty-fourth no-store-action member, `workspaceFolderRejected` — the rejected twin of `workspaceFolderCreated`, BARE (no field at all — stronger than `sessionSettingsRejected`'s `changeId` or `modalAnswerRejected`'s `modalId`, since only one create-folder dialog is ever open) and correlated by a new main-side `pendingCreateFolders: Set<number>` (the `pendingSettings` pattern, but a `Set` since there is no value to carry) against a content-free `daemon-error`'s `Envelope.in_reply_to`; on a match this precedence gate returns before the bundle-fail / modal-FIFO tier, the same ordering #269 established; consumed as a no-op by all three exhaustive bridges at ship time, real consumer is the not-yet-built #397 round-trip store (#396, security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/396.md)). Gained a further no-store-action member, `apiRetry{active,current,total}` — `stallDetected`'s peer status signal on the same v2 stream, but the first since `assistantDelta`/`turnEnd` to carry real content rather than ship nullary or content-minimised: unlike `stall`, the daemon's `api_retry` frame is **not** onset-only (an explicit `active: false` falling edge) and **not** deduped (the rising edge re-fires as the count climbs), so the transport holds no state and N daemon frames produce N events; `conversation_id` is still dropped at the emit. Consumed as a no-op by all three exhaustive bridges at ship time (#492, security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/492.md)); the [conversation timeline store](features/conversation-timeline-store.md)'s bridge has since moved it from that no-op group to a seventh owned arm, feeding a new `apiRetry: ApiRetryStatus | null` scalar `ApiRetryIndicator` renders and the `shouldShowThinking` supersede predicate reads (#493, see [codebase notes](codebase/493.md)). [#737](codebase/737.md) later widened the emit to carry `conversationId` by name (security-sensitive, PASS) — the id still stops at that bridge, so `ThreadEvent.apiRetry` and the render slice are unaffected. Gained a further no-store-action member, `compacting{active}` — `apiRetry`'s peer status signal on the same v2 stream, but **banner-only**: the wire streams no compaction progress at all, so the arm carries exactly one boolean (the smallest non-nullary shape in the union) and `conversation_id` is dropped at the emit, the same not-onset-only/not-deduped posture as `apiRetry`. Consumed as a no-op by all three exhaustive bridges at ship time (#495, security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/495.md)); the render slice #496 is the first consumer. `DaemonEventSink` gained a required `isDestroyed(): boolean`, closing the destroyed-window guard #18's own spec deferred: on macOS, closing the window destroys the `BrowserWindow` without quitting the app, and the connection kept emitting into it, throwing from the `webContents` accessor itself with nothing catching it; `emitDaemonEvent` now drops the event and returns instead, one guard covering all 32 `daemonConnection.ts` call sites plus the debug-bundle orchestrator's injected `emit` (security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/518.md)). The emitter's `sink` is now `live.sink`, the [live-window](features/live-window.md) holder that replaced a captured `mainWindow`: it forwards through this same guard but never reports itself destroyed, so a status event emitted during a closed-window gap is recorded and replayed into whichever window is current once its `did-finish-load` fires (#519, security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/519.md)). Gained a further no-store-action member, `backgroundTaskStarted{conversationId,taskId,toolCallId,description,taskType,truncatedFields}` — the first of three sibling frames (#565/#566 follow) reporting claude work that **outlives the turn that spawned it** (pyrycode#1240; lane confirmed as the v2 interactive stream, pyrycode#1394); decoded via a new `requireStringArrayOrNull` field narrower (required-present, nullable array — `truncated_fields: null` means nothing was cut, an omitted key fails closed). Unlike `compacting`, this member **keeps** `conversationId` — the frame carries no `turn_id` and opens/closes no turn, so it follows `queueState`'s (#292/#720) daemon-state rule rather than the turn-stream drop convention. (`apiRetry` also kept it, from #737 onward — see above.) `description`/`taskType` are untrusted, model-influenced daemon-relayed text (for `taskType: 'local_bash'`, `description` is the literal command line claude ran) — inert-text-only, never HTML, carried as a doc-comment constraint since this slice has no DOM sink. Consumed as a no-op by all three exhaustive bridges at ship time (#564, security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/564.md)); the background-task-roster store (#573, shipped) consumes only the sibling `backgroundTaskRoster` member below, so this arm stays dormant, awaiting #574. Gained a further no-store-action member, `backgroundTaskUpdated{conversationId,taskId,patch,truncatedFields}` — the second of three sibling frames (#566 follows), the peer of `backgroundTaskStarted` joined on `taskId`: that frame opens a task, this one reports what **changed** afterwards. **Four fields, not six** — no `toolCallId`/`description`/`taskType`, gains `patch`, claude's patch object carried whole and unparsed as an opaque string; reuses `requireStringArrayOrNull` unchanged (no new field narrower). `patch` is never fed to `JSON.parse` — the daemon truncates it at construction, so its own golden fixture is cut mid-token — and an empty `patch` (`''`, "claude sent no change") is a value, not an absence, while an omitted `patch` key still fails closed. Like its sibling, **keeps** `conversationId` (the `queueState` daemon-state rule) and performs **no join** against `backgroundTaskStarted`: ordering is claude's, not the daemon's, so an update for a task never seen opened still emits. `patch`'s keys may carry command text — render as plain text only, never HTML/attribute/URL. Consumed as a no-op by all three exhaustive bridges at ship time (#565, security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/565.md)); the background-task-roster store (#573, shipped) consumes only the sibling `backgroundTaskRoster` member below, so this arm stays dormant, awaiting #574. Gained the third and last sibling frame member, backgroundTaskRoster{conversationId,tasks,droppedTasks} — the AGGREGATE peer of the two above: they report what happened to one task, this reports the whole live set, as a snapshot rather than a delta. Reuses the wire BackgroundTask row type verbatim (the queueState/conversationsReceived nested-array precedent — snake_case, no remap); tasks:[] is emitted as the positive "nothing is alive" signal pyrycode#1240 needs, never filtered; droppedTasks is the frame's only truncation report. Consumed as a no-op by all three exhaustive bridges at ship time (#566, security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/566.md)); ships dormant no longer — the background-task-roster store (#573, shipped) is the first consumer. Gained a twentieth no-store-action member, `modelAnnounced{model,truncated}` — claude's own identity report of the model it resolved for the turn, off its `system`/`init` line (pyrycode#1616/#1638); `model` collides by name with `snapshotReceived`/`runConfigReceived`'s per-session-override `model`, disambiguated in the arm's own comment; `truncated` is load-bearing (a cut identifier always misses the display-name lookup); `conversation_id` dropped at ship time, not deduped (#587, security-sensitive, PASS, zero findings, see [codebase notes](codebase/587.md)); ships dormant no longer — the [announced-model store](features/announced-model-store.md) (#588, shipped, security-sensitive, PASS one NIT, see [codebase notes](codebase/588.md)) is the first consumer, still dormant pending #560's render surface. [#714](codebase/714.md) — the last arm in the family — later widened the emit to carry `conversationId` by name (security-sensitive, PASS, one NIT), replacing rather than renumbering the arm's arithmetic security clause; the id stops at the announced-model bridge, so `AnnouncedModel` and the store are unaffected. **[#621](codebase/621.md) removed `snapshotReceived` and `screenSnapshotReceived` outright**, along with the `case 'snapshot'` emit that produced both and each of the two no-op cases they forced in all three exhaustive bridges — `runConfigReceived` (#491/#500) had already superseded `snapshotReceived` as the run-config sheet's source, and `screenSnapshotReceived` never gained a reader before #618/#619 removed its consumer and store; the `assertNever` guards still terminate all three switches. **Split 2026-09-01** — the lifecycle and correlation sections now live in their own documents, listed below; this entry stays the map. **Split 2026-09-01** — the event union and the emit/subscribe plumbing now live in their own documents, listed below; this entry stays the map.
    - [Daemon event channel — emit and subscribe](features/daemon-event-channel-plumbing.md) — The two ends of the channel: the helper the main process emits through, the preload subscription the renderer listens on, and the flow between them.
    - [Daemon event channel — the sealed union](features/daemon-event-channel-sealed-union.md) — The event union itself: every arm the main process can emit to the renderer, and the rules that keep the switch over them exhaustive.
    - [Daemon connection — request correlation and diagnostics](features/daemon-connection-correlation.md) — How a reply is matched back to the request that asked for it, one section per correlated round trip, plus the diagnostic logging around them.
    - [Daemon connection — lifecycle](features/daemon-connection-lifecycle.md) — When the connection is dialled, torn down and redialled, how it works internally, and the state and concurrency model that holds it together.
- [Command channel](features/command-channel.md) — the mirror-image window→background command pipe: a sealed `RendererCommand` union + pure `sendMessageCommand` + `isRendererCommand` boundary guard in `src/shared/ipc/`, a typed `sendCommand` on `window.pyry`, and an `onCommand` receiver seam that validates each command at the untrusted renderer→main boundary (#17). Gained a second, **bare** member — `requestDebugBundle` (no payload; guard case is a bare `return true`) — triggering the debug-bundle download (#168); now handled in `main/index.ts`'s `onCommand` switch by the [debug-bundle orchestrator](features/debug-bundle-orchestrator.md) (#169, see below). Gained a third, **payload-carrying** member — `requestSnapshot{conversation_id}`, reusing the wire `RequestSnapshotPayload` verbatim, guarded by a new `isRequestSnapshotPayload` — triggering the on-demand model/effort/YOLO fetch (#180, see below). Gained a fourth, bare member, `requestConversations` (#139). Gained a fifth and sixth, payload-carrying pair — `answerModal{modal_id,option_id}` (payload type `AnswerModalCommandPayload = Omit<ModalAnswerPayload,'answer_token'>` — the token structurally excluded, minted main-side instead) and `cancelModal{modal_id}` (reusing the wire `ModalCancelPayload` verbatim) — guarded in lockstep by new `isAnswerModalPayload`/`isCancelModalPayload` (structural minimum; neither rejects a smuggled `answer_token`, since the main-side sender's fresh-literal construction is the actual net). Routed by `main/index.ts`'s `onCommand` switch to the [daemon connection](features/daemon-connection.md)'s new `answerModal`/`cancelModal` methods (#236, see [modal resolution envelope](features/modal-resolution-envelope.md), below). Both members now have a renderer dispatch site: `PermissionModal`'s option buttons and its new leading Cancel affordance, via the `modalResolution.ts` helper — closing the modal vertical (#237, see [conversation shell](features/conversation-shell.md), above). Gained a seventh, **payload-carrying** member — `createConversation{is_promoted,name,cwd}`, reusing the wire `CreateConversationPayload` verbatim, guarded by a new `isCreateConversationPayload` that checks each field's presence as well as type (all three are nullable-and-present, never optional) — the write-side twin of `requestSnapshot`, triggering the daemon-side conversation-create round trip (#241, see [conversation create](features/conversation-create.md), below). Gained an eighth, **payload-carrying** member — `setSessionSettings{session_id,model?,effort?,yolo?}`, reusing the wire `SetSessionSettingsPayload` verbatim, guarded by a new `isSetSessionSettingsPayload` — the first **optional-absent** (rather than nullable-present) presence check: each optional field, when present, must be correctly typed, and an absent optional is accepted outright — triggering the [session settings send](features/session-settings-send.md) round trip; ships dormant, no consumer until #257 (#263, see below). That eighth member gained a top-level sibling field, `changeId: string` — a renderer-minted, client-internal correlation key riding alongside `payload` (never nested inside it, so it stays structurally off the wire), guarded by a new `'changeId' in value && typeof value.changeId === 'string'` clause in `isRendererCommand` (#261, security-sensitive, PASS, see [session settings send](features/session-settings-send.md), [codebase notes](codebase/261.md)). Gained a ninth, **payload-carrying** member — `promoteConversation{conversation_id,name,cwd}`, reusing the wire `PromoteConversationPayload` verbatim, guarded by a new `isPromoteConversationPayload` that clones `isSendMessagePayload`'s present-and-string checks — the deliberate opposite of `isCreateConversationPayload`: all three fields are **required**, so a literal `null` is rejected, not accepted — triggering the daemon-side promote round trip; the write-side twin of `createConversation` (#273, see [conversation promote](features/conversation-promote.md), below). Gained a tenth, **payload-carrying** member — `unarchiveConversation{conversation_id}`, reusing the wire `UnarchiveConversationPayload` verbatim, guarded by a new `isUnarchiveConversationPayload` cloned from `isRequestSnapshotPayload`'s single-field shape rather than `isPromoteConversationPayload`'s trio — triggering the daemon-side unarchive round trip; ships dormant, first caller is #348 (#346, see [conversation unarchive](features/conversation-unarchive.md)). Gained an eleventh, **payload-carrying** member — `renameConversation{conversation_id,name}`, reusing the wire `RenameConversationPayload` verbatim, guarded by a new `isRenameConversationPayload` cloned from `isPromoteConversationPayload` with the `cwd` check dropped (two required strings, checked for type not emptiness — an empty `name` passes) — triggering the daemon-side rename round trip; ships dormant, intended first caller is the not-yet-built Rename dialog #360 (#359, see [conversation rename](features/conversation-rename.md)). Gained a twelfth, **payload-carrying** member — `archiveConversation{conversation_id}`, reusing a new, distinct wire `ArchiveConversationPayload` (deliberately not an alias of `UnarchiveConversationPayload`), guarded by a new `isArchiveConversationPayload` cloned from `isUnarchiveConversationPayload`'s single-field shape — the mirror-image twin of the tenth member, triggering the daemon-side archive round trip; ships dormant, intended first caller is the not-yet-built Channel Info sheet's Archive action #366 (#363, see [conversation archive](features/conversation-archive.md)). Gained a thirteenth, **payload-carrying** member — `deleteConversation{conversation_id}`, reusing a new, distinct wire `DeleteConversationPayload` (deliberately not an alias of either archive/unarchive payload), guarded by a new `isDeleteConversationPayload` cloned from `isUnarchiveConversationPayload`'s single-field shape — the **permanent** hard-delete verb, whose reply is a distinct, correlated `conversation_deleted{id}` record with **no broadcast** (unlike archive/unarchive's shared `conversation_updated` broadcast, so there is no free re-list reflection); ships dormant, intended first caller is the not-yet-built Channel Info sheet's Delete action #367 (#364, see [conversation delete](features/conversation-delete.md)). Gained a fourteenth, **payload-carrying** member — `changeWorkspace{conversation_id,cwd}`, reusing a new, distinct wire `ChangeWorkspacePayload` (field tag `cwd`, not `workspace` — the daemon #823 reconcile point), guarded by a new `isChangeWorkspacePayload` cloned from `isRenameConversationPayload` with the second field's key `name` → `cwd` — the field-count twin of rename, not of the single-field archive/unarchive/delete siblings — triggering the daemon-side workspace-move round trip; confirmed by the **existing** `conversation_updated` reply, so list-reflect is free (no new inbound decode); shipped dormant, intended first caller was the not-yet-built Workspace Picker UI (#157's remaining split-child) — wired live by [#383](codebase/383.md)'s `requestChangeWorkspace` helper (#379, see [conversation workspace change](features/conversation-workspace-change.md)). Gained a fifteenth, **bare** member — `requestRecentWorkspaces` (#380), the `requestConversations`/`requestDebugBundle` bare-member precedent — triggering the daemon-side recent-workspaces read round trip; shipped dormant; #382's [recent-workspaces store](features/recent-workspaces-store.md) bridge fires it as a de-duplicated one-shot on mount, itself shipped dormant — the Workspace Picker UI, #157's remaining split-child, mounted that bridge as its first real consumer in [#383](codebase/383.md) (#380, security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/380.md)). Gained a sixteenth, **payload-carrying** member — `createWorkspaceFolder{parent,name}`, reusing a new, distinct wire `CreateWorkspaceFolderPayload` (deliberately not an alias of `ChangeWorkspacePayload`), guarded by a new `isCreateWorkspaceFolderPayload` cloned from `isChangeWorkspacePayload` with the field keys rekeyed `conversation_id`/`cwd` → `parent`/`name` — the full-vertical write-side twin of `createConversation`, triggering the daemon-side create-folder round trip whose direct, correlated `workspace_folder_created{path}` reply flattens to the new `workspaceFolderCreated` [Daemon-event channel](features/daemon-event-channel.md) member (above); ships dormant, intended first caller is the not-yet-built Create-folder dialog #157 (#381, security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/381.md)). Gained a twelfth, **payload-carrying** member — `notify{kind}` (`kind: 'turn-complete' | 'prompt'`) — the sole member whose payload type is main-local (defined in `commands.ts`, not `../wire/types`, since it never reaches the transport) and whose guard, `isNotifyPayload`, checks closed-set membership rather than the sibling `typeof === 'string'` shape — triggering the [push notifications](features/push-notifications.md) delivery primitive (#391, see [codebase notes](codebase/391.md)); its first real sender is #392's renderer trigger (see [codebase notes](codebase/392.md)), shipped. **The third member, `requestSnapshot{conversation_id}`, was removed in [#620](codebase/620.md)**: gone from the union, from `isRendererCommand`'s switch, and from `isRequestSnapshotPayload` (deleted); the shape now falls through to the switch's default-deny, asserted by a new positive rejection test. Every other member listed above is unaffected.
- [Push notifications](features/push-notifications.md) — the main-process delivery primitive that raises a native OS `Notification` for a closed-`kind` `notify` command, but only when the main window is unfocused (`isFocused()` read synchronously at fire-time, no stateful tracker); title/body come from a main-owned `Record<NotifyKind,…>` copy table, never the command, so no daemon-relayed text can reach a notification. Slice 1 of 3 split from #158 (#391, see [codebase notes](codebase/391.md)). [#393](codebase/393.md) made the notification actionable: on click, a new injected `activateWindow` helper (restore-if-minimized, show, focus) brings the window forward, and a new **bare, main-local** `notificationActivated` `DaemonEvent` — the first arm on that channel not derived from a validated wire envelope — is consumed by a new filter-bridge (`notificationActivatedBridge`, mounted in [Paired shell](features/paired-shell.md) beside `useConversationCreatedNav`) that dispatches the existing `open` nav, landing on the thread view regardless of which paired view was showing; no `setActiveConversation` call, since the click carries no payload. Not security-sensitive, not UI-visible. Code review PASS, no findings (#393). [#392](codebase/392.md), the last of the three-way split, then made the feature live end-to-end: a new filter-bridge `pushNotifyBridge.ts` (`notifyKindForEvent` `switch`/`default: null` filter — deliberately not `assertNever`, so it introduces no new `DaemonEvent` arm — owning exactly `turnEnd → 'turn-complete'` and `modalShown → 'prompt'`; `subscribePushNotify` filters first then reads the [push-notification preference store](features/push-notification-preference-store.md) as a **per-event thunk** before sending `{type:'notify',payload:{kind}}`; `usePushNotify` mounts in [Paired shell](features/paired-shell.md) beside the other bridges, no `useRef` needed since all deps are module-level singletons). Not security-sensitive, not UI-visible. Code review PASS, no findings (#392). [#518](codebase/518.md) made both direct window touches destroyed-safe: `ActivatableWindow` gained a required `isDestroyed()` checked before `isMinimized()`, and a new `windowHasFocus(win: FocusableWindow)` helper — extracted since `index.ts` has no peer test — replaces the inline `mainWindow.isFocused()` closure, reporting a destroyed window as unfocused (correct: it can't hold focus) without touching the throwing `isFocused()`. Security-sensitive, code review PASS (1 cosmetic NIT). [#519](codebase/519.md) made both touches reach the **current** window instead of a safely-ignored destroyed one: `windowHasFocus`/`activateWindow` now route through the new [live-window](features/live-window.md) holder's `window` face, re-attached on every dock reopen. Security-sensitive, code review FAIL (1 MUST FIX: AC2's `did-finish-load`-after-React-passive-effect delivery ordering was unproven at every level) → rework added a permanent e2e spec (`e2e/window-reopen-converges.spec.ts`) observing and pinning the convergence with zero production change → PASS (1 cosmetic NIT). [#514](codebase/514.md) hardened the #392 trigger against the daemon's connect-time re-send of still-outstanding `modal_shown` frames ([#415](codebase/415.md) reconcile): `subscribePushNotify` now opens a closure-local `Set<string>` of announced `modalId`s and skips both the toggle read and the send when a `modalShown`'s id is already in it, recording the id only **after** a successful send so a delivery dropped by the push toggle is never marked announced. The set deliberately lives in the subscription closure, not the [modal store](features/modal-prompt-model.md) — since [#510](codebase/510.md) that store clears both `outstanding` and `resolved` on every `reconnected` edge, so it cannot hold memory that must survive the same edge this bug needs suppressed across; the fix instead leans on `App.tsx`'s `route` never moving on a daemon event, so `PairedShell`'s `usePushNotify` closure spans every reconnect without remounting. No pruning on `modalDismissed` (would reintroduce #510's swallowed-Allow bug in notification form) and no cap (unobserved failure mode). `turnEnd` untouched by construction — `modalId` is `null` for every other arm. Not security-sensitive (architect self-review PASS), not UI-visible. Code review PASS, 0 MUST/SHOULD, 1 NIT (#514, see [codebase notes](codebase/514.md)).
- [Daemon-event bridge (renderer)](features/daemon-event-bridge.md) — the renderer translation half: pure `translateDaemonEvent` maps each `DaemonEvent` to a `SessionAction`, and the `useDaemonEventBridge` hook pipes `onDaemonEvent → translate → dispatch` into the app-singleton store, unsubscribing cleanly on teardown (#19). Return type widened to `SessionAction | null` — the three debug-bundle events (above) map to `null` (consumed by the #72 download UI, not the store) and the hook skips dispatch on `null`; the `assertNever` exhaustiveness guard is unchanged, though this module's status as the *only* exhaustive `DaemonEvent` consumer stopped holding once #202 and #223 shipped a second and third (#168). Gained a fourth `null`-returning case, `snapshotReceived` — the concrete case study that the spec's "renderer needs zero change" claim didn't hold for this exhaustive switch (#180, see below); the reserved consumer landed as the [Run configuration store](features/run-config-store.md)'s data path (#187, see below). Gained a fifth and sixth `null`-returning case, `assistantDelta`/`turnEnd` — both carry real content (unlike every prior tolerated member) but still map to `null` here since their consumer is the [conversation timeline store](features/conversation-timeline-store.md)'s bridge, not this session-store bridge (#199, consumer landed #202). Gained an eighth `null`-returning case, `turnState` — the first `DaemonEvent` arm added since the timeline bridge existed, so it forced a compile-time case in both `assertNever`-guarded switches at once (#214). Gained a ninth `null`-returning case, `toolUse` — the second arm to force that same compile-time case in both switches at once (#217). Gained a tenth and eleventh `null`-returning case, `modalShown`/`modalDismissed` — the third pair to force both switches at once, and the first pair the timeline bridge ALSO discards (added to its inverse-filter list, not its owned block) since neither store consumes a modal (#201); the real consumer is the third, independent [modal store + bridge](features/modal-store-bridge.md) (#223, see below). Gained a twelfth `null`-returning case, `toolResult` — the outcome half of `toolUse`, the vertical's last transport slice; consumed by the timeline bridge's fifth owned arm (the first to **resolve** an existing item rather than append or set a scalar), not this bridge. By now `modalBridge.ts` (#223) also exists, so this is the first arm to force a case in **three** exhaustive `DaemonEvent` switches at once, not two — a cost the #229 spec's own scope self-check (written before #223 merged) undercounted by one file (#229, see below). Gained a thirteenth `null`-returning case, `conversationCreated` — the write-side twin of `conversationsReceived`; consumed by neither existing bridge, real consumer is the render sibling #242, not this bridge (#241, see below). Gained a fourteenth `null`-returning case, `sessionTransition` — the session-boundary marker's addressing id; consumed by none of the three exhaustive `DaemonEvent` switches' owning blocks (#254, see below); its real consumer is the [session-id store](features/session-id-store.md)'s independent App-level subscriber ([#259](codebase/259.md), see below). Gained a fifteenth `null`-returning case, `modalAnswerRejected` — the main-side-correlated rejection arm; owned (still dormant) by the [modal store + bridge](features/modal-store-bridge.md), not this bridge (#248, security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/248.md)). Gained a sixteenth `null`-returning case, `sessionSettingsUpdated` — consumed by none of the three exhaustive `DaemonEvent` switches' owning blocks; real consumer is #261 / #256, not yet built (#264, see [codebase notes](codebase/264.md)). Gained a further `null`-returning case, `conversationUpdated` — the write-side twin of `conversationCreated`, forced by the same `assertNever` guard across all three exhaustive bridges at once; real consumer is the list-reflect slice #275, not yet built (#273, see [conversation promote](features/conversation-promote.md), below). Gained a further `null`-returning case, `stallDetected` — the daemon's onset-only `stall` liveness signal, at ship time **nullary** (its one wire field was dropped at the emit); forced across all three exhaustive bridges at once, same as `conversationUpdated`; this bridge (the session store) still nulls it, but the [conversation timeline store](features/conversation-timeline-store.md)'s bridge has since claimed it as an owned arm, its real consumer (#315, security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/315.md); render landed #317, see below). [#732](codebase/732.md) later widened it with `conversationId` (security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/732.md)) — a daemon-asserted routing key reaching no sink; this bridge still nulls it. Gained a nineteenth `null`-returning case, `screenSnapshotReceived` — unlike `stallDetected`, a deliberate widening carrying real content (`text`/`ts`, the rendered daemon screen, reversing `snapshotReceived`'s #180 content-drop now that a consumer exists) that this bridge still discards; forced across all three exhaustive bridges at once; real consumer is the display slice #318, not yet built (#316, security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/316.md)). Gained a twentieth `null`-returning case, `relayLinkChanged` — the relay-socket leg, forced across all three exhaustive bridges at once (the `stallDetected`/`screenSnapshotReceived` dormant-arm precedent); real consumer is the not-yet-built relay-link store #329 (#328, security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/328.md)). Gained a twenty-first `null`-returning case, `conversationDeleted` — the correlated permanent-delete confirmation, forced across all three exhaustive bridges at once (the `conversationUpdated`/`stallDetected`/`relayLinkChanged` dormant-arm precedent); real consumer is the list-reflect slice #376 — shipped as the widened `shouldRefreshList` predicate in [conversation list store](features/conversation-list-store.md)'s bridge, not this bridge (#375, security-sensitive, PASS, see [conversation delete](features/conversation-delete.md), [codebase notes](codebase/375.md)). Gained a further `null`-returning case, `apiRetry` — `stallDetected`'s peer, but non-nullary (carries `active`/`current`/`total`) and neither onset-only nor deduped; forced across all three exhaustive bridges at once (#492, security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/492.md)); this bridge (the session store) still nulls it, but the [conversation timeline store](features/conversation-timeline-store.md)'s bridge has since claimed it as a seventh owned arm, render landed #493 (see [codebase notes](codebase/493.md)). Gained a further `null`-returning case, `compacting` — `apiRetry`'s peer, but **banner-only** (carries only `active`, no counter); like `apiRetry`, neither onset-only nor deduped; forced across all three exhaustive bridges at once (#495, security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/495.md)); still dormant — the render slice #496 is the first consumer. **[#621](codebase/621.md) removed the `snapshotReceived`/`screenSnapshotReceived` cases this bridge tolerated** — both were unconsumed (the run-config data path moved onto `runConfigReceived` at #491/#500, and `screenSnapshotReceived` never outlived #618/#619's removal of its own consumer) — the `assertNever` default still terminates the switch.
- [Push-notification preference store](features/push-notification-preference-store.md) — the renderer-local, persisted on/off `boolean` for "push notifications when claude responds", cloning [defaultWorkspaceStore](features/default-workspace-store.md) (#403)'s injected-storage-port idiom; defaults to enabled on empty storage (mobile knob's on-position, Figma 17-68), with a `null`-vs-stored-`false` sentinel via extracted `encodePushPref`/`decodePushPref` so an explicit toggle-off survives restarts. Renders nothing itself — the data half, consumed by [#392](codebase/392.md) (read, gates firing on [Push notifications](features/push-notifications.md), shipped) and [#409](codebase/409.md) (write, the [Settings screen](features/settings-screen.md)'s Notifications-section toggle, Figma 17-64, shipped) (#408, see [codebase notes](codebase/408.md)).
- [Relay connection](features/relay-connection.md) — the background-process connection primitive: `createRelayConnection` opens one `wss://` socket with caller-supplied headers, a 30s/30s heartbeat, a 1 MiB frame cap, carrying raw frames both ways as opaque bytes and funnelling every failure into one terminal `closed` event; semantics-blind, no reconnect (that's #22). Adds `ws`, the project's first network dependency (#21). Now **content-free-log by construction** (was log-free): an optional injected [`DiagnosticLog`](features/diagnostic-log.md) logs three lifecycle sites — `relay-open` / `relay-unexpected-response` (captures the relay's 404 HTTP upgrade status, lost until now) / `relay-closed` — carrying only event name, numeric status/close code, a module-static classification, and safe host+path; never a header, a frame byte, the URL query, or the wire close reason. The `unexpected-response` handler also self-terminates so the refused-upgrade terminal stays byte-identical despite `ws` suppressing its auto-destroy (#127).
- [Relay supervisor](features/relay-supervisor.md) — the resilience layer over #21: `createRelaySupervisor` wraps `createRelayConnection` by injection so the layers above see a connection that heals itself — re-dials after a retryable close with capped exponential backoff + ±20% jitter (1s→30s), resets the backoff after ≥60s continuously up, treats a fatal close code (`{4401,4421,4426}`, injected) as terminal to block a reconnect-storm DoS, tears down with no dangling timers, and forwards inbound frames byte-for-byte. Main-process only, log-free, no new dependency (#22). Now takes an optional per-dial `resolveConnection` provider: `dial()` is async and every *automatic* re-dial re-sources the connection from it (a null record fails closed with the synthetic `NO_PAIRED_RECORD_CLOSE_CODE = 4000`), fenced against a racing `stop()` by a post-`await` check; the first dial stays on the construction-time snapshot (reload-per-dial, #83). Now also surfaces a retryable `closed` rather than only absorbing it: `RelaySupervisorEvent` gains `relay-closed{code}`, emitted on the `else` branch **after** the re-dial is armed (`backoffTimer`/`attempt++` first) so a misbehaving sink can never strand recovery; the fatal `terminal` path is unchanged (#328, see [relay-link status](features/daemon-connection.md), [codebase notes](codebase/328.md)).
- [Secure store](features/secure-store.md) — the background-process secret-at-rest primitive: a key-domain-blind `createSecureStore` (`set`/`get`/`delete` over named `Uint8Array` blobs) that encrypts at rest with Electron `safeStorage` (OS keychain), fails closed when the keychain is unavailable (incl. the Linux `basic_text` obfuscation backend) so plaintext never hits disk, and ships its keychain + filesystem edges as injected seams (real adapters: `electronSecretEncryption` base64-bridged over `safeStorage`, `fileSecretPersistence` traversal-safe + atomic). Main-process only, log-free, no new dependency; the foundation #43/#44 consume (#42). The injected `encryption` seam is now chosen at the composition root by `selectSecretEncryption` — the real keychain backend on every path except a non-packaged, opt-in dev path that swaps in a keychain-free backend so `set` stops failing closed headless (#99, see below).
- [Device static keypair](features/device-keypair.md) — the desktop client's stable Noise_IK identity: a device static X25519 keypair generated once and persisted through #42's secure store, so every launch reads the same pair without the private key ever leaving the store or reaching the renderer. `createDeviceKeypairStore` is a pure core over two injected seams (`SecureStore` type-only + a `KeyPairGenerator`, real impl `noiseKeyPairGenerator` over `noise-c.wasm` `CreateKeyPair(NOISE_DH_CURVE25519)`); `ensure()` is generate-once (memoized promise + persisted 64-byte `priv‖pub` blob as source of truth), fail-loud on corruption (never silently regenerates), and log-free. Main-process only, no new dependency; the first consumer of #42, feeding the eventual Noise_IK handshake (#43).
- [Paired-server store](features/paired-server-store.md) — the persisted pairing record: the `{server, relay, token, server_static_pubkey}` QR/paste tuple serialized to one opaque JSON blob and stored through #42's secure store, so the client reconnects and drives the Noise_IK handshake each launch without re-pairing. `createPairedServerStore` is a pure core over one injected `SecureStore` seam (serialization is pure — no second adapter); `save` overwrites (re-pair), fail-closed on keychain-unavailable; `load` returns `null` only on genuine absence — a decrypt failure propagates and a present-but-malformed blob throws `MalformedPairedServerRecordError`, so a tampered record is never silently mistaken for never-paired. `PairedServerRecord` aliases `QrPayload` (wire drift is a type error). Main-process only, log-free, no new dependency; the second consumer of #42 (#44). Gained a symmetric, fail-closed `clear()` erase (#172) on a widened `ClearablePairedServerStore` return type — no fixture cascade, structurally cannot touch the device static keypair; now reachable from the renderer via the [unpair channel](features/unpair-channel.md) (#173, see below). `load()` gained a second, read-only renderer-reachable path via the [server-info channel](features/server-info-channel.md) — exposes exactly `record.server`/`record.relay` (never `token`/`server_static_pubkey`), the disconnected-safe source for a future Settings screen; the renderer consumer is the [server-info store](features/server-info-store.md)'s one-shot loader (#339, #340, see below).
- [Unpair channel](features/unpair-channel.md) — the renderer→main IPC surface that triggers `pairedServerStore.clear()`, returning the app to a clean not-paired state: byte-for-byte twin of the [pairing-status signal](features/pairing-status-signal.md) (#79/#80) — `UNPAIR_CHANNEL` + value-free `UnpairResult = { result: 'ok' } | { result: 'error' }` in `src/shared/ipc/unpair.ts`, an injected-target `registerUnpairHandler` (classify-don't-forward, log-free, store dep typed against `ClearablePairedServerStore`), a fixed-channel `window.pyry.unpair()` preload method, and composition-root registration alongside the pairing-status handler over the same store. Shipped unwired — the #131→#134 "ship the boundary ahead of its consumer" shape a third time (#173) — and now gets its first caller: the conversation screen's confirm-guarded unpair control (#166, see above). Gained an optional `onUnpaired?: () => void` dep, fired once after a successful `clear()`, outside the fail-closed catch and swallowing its own throw (the deliberate inverse of `onPaired`'s inside-the-try placement — `runUnpair` coerces both an `error` result and a rejected invoke to the same "stay put" outcome, so a teardown throw must never relabel an already-completed erase as failed); wired at the composition root to `connection.reconnect()`, closing the live-session-outlives-the-erased-record gap this doc used to list as an accepted edge case. Registration moved below `createDaemonConnection` to close over `connection` (#504, security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/504.md)). `runUnpair`'s ok branch used to also reset `sessionStore` directly; [#531](codebase/531.md) moved that reset upstream into a shared `PairedShell`-level clear wrapped around `onUnpaired`, so this channel's own contract (and `runUnpair`'s two `ConversationScreen.tsx` call sites) stayed untouched. Gained a second optional dep, `hostLabel?: Pick<HostLabelStore, 'clear'>`, and one more erase inside the listener: once `store.clear()` resolves, the listener also awaits `hostLabel?.clear()` in its own throw-dropping catch, ordered before `onUnpaired`, so the [host label](features/host-label-store.md) can no longer outlive the record it described. The record erase alone still gates `ok` — a failing label erase never downgrades it, by the same "the result reports on the record" rule #504's `onUnpaired` placement already established; the `Pick<…, 'clear'>` typing withholds `load`, so the label string is structurally unreachable in this module. Composition root reuses the one `hostLabelStore` instance, not a second one (#827, security-sensitive, architect self-review PASS).
- [Server-info channel](features/server-info-channel.md) — the third twin in the [pairing-status signal](features/pairing-status-signal.md)/[unpair channel](features/unpair-channel.md) family, and the first with a non-empty present arm: `SERVER_INFO_CHANNEL` + `ServerInfo = { status: 'available'; serverId: string; relayUrl: string } | { status: 'unavailable' }` in `src/shared/ipc/serverInfo.ts` — value-free-by-construction relaxed to exactly two non-secret fields (`token`/`server_static_pubkey` structurally absent), so a Settings screen can show which server is paired even while disconnected. `registerServerInfoHandler` reads `pairedServerStore.load()` fresh each call, builds the available arm as an explicit `{ serverId: record.server, relayUrl: record.relay }` literal (never a spread), and collapses every non-readable case (`null`, malformed, propagated decrypt failure) to a value-free `unavailable` with the caught error dropped, never logged. Reads only the at-rest record, never the live `hello_ack.server_id`. Shipped unwired — the #131→#134 shape a fourth time — the renderer store is #340, the visible Settings row is #334 (#339, security-sensitive, PASS).
- [Server-info store](features/server-info-store.md) — the renderer-side half of the server-info channel above: a dedicated `serverInfoStore` holding `{ serverId, relayUrl } | null` (the `sessionIdStore` DI-factory→singleton→hook→selector shape, absence-distinct `null`) plus `serverInfoLoader.ts` — a pure `mapServerInfo` collapse of the `ServerInfo` union (drops the `status` discriminant, never a partial) and a one-shot `loadServerInfo`/`ServerInfoData` pair that reuses `App.tsx`'s `pairingStatus()` mount-effect shape (`active`-flag guard against a StrictMode double-write), not a daemon-event subscription — there is no push event behind this value. Shipped dormant in #340, zero `App.tsx` edit; [#334](codebase/334.md) mounts `<ServerInfoData />` inside the paired-only Settings tree, not app-level, so a same-session pair is reflected on next open rather than requiring a relaunch, and renders the store's value in a new Server row (`ServerRow`/`ServerRowControl`, the #330 view/container split) — populated shows `serverId`+`relayUrl`, `null` shows a `Loading…` placeholder; an empty, unlabelled-by-`aria-label` host slot awaits the future #330-style two-dot indicator (#340, see [codebase notes](codebase/340.md); #334, not security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/334.md)).
- [Wire codec](features/wire-codec.md) — the transport's serialization layer in `src/main/transport/codec.ts`: pure `encode/decodeInnerFrame` + `encode/decodeEnvelope` + strict `base64Std*` + the one default-injecting `makeHelloClientPayload`, producing bytes byte-identical to pyrycode-mobile. Encode is total; the `decode*` functions fail closed with a category-only `WireDecodeError`; `Envelope.payload` stays opaque (`unknown`). Main-process only (structural renderer isolation); tightens `types.ts` optionals to `number | undefined` (never `null`) (#5).
- [Noise session](features/noise-session.md) — the production `Noise_IK_25519_ChaChaPoly_BLAKE2s` session in `src/main/transport/`: `createNoiseSession` performs the IK handshake (injected `hello` on msg 1, `hello_ack` from msg 2) then AEAD-encrypts/decrypts transport frames, fail-closed, over the vetted `noise-c.wasm`. A pure crypto unit — keys injected (raw 32-byte X25519), `hello`/`helloAck` opaque, `NOISE_PROTOCOL` reused verbatim; mirrors the relay-connection `sendFrame`/`onEvent` contract. Promotes the #29/#30-proven harness by `git mv` + rename and adds `noiseLib.ts`, a single hardened wasm loader (bounded `NoiseLoadError` timeout/classify instead of an async-load hang) shared with the device-keypair generator. Log-free, no use-after-close, main-process only; not yet wired to the relay (sibling ticket) (#7). Now also **recognizes** the daemon's in-session `rekey_request` control frame in `transport` state: a narrow type-peek (reusing the fail-closed `decodeEnvelope`, swallowing its throw) diverts a positively-classified `rekey_request` to a new bare `rekey-requested` `NoiseSessionEvent` instead of the app-`message` path that `parseInboundMessage` silently drops — purely additive (identical app-message bytes still flow through), fail-closed (any peek failure falls through), module-private constant kept OUT of `EnvelopeType` (mirrors the daemon's `v1TypeSet` exclusion, no wire drift), a bare byte-free signal; the driver drops it as a build-integrity guard (#108). On that recognized trigger the session now also **acts** (#111): it runs a fresh in-session IK re-handshake as INITIATOR (same injected static keys + `NOISE_PROTOCOL`, **empty** early-data both ways — no `hello` re-sent) and, on the daemon's reply, **atomically** swaps its cipher states (install both new, then free both old — never half-swapped), resuming transport under the new keys so a long-lived session outlives the daemon's ~1-hour rekey interval instead of dying at WS 4426; a failed / wrong-state rekey returns to `transport` on the intact old ciphers (**not** `closed`), reusing `handshake-read-failed` (no new reason, no new event, no driver change), and reusing the pinned daemon static IS the client-side peer-continuity guarantee (no phantom compare). The fresh msg1 is now framed `noise_init` on the wire (driver-local latch, no session change) and the daemon-initiated rekey round-trip is proven end-to-end against a rekey-initiating fakeDaemon (#112) (#111). Now **content-free-logs** the three **inbound-read** catches only (`transport-decrypt-failed`, and `handshake-read-failed` at the msg-2 read and the rekey-reply read) through an optional injected [`DiagnosticLog`](features/diagnostic-log.md): a `failWithFrame` helper logs the capped raw pre-decryption frame (ciphertext / handshake material — never plaintext, never the token-bearing outbound msg1) before calling the unchanged `fail(reason)`; `onEvent` still surfaces no bytes (#133). `onFrame` now takes an optional `innerType?: string` — the frame's `InnerFrameV2.type`, forwarded by the driver — read in exactly one state, `awaiting-rekey-reply`: labelled `noise_resp` (or unlabelled, the pre-fix/production-unreachable default) still routes to the handshake-reply read unchanged; anything else decrypts under the still-live OLD receive cipher and surfaces as an ordinary `message`, self-looping with `hs`/state untouched on a decrypt failure so the one-shot reply slot survives. Fixes a permanent-desync bug where an app frame the daemon fanned out during its own rekey window (spec #450) was TCP-ordered ahead of the reply and consumed as it, MAC-failing the client into stale old ciphers while the daemon had already swapped — a mismatch the relay then tore down at WS 4421 ([#532](codebase/532.md), following [#524](codebase/524.md)/[#525](codebase/525.md)'s daemon-side and tagging predecessors). Now also fixes the **outbound** mirror of that same window: `sendMessage` no longer drops a send issued in `awaiting-rekey-reply` — it holds a copy of the plaintext (bounded by exported `MAX_BUFFERED_SENDS = 8`, overflow surfaced as `rekey-send-buffer-full` and the incoming send dropped, never the oldest) and flushes it, sealed under the **new** send cipher and re-entering `sendMessage` itself (so a live cipher and issue order both hold), strictly after the atomic swap completes; sealing under the surviving old cipher was rejected as fatal (WS 4421), not merely lossy, since a windowed send is TCP-ordered behind the client's own `noise_init` and the daemon swaps both ciphers on processing it. A failed rekey discards the buffer unsent (`rekey-send-abandoned`, cleared before either error emits) and `close()` releases it silently; both new reasons reach `daemonConnection`'s existing generic forwarding with zero fan-out ([#533](codebase/533.md), the outbound sibling of #532).
- [Noise relay driver](features/noise-relay-driver.md) — the composition adapter in `src/main/transport/` that drives the #7 Noise session over the #22 supervisor: `createNoiseRelayDriver` starts a **fresh** handshake on every `(re)connect` (v2 has no session resume), composes the #5 codec between the supervisor's opaque frames and the session's Noise bytes (outbound tagged `noise_init`/`noise_msg`, inbound decoded fail-closed and never `type`-branched), and surfaces `handshake-complete`/`message`/`terminal`/`error` through one typed sink to the background-process consumer above. A `generation` counter fences the async `createSession` against synchronous supervisor events so a stale session can never install or send under a superseded key (the security-load-bearing nonce-freshness guard); the pending buffer is bounded at `MAX_PENDING_FRAMES = 8`. No new crypto — three proven primitives consumed unchanged; keys/`hello` injected from above (#42/#43/#10); main-process only, log-free, no renderer/IPC reach; not yet wired to the renderer (#17/#18/#19) (#50). Now exports `SessionMaterial`/`DialConfig`/`DialConfigProvider` and takes an optional `loadDialConfig`: a `resolveConnection` wrapper is the single per-dial `load()` that feeds the supervisor's connection AND stashes the next `onConnected`'s session from the same record snapshot (so headers and key never split on a mid-session re-pair), catches a thrown reload → returns null (fail closed), and gates the first connect onto `config.session` (reload-per-dial, #83). A one-shot `rekeyInitPending` latch beside `firstFrame` now re-arms `noise_init` framing for the session's fresh in-session rekey msg1: `route` arms it on the (still-dropped) `rekey-requested` trigger and `sendFrame` consumes it on the next outbound frame, which the session's synchronous emit-then-send ordering guarantees is the rekey msg1 — so the daemon routes it to its rekey responder instead of transport-decrypting the handshake bytes (WS 4421) (#112). Now also the **DI convergence point** for pre-decryption byte logging: an optional injected `diagnosticLog` logs the capped raw frame at the `onMessage` framing catch (`inbound-frame-decode-failed`) and is forwarded — the driver's own constant, not carried on `SessionMaterial` — into every session it builds, so the session's own inbound-read catches log through the same sink (#133). Now also forwards the relay-socket leg as its own signal, unclassified: on every supervisor `connected` it emits `relay-link-up` *in addition to* starting the fresh handshake (a second consumer of the same event), and a new `case 'relay-closed'` forwards `relay-link-down{code}` verbatim, the raw code left for [daemon connection](features/daemon-connection.md) to classify (#328, see below). Now **forwards the decoded inner `type` verbatim** alongside `.data` to `session.onFrame(raw, type)` on live delivery, and the `pending` async-create-gap buffer widened from `Uint8Array[]` to `{raw, type}[]` so a replayed frame keeps its real tag instead of `undefined` — pure pass-through, no routing decision at this layer; the [Noise session](features/noise-session.md) is the sole consumer of the label, in exactly one state ([#532](codebase/532.md)).
- [Pairing-payload gate](features/pairing-payload-gate.md) — the paste-only pairing input gate: a pure, synchronous, log-free `parsePairingPayload` in `src/main/` that parses the daemon's `base64url(JSON)` four-field tuple into a `QrPayload` (imported, not re-declared) with a bounded 8-category typed rejection taxonomy, and validates the `relay` URL (`wss:` only, exact-host match against a single-sourced main-process `RELAY_ALLOWLIST`, no embedded userinfo) — the only place the relay is checked before the transport dials it verbatim. Returns a discriminated `ok`/`reason` result (never throws, never leaks a field value); `server_static_pubkey`/`server`-id kept opaque (the fingerprint visual-verify in #53 is the load-bearing key check). No persistence/crypto/IPC/UI; validated output consumed by #53 (#52). The relay's scheme+host decision is now an injectable `RelayPolicy` (default = production strictness), the seam #97's loopback affordance swaps on the dev path (below).
- [Pairing-confirmation gate](features/pairing-confirmation.md) — the desktop's fingerprint-confirm gate in `src/main/`: `createPairingConfirmation({ store })` whose `prepare(record)` derives a short, human-comparable BLAKE2s-256 fingerprint of a validated record's `server_static_pubkey` (first 8 bytes, colon-separated lowercase hex — byte-identical to the daemon's `internal/pair.Fingerprint`, pinned vector `32:0b:5e:a9:9e:65:3b:c2`) and returns the **one** persist handle bound to exactly that record. Persist is confirm-gated structurally: exactly one `store.save` call site, inside the `confirm` closure, unreachable without `prepare` and **absent** on a malformed-key `ok:false` arm (so a bad key is unpersistable). Strict canonical-standard-base64 guard (re-encode-and-compare) is the first place `server_static_pubkey`'s shape is checked; a frozen snapshot closes the derive→confirm TOCTOU; catches nothing from the injected [paired-server store](features/paired-server-store.md)'s `save` (fail-closed propagates). Pure core, log-free; the human-verify step mobile's paste path skipped (#501). Only the fingerprint (a hash) is safe to surface — that's #54 (#53). The digest now comes from pure-JS `@noble/hashes` `blake2s`, **not** `node:crypto` `createHash('blake2s256')` — which throws `Digest method not supported` under Electron's BoringSSL (no BLAKE2 family), wedging the built app's pairing screen forever in `submitting`; the swap is byte-identical and synchronous, adds a value-free `fingerprint-unavailable` reject reason (collapsed to `invalid-key` at the handler, no new shared reason), and ships a `check:electron-digest` runtime gate vitest structurally can't cover (#101).
- [Pairing IPC channel](features/pairing-ipc-channel.md) — the typed **request/response** pairing pipe (renderer ↔ main), the request/response twin of the fire-and-forget [command](features/command-channel.md)/[event](features/daemon-event-channel.md) channels: `submitPairingPaste(paste)` → display fingerprint or a value-free typed error, then a bare `confirmPairing()` → success/failure — over `ipcRenderer.invoke`/`ipcMain.handle`. `src/shared/ipc/pairing.ts` ships the sealed `PairingRequest` union, the value-free response vocabulary, and `isPairingRequest` (the untrusted-boundary guard, rejecting non-string **and** over-length pastes before parse runs); `src/main/pairingHandler.ts` composes [#52 parse](features/pairing-payload-gate.md) + [#53 fingerprint/confirm](features/pairing-confirmation.md) behind a single handler holding ≤1 prepared pairing (supersede-on-submit + consume-before-await make "persists exactly once" and "no superseded record" structural); `src/main/index.ts` is the composition root that first constructs the real secure-store chain and registers the handler once. "Only the fingerprint or a value-free reason crosses back" (ADR 0002) is enforced by construction — no response arm has a field that could hold the token or server key (#54). **[#823](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-desktop/issues/823)** then widened `confirm` by one optional field, `label`, the write path for the [host-label store](features/host-label-store.md) (#822, shipped with no caller until now): bounded by a new exported `MAX_HOST_LABEL_LENGTH` (128, declared beside `MAX_PASTE_LENGTH`) at `isPairingRequest`, the guard's only bound on the label's write path; the guard deliberately accepts a *present* `label: undefined` alongside an absent one — structured clone (unlike `JSON.stringify`) preserves an own `undefined` property, so `{ type: 'confirm', label }` with an undefined label must not be rejected, a deliberate asymmetry with `submit`'s required-`paste` rejection. The handler takes `Pick<HostLabelStore, 'save'>` (structurally read/erase-proof) and writes the label — in its own inner try/catch that drops the error — after the record persists and before `onPaired`, so the response keeps reporting on the record (AC5) and the sidebar never observes a paired-but-unnamed host. `confirmPairing` widens to `(label?: string)`, building the pre-#823 request unchanged when omitted — assignable to `PairingBridge`'s zero-arg signature with no renderer edit. Composition root builds the store over the same `secureStore`, no `name` override. Code review PASS, 1 SHOULD (two comments elsewhere, in `unpairHandler.ts`, now cite a stale `pairingHandler.ts` line number that this diff shifted — non-blocking) / 1 NIT. The IPC read path shipped as [Host-label channel](features/host-label-channel.md) (#824). The label input field shipped as part of the [Pairing input screen](features/pairing-input-screen.md) (#825). Erase-on-unpair, closing the gap this ticket made reachable (the label outliving an unpair since #173 clears only `pairedServerStore`), shipped as [Unpair channel](features/unpair-channel.md)'s label erase (#827). Still open: the sidebar row ([#834](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-desktop/issues/834), split from the now-closed #826).
- [Pairing input screen](features/pairing-input-screen.md) — the desktop's paste-only pairing screen (renderer, `src/renderer/src/screens/pairing/`): paste the `pyry pair --print` payload, review the server-key fingerprint, and explicitly confirm — or cancel, discarding the paste. Styled from Figma node `19-54` plus the desktop-specific fingerprint-verify step (#53). A pure, React-free phase machine + IPC effect-runners + `groupFingerprint` formatter (`pairingState.ts`), a pure `PairingView`, and a thin `useReducer`-based `PairingScreen` container that drives the existing #54 preload methods through an injected `PairingBridge`. No secret reaches the renderer (the #54 response arms carry only the fingerprint + value-free reason); the paste is transient/single-use; async is handler-driven (no `useEffect`); ephemeral state resets on remount (`useReducer`, ADR 0006). Mounting/navigation was a later slice (`onPaired`/`onCancel` seams); [#662](codebase/662.md) wired both — `onPaired` since #80, `onCancel` since #662, returning to the [welcome screen](features/welcome-screen.md), which is now reached only by user action, no longer the unpaired app root. Adds 3 M3 tokens to `tokens.css` (#55). Both effect-runners are now total functions that never reject: a rejected or synchronously-throwing bridge call is coerced (bare `catch {}`, mirroring `runUnpair`) to the phase's failure event with the existing `malformed-request` reason, so an infrastructure-level invoke failure no longer wedges the screen in `submitting`/`confirming` with Cancel disabled — previously unrecoverable short of an app restart (#513, security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/513.md)). [#665](codebase/665.md) restyled the paste phase (`editing`/`submitting`) onto desktop's own Figma frame `103-2901`: `EntryCard` → `EntryPage`, a full-window page (`.pairing-page`) replacing the 420px dialog card (`.pairing-card`, kept for reviewing/confirming/paired), an M3 filled field with a new clear control, and the root's `.pairing` class staying constant across both treatments (an e2e contract). Added one new lesson: mutually-exclusive same-tag JSX siblings with no `key` reconcile to a single DOM node, so a `role="alert"` toggle mutated a live node instead of inserting one — fixed with distinct keys, code review MUST FIX resolved in rework `c2e4381`. Security-sensitive (architect self-review PASS): the field moved from `<textarea>` to `<input>`, hardened against autofill/spellcheck/password-manager surfaces. Code review FAIL → PASS. [#825](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-desktop/issues/825) added a second, optional field to the same paste-phase hero — the operator's host-name label for [#823](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-desktop/issues/823)'s pairing-path write, threaded through the phase union alongside `paste` (typed on `editing`, sent on `confirm`) rather than a component-local `useState`, so `cancel`'s existing reset discards both in one move. `runConfirm`/`PairingBridge.confirmPairing` widened by one optional argument, normalised once at the confirm boundary (trim; empty/whitespace-only → `undefined`, never `''`) by a module-private `hostLabelToSend`. The field mirrors the code field's M3 treatment and secret-hygiene posture (`autoComplete="off"`, `spellCheck={false}`, no `name`/`id`/`<form>`) even though the label itself isn't secret — the risk is a second input beside a bearer-token field being read as a credential form. Bounds against the same imported `MAX_HOST_LABEL_LENGTH` as the IPC guard and the read path; a mis-paste of the pairing payload into this field is an accepted residual risk (truncated, only sent alongside a successful confirm, sink is encrypted-at-rest and log-free). Architect self-review PASS. The Figma frame (`103-2901`) draws no such field — the treatment is provisional pending an updated frame.
- [Hello exchange](features/hello-exchange.md) — the application-handshake envelope layer in `src/main/transport/helloExchange.ts`: two pure functions on top of the [wire codec](features/wire-codec.md) that fill in the opaque `hello`/`helloAck` bytes the [Noise session](features/noise-session.md) and [relay driver](features/noise-relay-driver.md) carry but defer. `buildClientHello` wraps a defaults-injected `HelloClientPayload` (via `makeHelloClientPayload`, never hand-rolled) in a `hello` envelope → UTF-8 early-data bytes, with `id`/`ts` as inputs so it's deterministic; `parseHelloAck` narrows daemon `hello_ack` bytes into a validated, typed `HelloAckPayload`, failing closed via the codec's `WireDecodeError` (never a partial value). `capabilities` is an optional build input (never hardcoded `interactive`) and optional-with-default-`[]` on parse (the daemon marshals it `omitempty`) — treating it as required would silently break a real ack. Category-only errors, zero `console.*`; main-process only, no production caller on merge (the driver wiring is #62) (#10).
- [Daemon connection](features/daemon-connection.md) — the background-process transport consumer in `src/main/daemonConnection.ts` that the [Noise relay driver](features/noise-relay-driver.md) was built for: the first end-to-end path. `createDaemonConnection({deviceKeypair, pairedServer, sink, deviceName, clientVersion})` sources the device static key (#43 `ensure()`), the paired-server record (#44 `load()`), and the `hello` early-data (#10 `buildClientHello`), constructs and drives the driver, and maps its four lifecycle events onto the typed [daemon-event channel](features/daemon-event-channel.md) through one `onDriverEvent` choke point: `handshake-complete` → `connected{ack}` (via `parseHelloAck`), `terminal`/`error` → `failed{static code}`, plus a synchronous `connecting` at connect start. Every failure (no record / malformed record / bad-or-wrong-length server key / rejected keychain / malformed ack) fails closed to a non-connected event — never a crash; the clean `stop()` path emits nothing, and a single `stopped` flag both closes the start/stop race and suppresses the clean-stop terminal. Composition/wiring layer *above* transport (imports the driver **and** the IPC layer, so it lives at top-level `src/main/`, not IPC-free `transport/`); log-free, classify-don't-forward, no secret crosses to the renderer. Wired at the composition root (reuse paired-server store, add device-keypair store, start on `did-finish-load`, stop on `will-quit`) (#62). Now also exposes `reconnect()` — a generation-fenced re-arm of the once-only lifecycle that tears down any live driver and dials fresh, re-sourcing the paired-server record at dial time; the [pairing handler](features/pairing-ipc-channel.md)'s new `onPaired` trigger wires a confirm-success to it so a mid-session pairing connects with no restart (connect-on-pair, mirrors mobile #489; #82). Also constructs the per-dial `loadDialConfig` provider (the extracted record-load + derive) and injects it into the driver, so the supervisor's own *automatic* transient-drop reconnect re-sources the record too — for both the connection headers and the Noise session material — with no stale pre-pairing view surviving in memory (reload-per-dial, mirrors mobile #489; #83). Now also **content-free-log by construction** (was log-free): the injected [`DiagnosticLog`](features/diagnostic-log.md) shadows the daemon-composition lifecycle at three pre-existing seams — `daemon-dial` (the coordinate-free dial anchor, present even in the not-paired case where #127's relay leg is silent) / `daemon-connected` (the Noise handshake finishing, never the ack bytes) / `daemon-failed{code}` (every `emitFailed` classification) — logging only the static classification code and event name, never the caught error, the banner text, or the numeric close code (that is #127's relay leg). The daemon leg logs the *classification*; #127's relay leg logs the connection coordinates + socket close code (#128). Now also routes the same singleton logger a **second** downward path — one line, `diagnosticLog: deps.diagnosticLog`, into `createDriver({...})` — so the driver's pre-decryption framing catch and every session it builds can log through it too (#133). Now also exposes `requestSnapshot(payload)` — the `send` **twin** (inert no-op when not connected; a snapshot has no consumer to fail, unlike `requestDebugBundle`) that encrypts a payload-carrying `request_snapshot` onto the live session, and a new `case 'snapshot'` in the `case 'message'` consumer arm that emits a **content-minimised** `snapshotReceived{model,effort,yolo}` — the reply's `text`/`ts`/`conversation_id` are decoded but dropped right here (#180, see below). That emit site now also carries `used_tokens`/`window_tokens` (context-window usage figures) beside the three settings fields, still dropping `text`/`ts`/`conversation_id` (#191). Gained a `case 'turn-state'` consumer arm emitting `turnState{state}` (`conversation_id` dropped, `state` carried — a closed 3-value enum, nothing left to minimise); no new outbound method, `turn_state` is inbound-only (#214). Gained a `case 'stall'` consumer arm emitting, at ship time, a **nullary** `stallDetected` (`conversation_id`, the payload's only field, dropped — nothing left to carry); not `assertNever`-guarded in this inner switch, so the round-trip test is the guard, not the compiler; no new outbound method, `stall` is inbound-only and onset-only (no clearing frame) (#315, security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/315.md)). Gained a `case 'tool-use'` consumer arm emitting `toolUse{turnId,toolUseId,name,inputSummary}` (`conversation_id` dropped; `name`/`inputSummary` are opaque daemon display text carried onward for the render slice #218); no new outbound method, `tool_use` is inbound-only (#217). Widened with a sixth, optional field `input`, carried by reference from the already-narrowed fresh map, unconditional (`undefined` when the wire omitted it); ships dormant (#642, see [codebase notes](codebase/642.md)). Gained `case 'modal-shown'`/`case 'modal-dismissed'` consumer arms emitting `modalShown{modalId,class,title,prompt,options,defaultOptionId}` / `modalDismissed{modalId,outcome,source}` — the first pair in this stream to drop **no** `conversation_id` (a modal carries none); `options` reused verbatim (already stripped to `{id,label}` by `parseModalOption`); consumed by neither existing bridge, real consumer is the third, independent [modal store + bridge](features/modal-store-bridge.md), shipped in #223; no new outbound method, both kinds inbound-only (#201).
