Gained a `case 'tool-result'` consumer arm emitting `toolResult{turnId,toolUseId,isError,resultSummary}` (at ship time `conversation_id` dropped; `isError` a boolean, `resultSummary` opaque daemon display text carried onward for the render slice #230) — [#766](codebase/766.md) later widened it to carry `conversationId` too, copied by name, required, read bare because the decode already guarantees it — the vertical's last transport slice, feeding the timeline bridge's `fillResult` correlation rather than appending a new item; no new outbound method, `tool_result` is inbound-only (#229). Gained two new outbound methods, `answerModal(payload)`/`cancelModal(payload)` — the `send`/`requestSnapshot` inert-no-op-when-disconnected twins, resolving an outstanding modal rather than reacting to an inbound event. `answerModal` mints a fresh client-side idempotency `answer_token` via a new `mintToken` DI seam (default `crypto.randomUUID`, resolved alongside `now`/`createDriver`) and builds the wire `ModalAnswerPayload` as a **fresh object literal** (never a spread of the caller's payload — the deterministic net against a renderer-smuggled token) before calling [#235](codebase/235.md)'s `buildModalAnswer`; `cancelModal` does the same into `buildModalCancel` with no token. Both share the existing `nextEnvelopeId` counter, routed from the two new [command channel](features/command-channel.md) members via `main/index.ts`'s `onCommand` switch; `security-sensitive`, security review PASS (#236, see [modal resolution envelope](features/modal-resolution-envelope.md)). Now also exposes `createConversation(payload)` — another `send` twin, the write-side counterpart of `requestConversations`, that builds a **fresh literal** naming exactly the three modeled fields (never a spread of the caller's payload) before encrypting a `create_conversation` envelope onto the live session; a new `case 'conversation-created'` consumer arm emits `conversationCreated{conversation}` as a verbatim passthrough — nothing to drop, like `conversations`. Its caller is the render sibling #242, blocked on this ticket (#241, see [conversation create](features/conversation-create.md)). Gained a `case 'session-transition'` consumer arm emitting `sessionTransition{newSessionId}` — a fresh literal carrying only the addressing id, `previous_session_id`/`reason`/`occurred_at`/`workspace_cwd` decoded and validated but dropped here (the #180 content-drop model's second application); no new outbound method, `session_transition` is inbound-only (#254, see [codebase notes](codebase/254.md)). Now also exposes `setSessionSettings(payload)` — a faithful `requestSnapshot` twin whose method body is unchanged from the `send`-twin shape; unlike `createConversation`, the fresh anti-smuggling literal moves into the **builder** (`setSessionSettingsEnvelope.ts`) alongside the omitempty presence contract itself (absent field = leave unchanged, present zero value `''`/`false` = set), since the golden test targets the builder and the two concerns are one operation. No empty-`session_id` guard, mirroring `requestSnapshot`'s empty-`conversation_id` posture (Evidence-Based Fix Selection). Ships dormant — no `case 'session_settings_updated'` consumer arm yet (correlation is #261; the render caller is #257) (#263, see [session settings send](features/session-settings-send.md)). Gained a `case 'session-settings-updated'` consumer arm emitting `sessionSettingsUpdated{sessionId}` — a fresh literal carrying the reply's one field verbatim, nothing decoded to drop (unlike `session-transition`); no new outbound method, `session_settings_updated` is inbound-only, the confirmation `set_session_settings` (#263) landed (#264, see [codebase notes](codebase/264.md)). Gained a module-local `outstandingAnswers: string[]` FIFO correlation window, closed over beside `nextEnvelopeId`/`reassembler`: `answerModal` pushes the answered `modal_id` after a successful send, `case 'daemon-error':` shifts the oldest id and emits a new `modalAnswerRejected{modalId}` (`reassembler?.fail('daemon-error')` stays first, unchanged — independent consumers, documented double-fire on the rare bundle+answer overlap), `case 'modal-dismissed':` drains the matching id (an accepted answer can't later mis-attribute an unrelated `error`), and `dial()` resets the queue alongside `nextEnvelopeId = 2`. Exists because the daemon `error` carries no `modal_id` (ADR 0009) — attribution is pure main-side send-order memory. No new outbound method; rides the existing `answerModal`/`case 'message'` seams (#248, security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/248.md)). `setSessionSettings` widened to `(payload, changeId)`: captures `envelopeId = nextEnvelopeId` before the build and, only after a successful send, records `pendingSettings.set(envelopeId, changeId)` in a second module-local `Map<number, string>` sited beside `outstandingAnswers` (same single-writer, push-after-send discipline). `case 'session-settings-updated'` is now correlation-gated and fail-closed, not unconditional: an absent `inbound.inReplyTo` or a `pendingSettings` miss short-circuits with no event (AC3 — covers both a stale reply and a hostile daemon forging a confirmation for an unsent id); a hit deletes the entry and emits a fresh literal carrying `sessionId` + the map's `changeId`, never the wire `in_reply_to` itself. `dial()` also clears `pendingSettings` (AC5), making `nextEnvelopeId`'s restart-at-2 recycling safe. Orphaned entries from a **rejected** change persist until `dial()` — cleanup is #269's job, not this ticket's (#261, security-sensitive, PASS, see [session settings send](features/session-settings-send.md), [codebase notes](codebase/261.md)). `case 'daemon-error':` is now itself a precedence gate (#269): it correlates `inbound.inReplyTo` against `pendingSettings` **first**, ahead of both `reassembler?.fail` and the `outstandingAnswers` FIFO shift above; a match `delete`s the entry (closing the orphan the previous sentence describes) and emits `sessionSettingsRejected{changeId}`, then returns — consuming the frame entirely and skipping both existing consumers, since an error correlated by a unique per-request envelope id can be neither a bundle error nor a modal-answer rejection; a miss falls through to those two exactly as before (#269, security-sensitive, PASS, see [session settings send](features/session-settings-send.md), [codebase notes](codebase/269.md)). Now also exposes `promoteConversation(payload)` — another `send` twin, faithfully mirroring `createConversation`'s fresh-literal shape (`{ conversation_id, name, cwd }`, never a spread of the caller's payload); a new `case 'conversation-updated'` consumer arm emits `conversationUpdated{conversation}` as a verbatim passthrough, **unconditionally** — the reply is an unsolicited daemon **broadcast**, not correlated by `in_reply_to`, so unlike the `pendingSettings`-gated session-settings paths above there is no correlation memory to check or maintain. Its caller is the Save-as-channel dialog (#274), blocked on this ticket (#273, see [conversation promote](features/conversation-promote.md)). Now also exposes `unarchiveConversation(payload)` — the simplest `send` twin yet, a single-field fresh literal (`{ conversation_id }`); its `case 'conversation-updated'` reply is the same unconditional broadcast emit `promoteConversation` already wired, so no new inbound arm was needed. Ships dormant, first caller is #348's Archive-screen restore row (#346, see [conversation unarchive](features/conversation-unarchive.md)). Now also exposes `renameConversation(payload)` — a `send` twin one field richer than `unarchiveConversation`'s, building a fresh literal naming exactly `{ conversation_id, name }` (never a spread of the caller's payload); fire-and-forget, no reply correlated or awaited here — the daemon's confirming `conversation_updated` (correlated daemon-side, though desktop doesn't use that) flows through the same existing unconditional decode + re-list path `promoteConversation`/`unarchiveConversation` already feed, so no new inbound arm was needed here either. Ships dormant, intended first caller is the not-yet-built Rename dialog #360 (#359, security-sensitive, PASS, see [conversation rename](features/conversation-rename.md)). Now also exposes `archiveConversation(payload)` — the mirror-image twin of `unarchiveConversation`, an equally simple single-field fresh literal (`{ conversation_id }`); its `case 'conversation-updated'` reply is the same unconditional broadcast emit the sibling verbs already wired, so no new inbound arm was needed. Ships dormant, intended first caller is the not-yet-built Channel Info sheet's Archive action #366 (#363, security-sensitive, PASS, see [conversation archive](features/conversation-archive.md)). Gained two `case`s mapping the driver's new relay-leg events to `relayLinkChanged{status}` — `relay-link-up` → `'connected'`; `relay-link-down{code}` → classify at this module's single choke point (`code === 4404` → `'daemon-absent'`, else `'offline'`), dropping the relay-controlled raw code so only the category crosses IPC (the untrusted→trusted boundary). Ships dormant; real consumer is the not-yet-built relay-link store #329 (#328, security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/328.md)). Gained a `case 'conversation-deleted'` consumer arm emitting `conversationDeleted{id}` — unlike `conversation-updated`'s unconditional broadcast emit, this reply **is** correlated (matched by `in_reply_to`), but the emit still fires unconditionally on decode since the `id` is self-sufficient and no correlation memory (no `pendingSettings`/`outstandingAnswers`-style map) is threaded; not `assertNever`-guarded in this inner switch (the `stall` arm's precedent), so the round-trip test is the guard, not the compiler. No new outbound method, `conversation_deleted` is inbound-only. Ships dormant; the list-reflect consumer, #376, has since shipped in [conversation list store](features/conversation-list-store.md), not in this module (#375, security-sensitive, PASS, see [conversation delete](features/conversation-delete.md), [codebase notes](codebase/375.md)). Gained `case 'api-retry'` / `case 'compacting'` consumer arms — `apiRetry{active,current,total}` (#492) and its **banner-only** peer `compacting{active}` (#495), both fresh named-field literals dropping `conversation_id`, neither onset-only nor deduped; not `assertNever`-guarded in this inner switch, so the round-trip test is the guard for both. Both security-sensitive, PASS (see [codebase notes](codebase/492.md) / [codebase notes](codebase/495.md)); ship dormant, render lands in #493 (shipped) / #496 (shipped, see [codebase notes](codebase/496.md)). `reconnect()` gained a third caller: the [unpair channel](features/unpair-channel.md)'s new `onUnpaired` trigger, torn down with **zero changes to this file** — the existing `generation` fence (dropping the superseded driver's in-flight session-scoped events) and driver-stop (closing the socket) already covered it, and `bootstrap`'s null-record branch already returns before `createDriver` (#504, security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/504.md)). That third `reconnect()` caller exposed a gap `dial()` itself had all along: its `gen = ++generation` bump fences the superseded driver's `terminal` out of `onDriverEvent` before the bundle reassembler's fail ever fires, so `dial()` now calls the same shared `failBundleStream()` the `relay-link-down` arm gained too — both release-then-fail an armed bundle slot as a fourth per-connection reset, closing a wedge either a re-dial or a retryable relay drop could leave behind (#505, security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/505.md)). Gained case 'background-task-started' / case 'background-task-updated' / case 'background-task-roster' consumer emits — three sibling frames reporting claude work that outlives the turn that spawned it (pyrycode#1240); each is a fresh named-field literal copied by name (never a spread) and each, unlike every turn-stream arm above, KEEPS conversation_id — the frames carry no turn_id and open/close no turn, so they follow the queueState (#720) daemon-state rule. The roster arm (#566) is the aggregate peer of the other two: it passes its already-narrowed row array through by reference, snake_case, with no join and no snapshot diff against a held previous roster. None is assertNever-guarded in this inner switch; the round-trip test is the guard for all three. Shipped dormant across all three; the background-task-roster store ([#573](codebase/573.md), shipped) is the first consumer, but only of the `background-task-roster` kind — the two scalar kinds stay dormant, awaiting #574 (#564/#565/#566, all security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/564.md) / [codebase notes](codebase/565.md) / [codebase notes](codebase/566.md)). Gained a `case 'model-announced':` consumer emit — a fresh two-field literal (`model`, `truncated`) copied by name, `conversation_id` dropped (the `turnState`/`stallDetected`/`apiRetry`/`compacting` identity-report convention, not the background-task keep-it one); not `assertNever`-guarded, the round-trip test is the guard; deliberately stateless, so a verbatim repeat still emits (#587, security-sensitive, PASS, zero findings, see [codebase notes](codebase/587.md)). **`requestSnapshot(payload)` — the `send` twin described above — was removed in [#620](codebase/620.md)**, along with its entry in the returned `DaemonConnection` object literal; the interface no longer declares it. The `case 'snapshot'` inbound consumer arm (`snapshotReceived`/`screenSnapshotReceived`) was removed in turn by [#621](codebase/621.md), and the decode it consumed (`parseScreenSnapshotPayload`, the `snapshot` kind) by [#622](codebase/622.md) — a `screen_snapshot` frame now falls to `parseInboundMessage`'s tolerant `default` arm.
- [E2E test harness](features/e2e-harness.md) — the Playwright `_electron` harness that launches the **built** app from `out/main/index.js` and drives its renderer window, so later UI scenarios (pairing, send, stream) can assert against the real assembled app. Originally a scenario-agnostic launch/teardown fixture (`e2e/fixtures/electronApp.ts`, `test.extend` → `electronApp` + `page`); never gained an importer (every scenario isolates `--user-data-dir`, which it didn't), so [#546](codebase/546.md) deleted it — a new scenario now drives `launchPairedApp`, `realDaemon.ts`, or its own local fixture, plus one smoke assertion (its own local isolated-launch fixture, `.pairing` visible on a genuinely unpaired boot — #105). `args: ['.']` resolves the built entrypoint via `package.json` `main`; every fixture strips a stray `ELECTRON_RENDERER_URL` so the child always loads the built renderer. Teardown riding the runner's post-`use()` fixture lifecycle (fires on pass *and* fail) covers a *test* failure but not a *setup* one — a throw between resource creation and `use()` returning left that cleanup code unreachable in three fixtures (`realDaemon.ts`'s `page`/`relay`, `smoke.spec.ts`'s local `page`), leaking the Electron process and its credential-bearing `--user-data-dir` for the rest of the `workers: 1` run; [#517](codebase/517.md) converted all three to nested `try`/`finally` (one level per resource, close-then-`rm` falling out of the nesting for free) and lifted the `page` body into an exported `withIsolatedElectronApp(run)` so `e2e/fixture-teardown-leak.spec.ts` can drive the real converted path and prove it. `launchPairedApp.ts` was already immune and stayed untouched: every resource it creates lives inside the `use()` callback it hands to the test, so a mid-drive failure is a test failure with `use()` still returning. `npm run e2e` = `build && playwright test`; two-way separate from the vitest unit run (`testDir: './e2e'` ↔ vitest `include: ['src/**']`). Scaffolding at #40; the first UI scenario (pair→fingerprint→confirm→conversation→Send-enabled, against the in-process fake relay + fake daemon) landed as #93 (`e2e/pair-to-conversation.spec.ts`), forking a **local** `test.extend` for its per-run env + isolated `--user-data-dir` while the shared fixture stays scenario-agnostic; the send/stream half landed as #93's twin #94 (`e2e/send-and-stream.spec.ts`, a reply-driving `buildReply` over the same harness), closing the automated side of the Phase-1 milestone. #105 later forked the same isolated-launch shape (stripped to the minimum) for smoke itself, fixing its stale `.conversation`-at-boot assertion. Both UI scenarios went red on `main` after #140 moved the paired route's entry point to the ChannelList (`route='list'`), invisible because `npm run e2e` isn't CI-gated; #435 repaired both by seeding a one-row `conversations` reply (via the fake daemon's `buildReply`/`buildReplyFrames`) so ChannelList has a clickable row, driving that real `.channel-list__row-open` click before the `.conversation`/Send assertions (the row's auto-wait doubles as a connected gate), and — for #94 — also fixing a second, independent staleness where post-#140 renames (#179/#245/#203) had moved bubble rendering from `data-message-role` to the timeline's `data-thread-role` and retired the coarse `message` reply for `[assistant_delta, turn_end]`. #433 then extracted that repaired drive plus the launch/env/user-data-dir plumbing both specs still duplicated into one shared factory fixture, `e2e/fixtures/launchPairedApp.ts` — a Playwright factory fixture (yields a function, not a value) since consumers pass differing daemon-reply options; teardown is a per-resource LIFO stack pushed as each resource comes up, drained best-effort in the fixture epilogue so a mid-drive failure orphans nothing; `LaunchPairedAppOptions = Omit<FakeDaemonOptions,'url'>` passes `buildReply`/`buildReplyFrames`/`helloAck` straight through, and the fixture's exported `seedConversationsFrame()` lets a spec that overrides the default reply (`send-and-stream.spec.ts`, via `buildReplyFrames`, which takes precedence) reuse the same row instead of re-declaring it. `pair-to-conversation.spec.ts` went thin; this fixture — not `electronApp.ts`, and not a fresh per-spec fork — is the intended home for the #422–#429/#434/#420 e2e batch. #420 then extracted the real-stack sibling, `e2e/fixtures/realDaemon.ts`, from `e2e/real-claude.spec.ts` — the same `base.extend` chain-move + LIFO-teardown shape, but for the `relay → daemon → page` real-daemon spawn recipe rather than the fake-daemon pairing drive (which stayed spec-local, per AC1's scope); both configs' `real-*` partition widened together (`testMatch`/`testIgnore` from `/real-claude\.spec\.ts$/` to `/real-.*\.spec\.ts$/`) so a future real-* spec can't leak into the daemon-less default `npm run e2e`; `1 skipped` on `npm run e2e:real-claude` is the load-time proof the moved import graph resolved. #434 then added the fake-daemon-side stateful sibling, `e2e/fixtures/conversationStateFake.ts` — a `conversationStateFake(options)` factory returning a `buildReplyFrames` closure that HOLDS a seeded `ConversationSummary[]` and mutates it across a real UI drive (answering `list_conversations` from current state, applying all seven list-mutation verbs, and reflecting through the app's two real paths — a `conversation_updated` broadcast that triggers re-listing, and a correlated `conversation_deleted{id}` echoing `in_reply_to`), unlike the stateless per-call `buildReplyFrames` `send-and-stream.spec.ts` uses; its demonstrating spec clones `real-daemon-rename.spec.ts`'s drive against the fake, choosing rename over archive since `partitionByPromotion` doesn't filter `is_archived` (the #440 trap); this is the fixture the #422–#429 per-flow family (blocked-by #434) rides for list state instead of re-transcribing the wire per spec. [#451](codebase/451.md), split from #422, is the first of that family to ship: `e2e/conversation-create-rename.spec.ts` covers FAB create-nav (asserted via navigation, not list membership — `shouldRefreshList` is false for `conversationCreated`), the Channel-info **sheet** rename entry point (`.channel-info__action` is non-unique, targeted by accessible name), and the resulting two-row re-listed Channel List; also flags that the Channel-info sheet's full-surface scrim blocks `.conversation__back` until `.status-sheet__close` is clicked first. [#452](codebase/452.md), #451's independent sibling (also split from #422), is the fake-stack twin of the real-daemon lifecycle spec [#440](codebase/440.md): `e2e/conversation-archive-lifecycle.spec.ts` drives one FAB-created conversation through archive → restore → delete on the same single launch, asserting the Archive view's `role=tab` count deltas (`Discussions` 0→1→0) rather than active-Channel-List departure/return (Gaps A/B make that unrealizable — an archived row never actually leaves the active list), with delete asserted as gone from both surfaces since it splices the row rather than tagging it; also nuances the #451 scrim lesson — `onArchive`/`onDeleteConfirm` both self-close the sheet, so no `.status-sheet__close` step is needed or possible there. [#423](codebase/423.md), the third #422-family sibling, covers the only client flow with two location branches — `e2e/save-as-channel-promote.spec.ts` drives the Save-as-channel dialog's scratch (`promote_conversation` in place) and dedicated (`create_workspace_folder` → `workspace_folder_created{path}` → `promote_conversation` with the *returned* path, never the templated preview) branches, in **two** isolated `test()` blocks since promotion is one-way (strips `.channel-list__save`) and `launchPairedApp`'s strict single-row-open click forbids a two-seed launch; a spec-local `promoteFake` wrapper composes over `conversationStateFake` to answer `create_workspace_folder` (its default arm returns `[]`, a realizability gap only this spec needs closed), and the same Recent→Channels section-header trio used for section-membership also doubles as end-to-end proof that reply landed. [#456](codebase/456.md), split from #424, covers the Workspace Picker sheet's (#383) two round-trips — `e2e/workspace-picker.spec.ts` adds a `recent_workspaces` answer to the shared `conversationStateFake` (reused by split sibling #457, blocked-by this ticket) plus a spec-local `create_workspace_folder` answer (single consumer, the #423 precedent), in the same two-isolated-`test()`-block shape as #423: recent-pick asserts `change_workspace{conversation_id,cwd}` carrying the chosen row's path, and create-folder asserts the chained `change_workspace` carries the daemon-*returned* path verbatim (never a client preview, the #288 proof) after `create_workspace_folder{parent,name}` → `workspace_folder_created{path}`. Both blocks pin the [#448](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-desktop/pull/450) precondition (a `launchPairedApp` row-open now sets the active conversation, satisfying the `WorkspaceChip` gate on an `is_promoted:false` seed with no message sent) by asserting the "Change workspace" button is enabled before driving the picker; since the changed cwd has no DOM reflection (`change_workspace`'s reply is a no-op for `activeConversationStore`, the #440 unrealizable-active-list trap again), both flows assert the captured **outbound wire frame** via `expect.poll`, not a rendered reflection. [#425](codebase/425.md), split from #421, covers the run-config sheet's (#257) model/effort/YOLO `set_session_settings` write family in **one** `test()` block on one launch (nothing here is one-way like #423, and the session persists across all three controls): its own spec-local capturing factory confirms `conversationStateFake` doesn't answer `set_session_settings`/`request_snapshot`/`session_transition` (all fall to its `default`), and the drive needs two preconditions `launchPairedApp` doesn't provide — a pushed `session_transition` to feed `sessionIdStore` (the operability gate; absent it, controls render #188's inert markup) and a seeded `screen_snapshot` reply to `RunConfigData`'s one `request_snapshot` (the baseline a reject reverts toward). Since the view has no pending/disabled DOM state (`selectEffectiveSettings` composes `pending` and `confirmed` to the same value), each change's send is proven from the captured outbound `set_session_settings` frame (`isDeepStrictEqual` + `expect.poll(...).toBe(1)`, proving send-once and only-the-changed-field together), while the one scripted rejection (a correlated `error`) is the visually distinct outcome asserted in the DOM (control reverts + `.run-config__error[role="alert"]`). [#457](codebase/457.md), split from #424 (#456's twin, blocked-by #456), covers the Settings "Default workspace" preference reaching the FAB's `create_conversation` — `e2e/default-workspace.spec.ts` drives `DefaultWorkspaceRow` (#404) → `WorkspacePickerSheet` (#383) → `defaultWorkspaceStore` (#403) → the channel-list FAB (#242) in **one** `test()` block on one launch, reusing #456's shared `recentWorkspaces` fake answer through a capturing wrapper with **zero** spec-local verb handling (strictly simpler than #456's own, since every verb this drive sends is already answered by the shared fake); the Settings choose writes the store with no wire traffic at all (unlike #456's thread picker), so the load-bearing assertion is the FAB's own `create_conversation{is_promoted:false,name:null,cwd:CHOSEN}`, `expect.poll`ed on the spec-captured inbound frames (the cwd has no DOM reflection, the #440/#456 trap again); corrects #456's seed-promotion constraint as irrelevant here (the Settings picker has no active-conversation gate); and establishes that a negative wire guard is race-free when asserted *after* a positive poll on the same in-order Noise channel. [#426](codebase/426.md) covers the permission/trust modal's (`PermissionModal`) five answer paths — default one-tap, non-default confirm sub-step (Back vs Confirm), cancel, a rejected answer's dismissible banner, and a remote `modal_dismissed` clear — surfaced via `daemon.pushFrame(modal_shown)` after launch (the #425 push technique) since the modal is not interactive-timeline-gated; split into **two** `test()` blocks not by convenience but because the modal reject correlates by **FIFO send order, not `in_reply_to`** (a bare `error` dequeues the oldest `outstandingAnswers` entry), so block 1 (answer/confirm/cancel/dismiss) never triggers a reject and block 2 (reject) starts from a fresh launch's empty queue; `answer_token` (main-minted via `crypto.randomUUID`) is asserted present-but-opaque rather than deep-equalled. [#427](codebase/427.md), cloning #425's capture-and-push skeleton, covers three server-push-driven flows in **one** `test()` block on one launch — the queued-backlog render (#294), dropping a queued message (`dequeue_message`, #296/#300), and interrupting a running turn (#215/#307) — explicitly generalizing *why* a flow does NOT need #423/#426's two-block split (neither the queue store nor the timeline `phase` accumulates one-way residue within a launch); establishes that a pushed `queue_state` must carry `conversation_id === SEEDED_ROW.id` or the backlog renders zero rows silently (`selectBacklogFor(activeConversation.id)`), that it must be pushed only after `launchPairedApp` resolves (past the `connected`-triggered backlog reset, #197), and that the interrupt/thinking UI quiesces on `turn_state{idle}` — not `turn_end`, which appends a turn boundary without resetting `phase`; both mutations are non-optimistic, so each gets a two-part assertion (capture the outbound frame, then push the reflecting state and assert the DOM change). [#428](codebase/428.md), the last of the #422-family reliability-affordance specs, covers the stall indicator, the "Show daemon screen" screen snapshot, and the debug-bundle download in **one** `test()` block on one launch — three distinct daemon-interaction shapes (a pure server push, a request→reply, a chunked reply stream); confirms stall is a `daemon.pushFrame`, never a bundled reply (the #426/#427 stale-premise correction, again); defuses a most-recent-wins overwrite trap where the Run-config sheet's own `request_snapshot` on mount would clobber the scripted snapshot text, by answering every `request_snapshot` identically and asserting the `<pre>` before that sheet opens (the first #422-family spec where `text` is actually surfaced, unlike #425's empty-string baseline); and proves `debugBundleProgress` climbs to its final chunk count from streamed `debug_bundle_chunk` frames alone, deliberately never sending `debug_bundle_done` since a completed save writes a real archive to `app.getPath('downloads')` with no dialog to stub and no downloads-directory isolation in the fixture. [#465](codebase/465.md), split from #429, covers the paired region's inner navigation — `e2e/paired-shell-navigation.spec.ts` drives every remaining `nextPairedRoute` transition (`PairedShell`) beyond the launcher's own `list → thread` click: the pair-another-server round-trip (#152) and the back-navigation chain `thread → list → settings → list → archive → list`, in **one** `test()` block on one launch (the #425/#427/#428 precedent extended from server-push flows to five pure client-nav transitions); establishes that teardown-vs-in-shell-pair-another is provable **only** via the Cancel→Settings round-trip, not an on-pairing-surface assertion — the `pairServer` route and the app-root pairing route both render the identical `PairingScreen`, so the DOM is byte-identical whether the session survived (the #440-realizability discipline applied to a same-component-two-routes case) — with the Cancel destination (Settings re-renders) as the one observable that separates them; also selects the three identically-named (`'Back'`) back buttons by screen-scoped class rather than role-name. [#466](codebase/466.md), split from #429, is the family's first **two-launch** scenario — `e2e/push-toggle-persist-relaunch.spec.ts` proves the push-notification preference (#408) survives a relaunch reusing the same `--user-data-dir`; adds `LaunchControl`'s `reuseUserDataDir` flag to `launchPairedApp` (one flag coupling dir-reuse with pairing-drive-skip, since a reused *paired* dir's drive would hang) plus an additive `PairedApp.userDataDir` return field — zero fan-out to the ~9 existing consumers; the reuse launch starts a vestigial daemon (kept non-optional to avoid a possibly-undefined ripple) and returns at the **list**, skipping the row-click and Send-enabled wait since `routeForStatus` reads the persisted pairing record at mount, independent of the Noise handshake; `app.close()` before the second launch is a hard barrier — it both releases the `SingletonLock` and flushes renderer `localStorage`, the mechanism the whole test relies on. [#464](codebase/464.md), split from #429 (sibling of #465/#466), covers the session-EXIT path — the flip from a paired, connected thread back to the app-root `PairingScreen`, previously uncovered: `e2e/unpair-repair.spec.ts`'s block A (one launch) drives the two-phase `UnpairControl` (Cancel keeps the thread mounted with Send enabled and the pairing field at count 0; re-opening and Confirm, same launch, flips to the app-root pairing screen), and block B (its own launch, since a fatal close is terminal) surfaces the `Re-pair` affordance (#167) via a new [fake relay forwarder](features/fake-relay-forwarder.md) hook, `closeClientLeg(4401)`, exposed through `launchPairedApp` for the first time. The app-root pairing field's visibility/count-0 doubles as the return/intact proof with no round-trip needed (unlike #465's Cancel→Settings), since both #464 exits flip the *top-level* App route and unmount `PairedShell` entirely — the Re-pair trigger's reachability was traced end-to-end through merged code before the infra was written, confirming the fatal-close hook as feasible rather than falling back to the ticket's "route back instead of asserting the unrealizable" escape hatch (the #440 discipline). [#664](codebase/664.md), split from #613 and blocking #665, swept every non-owning site (11, across the same five files #661/#662 had touched) off the `textarea[aria-label="Pairing code"]` selector and the `Paste pairing code` heading onto the bare `[aria-label="Pairing code"]` idiom, so #665's restyle (M3 filled `<input>`, no heading) can land without a silent-green cascade; zero production code, `getByLabel` deliberately not introduced (it would re-match twice once #665 wraps the field in a `<label>`).
- [Fake routing relay](features/fake-routing-relay.md) — the routing-aware counterpart to the [fake relay forwarder](features/fake-relay-forwarder.md): a test-only `ws` relay presenting the unchanged raw `/v1/client` leg to the app while speaking the daemon's JSON `RoutingEnvelope` protocol on `/v1/server`, so a **real** `pyry` daemon (not just the fake one) can be driven through the built UI. New `routingEnvelope.ts` (pure codec — `frame` spliced verbatim/opaque, fail-closed decode, `token` first-frame-only and never read back off the wire) + `fakeRoutingRelay.ts` (the relay, reusing the forwarder's lifecycle scaffolding; content-blind and log-free by import surface). Ships alone, no production consumer; consumer is #252 (#251, security-sensitive, PASS).
- [Session-id store](features/session-id-store.md) — the renderer's held copy of the open conversation's current daemon `session_id`, retained from the always-arriving `sessionTransition` marker (#254) so the interactive [Run configuration controls](codebase/257.md) can address a `set_session_settings` write to the session actually running. A dedicated `sessionIdStore` (`string | null`, `null` = no marker seen yet, last-write-wins whole-value setter) plus `sessionIdBridge.ts`'s pure `translateSessionTransition` filter and the App-level headless `SessionIdData` leaf — mounted unconditionally next to `ConversationListData`, **not** sheet-scoped like `RunConfigData`, since a marker can arrive before the Run config sheet is ever opened. Reactive-only: no request half, no correlation, holds an empty-string id verbatim rather than dropping it. Not security-sensitive; not UI-visible. #257 (see [codebase notes](codebase/257.md)) is the live reader: `session_id !== null` is the AC5 gate on the interactive controls (#259, see [codebase notes](codebase/259.md)). Gained a clear path, `clearSessionId` — returns the state to `initialSessionIdState`, a named setter beside `setSessionId` rather than a reducer conversion, sourced from the exported constant so a future second field stays covered; shipped with no production caller; [#530](codebase/530.md) (navigation, shipped) wired the first, gated on the active conversation's id actually changing, and [#531](codebase/531.md) (unpair / pair-another-server, shipped) wired the second, unconditionally, via `clearPairingScopedState` (#529, see [codebase notes](codebase/529.md)).
- [Real-claude liveness e2e](features/real-claude-liveness-e2e.md) — `e2e/real-claude.spec.ts`: drives the **built** app through the real pairing UI against a freshly-spawned **real** `pyry` daemon running real `claude --model haiku`, bridged through the fake routing relay above, asserting a non-empty `data-thread-role="assistant"` reply streams for two consecutive sends — the thin client-layer net over the daemon-side liveness fix `pyrycode#854`. Resolves the "desktop always sends `conversation_id: 'default'`" binding problem by seeding the daemon's registry (`sessions.json`/`conversations.json`, opaque-string conv id) before spawn. Skips cleanly (never fails) when `claude`/`pyry`/an Anthropic credential is missing; gated out of `npm run e2e` via a filename `testIgnore` and run instead via `npm run e2e:real-claude`, part of the operator's pre-ship gate (no CI by org policy). Zero production `src/` change. Depends on #251 and the #179 interactive-flip (a v2 daemon's structured reply only reaches an interactive client). RED/GREEN (pre-/post-#854 daemon) is a manual PR observation, not automated (#252, security-sensitive, PASS). Its `relay → daemon → page` fixture chain was extracted verbatim into the shared `e2e/fixtures/realDaemon.ts` — the pairing drive stayed spec-local (#420, NOT-sec, PASS with 2 non-blocking NITs). [#445](codebase/445.md), split from #431 (twin #446, shipped next), is the first **turn-coupled** sibling on this claude-spawning tier: `e2e/real-claude-interrupt.spec.ts` clones this spec's precondition verbatim, starts a deliberately long haiku turn ("count to 300"), gates on the interrupt affordance + streaming cursor becoming visible, clicks interrupt, then asserts a two-signal quiesce (`interruptButton` count-0 from `turn_state{idle}` **and** `.bubble__cursor` count-0 from `turn_end`, captured before the settled-content baseline to absorb any trailing chunk) followed by a 3s stable-content re-read — the real-stack liveness net over the [interrupt envelope](features/interrupt-envelope.md)'s client wiring (#305–#307) that the fake-stack twins (#307, #427) can only prove against a scripted `daemon.pushFrame`. Zero production `src/` change; inherits `spawnClaude:true` and the full skip-gate for free. PASS, no findings. [#446](codebase/446.md), #445's twin, is `e2e/real-claude-queue-drop.spec.ts` — same precondition and long-prompt shape, body swapped for queue-while-busy-then-drop: a second send mid-turn 1 enqueues as a `data-thread-role="queued"` [queue-store](features/queue-store.md) row (`composerAvailability` gates on connection only, never turn phase), the drop removes it while turn 1 is still provably running (an `interruptButton`-visible check bracketing the drop, the DOM-only answer to the #442-class dequeue-before-drain hazard), and turn 1's natural drain — reusing #445's two-signal-quiesce-plus-settle idiom verbatim — proves the dropped send ran no assistant turn at all. Zero production `src/` change; both #431 children now shipped. PASS, no findings. [#432](codebase/432.md), the tier's third and deepest sibling, is `e2e/real-claude-permission-modal.spec.ts` — clones the same precondition, swaps the turn body for a prompt forcing exactly one permission-gated tool call, waits for `page.getByRole('dialog')` (the real daemon relaying claude's per-tool prompt as `modal_shown` — a timeout is a genuine liveness signal, not a flake), answers "allow" via a helper absorbing two daemon-supplied unknowns (the affirmative label, matched by a start-anchored regex; whether it's the default, resolved by a conditional `Confirm` click), then reuses #445/#446's two-signal quiesce to prove the turn completes — a structurally airtight causation proof, since `turn_end` cannot fire without the tool running, which cannot happen without the allow. Adds a single-consumer `skipPermissions` fixture option (default `true` = byte-for-byte current args) dropping `--dangerously-skip-permissions` for this spec alone, mirroring #439's `seedPromoted` precedent. Zero production `src/` change. PASS, no findings. **As of [#449](codebase/449.md) this spec is red on the live stack** for a reason unrelated to any of the above — the isolated-HOME harness daemon delivers the turn but no reply ever fans back to the app; it still SKIPs cleanly under the pipeline's `npm run e2e`, but `npm run e2e:real-claude` is not currently a working pre-ship gate — `scripts/live-drive.mjs` is the interim one (see [live e2e runbook](features/live-e2e-runbook.md)). #449 shipped a doc-only reconciliation (PR#476); the fixture-seed falsifier and the daemon-side handoff are operator-run live-stack diagnosis, not yet resolved. [#933](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-desktop/issues/933) added a fourth, opt-in skip gate to the shared `realDaemon.ts` fixture: a spec declares `requiredCapabilities` and the fixture reads the daemon's `hello_ack` via a harness-side Noise probe (`e2e/fixtures/daemonCapabilityGate.ts`, advertising exactly the declared set since the ack is an intersection, failing closed into a skip on any handshake error/malformed-ack/timeout, never a failure or a hang), skipping cleanly with a reason naming the missing capability and a rebuild command — turning a stale-daemon test failure (routed to a builder who can't rebuild a Go binary) into an environment-fault skip (routed to the operator). Declaring nothing dials no probe and leaves the nine pre-#933 specs byte-for-byte unaffected. `real-claude-question-answer.spec.ts` is the first consumer, declaring `question` against pyrycode#2020. NOT-sec review PASS, two SHOULD-FIX findings (missing-set computed as a filter over `required` not `capabilities`; `stop()` on every settle plus an absolute retry deadline) both addressed in the shipped code.
- [Real-daemon credential-light e2e](features/real-daemon-credential-light-e2e.md) — a real spawned `pyry` on the fake routing relay, gated on the `pyry` binary **alone** (no `claude`, no credential), for the registry-backed user actions (rename/archive/restore/delete/…) that never touch claude — the tier that would have caught `promote_conversation`'s missing-handler gap (pyrycode#949), which the always-answering fake suite can't. `realDaemon.ts` (#420) gains two additive Playwright option fixtures, `spawnClaude` (default `true`) and `seedPromoted` (default `false`) — `real-claude.spec.ts` sets neither and is byte-for-byte unaffected. Claude-less mode points `-pyry-claude` at a harness-owned no-op placeholder (`exit 0`, never invoked since rename triggers no `send_message`) rather than omitting the flag. Liveness proof is `e2e/real-daemon-rename.spec.ts`: seeds a promoted conversation, gates readiness on the `.channel-list__rename` pencil rendering (handshake + connected + `list_conversations` + promoted-echo, all in one assertion), renames it through the product UI, and asserts the new title renders while the old is gone — proven via the daemon's real `conversation_updated` broadcast, not a renderer-side echo. Ran green on a real daemon (#439, NOT-sec, PASS, no findings); unblocks the four sibling real-daemon-* action specs (#440–#443). [#440](codebase/440.md), the first sibling to ship, is `e2e/real-daemon-conversation-lifecycle.spec.ts` — the real-daemon twin of the fake-stack [#452](codebase/452.md), grafting its archive→restore→delete assertion surface onto this fixture (`spawnClaude:false, seedPromoted:true`); ran green against a live `pyry dev` build (2.8s) confirming all four registry handlers (create/archive/unarchive/delete) wired, no #949-class gap found; and — since the real-daemon seed is name-less (unlike #452's uniquely-named fake seed) and both it and the FAB-created row render "Untitled" — established scoping created-vs-seed rows by section affordance (`.channel-list__rename` vs `.channel-list__save`) rather than title text whenever a spec's seed and test-created row can collide on display string. PASS, one non-blocking NIT (5s default timeout on the create-nav assert, inconsistent with the tier's own 15s `ROUNDTRIP_TIMEOUT_MS` elsewhere). [#441](codebase/441.md), the second sibling to ship, is `e2e/real-daemon-workspace.spec.ts` — the real-daemon twin of the fake-stack [#456](codebase/456.md), driving the `WorkspacePickerSheet`'s recent-workspaces and create-folder round-trips onto this fixture with `seedPromoted:false` (a Recent discussion seed, generalizing #440's section-affordance readiness gate to `.channel-list__save`); ran green end-to-end against a live `pyry` (2.4s, not just skip-clean), confirming `recent_workspaces` and `create_workspace_folder` both wired, no #949-class gap; and established that a real-daemon close/unmount assertion should target a DOM element rendered unconditionally (`.workspace-picker__other`) rather than one whose presence depends on the same daemon-derived round-trip under test, since a row-count-0 check can pass vacuously. PASS, no findings. [#443](codebase/443.md), the tier's **marquee case**, is `e2e/real-daemon-promote.spec.ts` — the real-daemon twin of the fake-stack [#423](codebase/423.md), keeping only its scratch ("Keep in scratch") branch: `SaveAsChannelDialog` fires `promote_conversation` alone (no `create_workspace_folder` prelude) against the name-less `seedPromoted:false` seed, and the spec asserts the row moves "Recent discussions" → "Channels" only after the daemon's `conversation_updated` reply drives a re-list — the scratch arm never touches the list store, so this is a non-optimistic proxy that would time out on a pre-#949 binary (the exact regression this tier exists to catch). The dedicated branch is deliberately out of scope: its `create_workspace_folder` prelude is already real-wire-proven by #441, and #949's Option B daemon ignores the promote payload's `cwd`, leaving no real-wire DOM surface for the dedicated leg here. A fourth candidate, #442 (dequeue + set-session-settings), was demoted to Inbox unshipped in its original form; dequeue stays demoted, but set-session-settings was revisited under #481 and shipped as `e2e/real-daemon-session-settings.spec.ts`, refining rather than confirming #442's "DOM-invisible" verdict — #558's `aria-busy` on the control element means the reply gates the *settling*, even though the displayed value still flips optimistically, sharpening the tier's provability rule to "the reply must gate some DOM transition, value re-read after settle." #975/#976 later moved the Model rows and Effort segments onto the daemon's published `model_list` frame, unreachable claude-less, breaking that spec; #987 re-keyed it onto the YOLO switch, the one control the frame doesn't reach. PASS.
- [Inbound message decode](features/inbound-message-decode.md) — the untrusted→trusted boundary for a decrypted daemon message: `parseInboundMessage` in `src/main/transport/inboundMessage.ts` turns the relay driver's opaque `message{plaintext}` bytes into a narrowed `messageReceived` / `messagesReceived` `DaemonEvent`, or fails closed. Size guard (`MAX_PLAINTEXT_BYTES`) + `decodeEnvelope` + route by wire `type` + per-field payload narrowing (each of `conversation_id`/`message_id`/`text` a string, `role` exactly `user`/`assistant`, `messages` an array whose every element narrows — one bad element fails the whole chunk); a single `WireDecodeError` covers oversized/malformed/unparseable/mistyped so the consumer's one `catch` drops the frame. Sibling to `helloExchange.ts` / `sendMessageEnvelope.ts`; IPC-free (the [daemon connection](features/daemon-connection.md) `case 'message'` arm does the IPC map); category-only errors, no dedupe/reorder (that's the store, ADR 0004). Fills the last no-op arm #62 left; feeds the already-complete #18→#19→#2 renderer pipeline (render-binding is #69) (#68). Now **content-free-log by construction** (was log-free): the optional injected [`DiagnosticLog`](features/diagnostic-log.md) records each of the two non-throwing outcomes — `inbound-decoded` (a modeled `message`/`message_chunk`) and `inbound-unmodeled` (a well-formed-but-unmodeled type, previously *silently dropped*) — carrying the envelope type, the logger's `seq`, the plaintext byte length, and a one-way BLAKE2s-256 hash of the *frame* (never `envelope.payload`, so the digest is implicitly salted by the server-assigned id/ts/message_id), never the payload value. Log fires *after* the narrower so the throw path stays unlogged (pre-decryption raw bytes are sibling #133); the one peer-controlled string (`envelope.type` on the unmodeled arm) is capped `slice(0,64)`; adds one additive `hash?` field to `DiagnosticEvent` (length reuses `bytes?`) (#130). Extended a second time with a `screen_snapshot` → `snapshot` kind and a fail-closed `parseScreenSnapshotPayload` (a new `requireBoolean` sibling of `requireString`/`requireNumber` checks `yolo`'s *type*, so `false` decodes as a real value, never an absence); all six fields on the daemon's `ScreenSnapshotPayload` are always present (no `omitempty`) (#180, see below). Extended a third time with two more `requireNumber` fields, `used_tokens`/`window_tokens` (context-window usage, pyrycode/pyrycode#857) — eight fields total now, `0` decoded as a real value like the bundle `seq`/`total` (#191). Extended a fourth time with `assistant_delta` → `assistant-delta` and `turn_end` → `turn-end` kinds — `parseAssistantDeltaPayload`/`parseTurnEndPayload`, built on the existing helpers verbatim, no new helper needed. Unlike every prior extension, the consumer arm carries the decoded `text` onward (dropping only `conversation_id`) rather than minimising it — the assistant delta text is the render payload, not a secret; feeds the [thread timeline](features/thread-timeline.md) model once #202's bridge lands (#199). Extended a sixth time with a `turn_state` kind and `parseTurnStatePayload` — the closed-enum idiom's second instance in the file (cloned from `role`, not `requireString`), decoding the coarse turn-lifecycle scalar the same v2 stream carries; feeds the timeline bridge's `phase`, giving `selectPhase` its first real source (#214). Extended a seventh time with a `tool_use` kind and `parseToolUsePayload` — the required-string-presence idiom scaled to five fields with no enum, decoding the tool-call enrichment of the same v2 stream; feeds the timeline bridge's fourth owned arm, the first to append a durable `toolCall` item rather than driving text or a scalar (#217). `ToolUsePayload` widened with a sixth, optional field `input?: Record<string, string>` (pyrycode#1678), decoded via the new `optionalStringMap` narrower — the first narrower in the file where the daemon chooses the object keys, dropping the three prototype-reserved keys after the value-type check runs (#642, see [codebase notes](codebase/642.md)). Extended a tenth time with a `conversation_created` → `conversation-created` kind and `parseConversationCreatedPayload` — the write-side twin of `conversations` (#139), decoding the daemon's own 5-field `ConversationCreatedPayload` (not `ConversationSummary`) via four `requireString`s, one `requireBoolean` (`is_promoted`), and `requireStringOrNull`'s second use (`name`) (#241, see [conversation create](features/conversation-create.md)). Extended an eleventh time with a `session_transition` → `session-transition` kind and `parseSessionTransitionPayload` — the interactive session-boundary marker, decoding `SessionTransitionPayload` via three `requireString`s, `requireStringOrNull`'s third use (`workspace_cwd`), and the closed-enum idiom's third instance (`reason`, cloned from `parseTurnStatePayload`'s `state` check); the consumer arm drops four of the five decoded fields at the emit (#254, see [codebase notes](codebase/254.md)). Extended a twelfth time with a `session_settings_updated` → `session-settings-updated` kind and `parseSessionSettingsUpdatedPayload` — the `set_session_settings` (#263) confirmation reply, scaled to a single `requireString` (`session_id`), no enum, no nullable; unlike `session_transition` the consumer arm drops nothing at the emit, since the reply has only the one field (#264, see [session settings send](features/session-settings-send.md), [codebase notes](codebase/264.md)). The `session-settings-updated` kind widened with an optional `inReplyTo?: number` — the numeric `Envelope.in_reply_to` routing id `decodeEnvelope` already decoded but the consumer had never propagated; this ticket's decode case reads `envelope.in_reply_to` directly (no re-parse) so [daemon connection](features/daemon-connection.md)'s correlation lookup can fail closed on an absent value (#261, security-sensitive, PASS, see [session settings send](features/session-settings-send.md), [codebase notes](codebase/261.md)). The original, always-content-free `daemon-error` kind (#116) gained the same `inReplyTo?: number` carrier — propagated, never re-parsed, no `ErrorPayload` field ever narrowed — so [daemon connection](features/daemon-connection.md)'s `case 'daemon-error':` can correlate a rejection against the same `pendingSettings` map #261 built, ahead of that same case's pre-existing bundle-reassembler and #248 modal-FIFO consumers (#269, security-sensitive, PASS, see [session settings send](features/session-settings-send.md), [codebase notes](codebase/269.md)). [#179](codebase/179.md) flipped the `interactive` capability the `assistant_delta`/`turn_end`/`turn_state`/`tool_use`/`tool_result`/`modal_shown`/`modal_dismissed` kinds were all gated on — every one of them now decodes live daemon traffic in production, not just unit-test-driven bytes. Extended a thirteenth time with a `stall` kind and `parseStallPayload` — `parseTurnStatePayload` scaled to its single always-present field with no enum check, decoding the daemon's onset-only liveness signal on the same v2 stream; the consumer arm dropped the one decoded field (`conversation_id`) and emitted a nullary `stallDetected` until [#732](codebase/732.md) widened it to carry `conversationId` onward (copied by name) — the same routing-key widening #724 did for `turn_state`, retiring the arm's "nullary ⇒ nothing can ride it" security claim in favour of the daemon-asserted-routing-key argument (never rendered, never a filename/cache key/lookup path, reaches no log sink); the id still stops at the renderer timeline bridge, and `ThreadEvent.stallDetected` stays nullary. Sat on the same `interactive`-capability gate, live since #179. Ships dormant; the render slice #317 is the first consumer (#315, security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/315.md); #732, security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/732.md)). Extended a fourteenth time with an `api_retry` → `api-retry` kind and `parseApiRetryPayload` — `parseStallPayload` scaled from one field to four (`requireString`/`requireBoolean`/two `requireNumber`s, no new helper), the not-onset-only, not-deduped peer of `stall`; the consumer arm dropped only `conversation_id` (carrying `active`/`current`/`total` onward) until [#737](codebase/737.md) widened it to carry `conversationId` onward too (copied by name) — the same routing-key widening #724 gave `turn_state` and #732 gave `stall`, retiring this arm's own "no string field can ride it" security claim in favour of the daemon-asserted-routing-key argument; the id still stops at the renderer timeline bridge, and `ThreadEvent.apiRetry` keeps its four fields. Ships dormant; the render slice #493 is the first consumer (#492, security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/492.md); #737, security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/737.md)). Extended a fifteenth time with a `compacting` → `compacting` kind and `parseCompactingPayload` — `parseApiRetryPayload` minus its two `requireNumber` lines, **banner-only** (no counter on the wire at all); the consumer arm carries only `active` onward, dropping `conversation_id` — `stall`'s content-drop shape, not `api_retry`'s. Ships dormant; the render slice #496 is the first consumer (#495, security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/495.md)). Extended a sixteenth, seventeenth, and eighteenth time with background_task_started / background_task_updated / background_task_roster kinds — the three background-task-panel sibling frames (pyrycode#1240; lane confirmed as the v2 interactive stream, pyrycode#1394). The first two scale parseApiRetryPayload with a new requireStringArrayOrNull field narrower for truncated_fields (required-present, nullable array). The third, background_task_roster, is the aggregate peer reporting what is alive rather than what happened to one task: its tasks field takes parseQueueStatePayload's inline Array.isArray + raw.map shape rather than requireStringArrayOrNull, because within that one frame tasks:null must fail closed while a ROW's truncated_fields:null is a valid value — the daemon's only custom MarshalJSON normalises a nil Tasks to [] and deliberately does not normalise truncated_fields. Its row narrower, parseBackgroundTask, clones parseQueuedItem's posture rather than the scalar siblings' field list — the row is four fields, not the six/four the siblings carry (no tool_call_id, no patch). Each consumer emit KEEPS conversation_id (the queue_state #720 rule). All three shipped dormant; the background-task-roster store ([#573](codebase/573.md), shipped) is the first consumer, but only of the `background-task-roster` kind — the two scalar kinds stay dormant, awaiting #574 (#564/#565/#566, all security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/564.md) / [codebase notes](codebase/565.md) / [codebase notes](codebase/566.md)). Gained a nineteenth kind, `model_announced` → `model-announced` — claude's own identity report of the model it resolved for the turn (pyrycode#1616/#1638); `ModelAnnouncedPayload{conversation_id, model, truncated}` decodes via `parseUnrecognizedMessagePayload`'s shape minus `site`/`message_type`, no new helper; `model` held verbatim (no length/charset check, no allow-list), `truncated` required and never defaulted. Consumer emit DROPPED conversation_id at ship time, unlike the background-task family — an identity report, not daemon state keyed by conversation. Ships dormant; #588 is the first consumer (#587, security-sensitive, PASS, zero findings, see [codebase notes](codebase/587.md)). [#714](codebase/714.md) later carried it onward as `conversationId` — the last of the family (#724/#732/#737/#742 did the other four) — stopping at the announced-model bridge, so #588's store is unaffected (security-sensitive, PASS, one NIT, see [codebase notes](codebase/714.md)). **[#622](codebase/622.md) removes the second extension outright**: the `screen_snapshot` → `snapshot` kind, `parseScreenSnapshotPayload`, and the `screen_snapshot`/`ScreenSnapshotPayload` wire types are gone — the union is open (`EnvelopeType | string`), so a `screen_snapshot` frame now falls to the `default` arm, logged `inbound-unmodeled` (previously `inbound-decoded`) instead of throwing on malformed input; the malformed/well-formed distinction is deliberately erased beyond byte length and hash, pinned as an equality property between the two log records. The retained oversize test at the old `screen_snapshot fail-closed` describe is retitled: it's now the only oversize test in the file using an unmodeled type, so it alone proves `MAX_PLAINTEXT_BYTES` runs before the type switch. Two production files, deletions only. Security review PASS. **[#870](codebase/870.md)** mirrored the daemon's `ModalShownPayload.conversation_id` (pyrycode#1065) into `ModalShownPayload` and `parseModalShownPayload` — a required, fail-closed `requireString`, first in wire order — correcting three stale doc blocks the addition falsified (this file's `ModalShownPayload`/`InboundDaemonMessage` blocks and the emit-site comment in [daemon connection](features/daemon-connection.md)) without touching the still-true "no `conversation_id`" claims on the sibling `ModalDismissedPayload`/`ModalAnswerPayload`/`ModalCancelPayload`. The consumer emit deliberately drops the new field this slice (decode-only, no renderer-visible surface change); [#871](codebase/871.md) carries it across IPC. Security review PASS. **[#871](codebase/871.md)** delivered that: the `daemonConnection.ts` emit now copies `conversationId` onto the `modalShown` `DaemonEvent` by name, required never optional, read bare since the decode already guarantees it — the tenth arm in the `#675` routing-key family (after `turnState`/`stallDetected`/`apiRetry`/`compacting`/`assistantDelta`/`turnEnd`/`toolUse`/`toolResult`/`unrecognizedMessage`), and an outbound scoping key only — `modalId` stays the sole correlation key for answering. Ships dormant: the [modal store + bridge](features/modal-store-bridge.md) rebuilds a fresh `ModalEvent` and still drops it; [#872](codebase/872.md) is the consumer. `modal_dismissed` is unaffected. Security review PASS. **Split 2026-09-01** — the contract, internals and limits now live in their own documents, listed below; this entry stays the map.
    - [Inbound message decode — extension history](features/inbound-message-decode-history.md) — The chronological, kind-by-kind account of every additive extension to `InboundDaemonMessage`, from #68 through #972's `model_list` decode; split out 2026-09-02 once it alone had grown past the size cap.
    - [Inbound message decode — public contract](features/inbound-message-decode-contract.md) — The decoder's public surface: what callers pass in, what they get back, and the guarantees attached to each.
    - [Inbound message decode — internals](features/inbound-message-decode-internals.md) — How a frame is actually decoded: payload narrowing, category-only error messages, the diagnostic logging around them, and the consumer arm that drives it.
    - [Inbound message decode — edge cases and limits](features/inbound-message-decode-limits.md) — The decoder's edge cases, including why it keeps an explicit size guard when the transport already bounds the plaintext.
- [Composer send](features/composer-send.md) — the **renderer half of the send flow** (twin to the outbound send path): the previously-inert conversation composer, now wired. A controlled `<textarea>` + a submit (send button or Enter) that, via the pure React-free `submitMessage` in `composerSend.ts`, mints a `message_id`, emits `sendMessage` over `window.pyry.sendCommand` (guarded — a bridge failure never crashes the window), and appends an optimistic wire `MessagePayload { role: 'user' }` echo to the session store through a new `messageSent` action. The one minted id is reused for both the wire payload and the echo, so the store's `appendUnique` drops the daemon's later same-id echo instead of double-posting (AC3). Ephemeral input in component `useState` (ADR 0006); single milestone conversation via a `MILESTONE_CONVERSATION_ID` constant (#66) — since superseded by #448's `conversationId` parameter (per-conversation sends). [#756](codebase/756.md) added a required `dispatchFor` dep: the same echo `ThreadEvent`, built once, is now also folded into the [conversation timeline holder](features/conversation-timeline-holder.md)'s slice for the conversation sent to, dual-write alongside the existing `dispatch`. The send control is now gated on the live connection status: while `selectStatus` is not `connected` the button is disabled, the Enter path is inert, and a `role="status"` caption names why (`Connecting…`/`Not connected`/`Connection error`) — a pure `composerAvailability(status)` predicate over `ConnectionStatus`'s four arms; a UX affordance over #65+#66's already-safe path, re-enabling reactively on connect (#31). Gained a second, sibling predicate, `shouldOfferRepair(status)` — a boolean (not a total mapping) over the single `error` arm: `true` only for a non-retryable error whose `code !== 'unpair'` (excluding the self-inflicted unpair-failure error, a loop guard), gating the conversation screen's proactive re-pair affordance (#167, see below). Gained a fourth predicate on a different axis, `shouldSubmitOnKeyDown({key, shiftKey, isComposing})` — reads the keydown itself, not `ConnectionStatus`: the Enter that commits an in-progress IME composition (`isComposing` via `event.nativeEvent`, absent from React's synthetic event) now returns `false` instead of submitting the half-composed text and suppressing its own commit (#512). [#678](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-desktop/issues/678): the send `<button>` is now `ComposerSendButton`, a two-variant control that also renders the [interrupt envelope](features/interrupt-envelope.md)'s stop affordance while a turn runs — see that page for the variant contract.
- [Outbound send path](features/outbound-send-path.md) — the main/transport half of the send flow: a validated `sendMessage` command reaching `onCommand` becomes an encrypted `send_message` envelope on the live Noise relay session. Three pieces — a pure `buildSendMessage` builder (`src/main/transport/sendMessageEnvelope.ts`, sibling to `buildClientHello`, minus default injection), a `send(payload)` entry point on `createDaemonConnection` (id counter continuing from 2 after the hello's id 1; a single `driver === null` guard + a full-body `try/catch` make it an inert no-op when disconnected/pre-handshake/post-terminal and never a throw — parity mobile #490), and the single `onCommand` registration at the composition root routing `sendMessage` → `connection.send`, unsubscribed on `will-quit`. Closes #17's deferred single-registration item; entirely `src/main/`; log-free, classify-don't-forward, no new dependency. The renderer half (composer submit + optimistic echo) is #66 (#65).
- [Save debug bundle (persistence)](features/save-debug-bundle.md) — the persistence leaf of the client debug-bundle download: a pure `node:fs/promises` `saveDebugBundle(dir, bytes): Promise<string>` in `src/main/` that writes the reassembled opaque `.tar.gz` bytes **verbatim** into an injected directory (production `app.getPath('downloads')`, computed by the orchestrating command handler — so no `electron` import, Electron-free test graph) under a collision-free, owner-only (`0o600`) filename, and resolves the **absolute** path. Exclusive-create (`flag: 'wx'`) is **both** the no-overwrite guarantee **and** the collision-race close in one syscall — no `existsSync` TOCTOU, no clobber; the browser-style suffix (`pyrycode-debug-bundle.tar.gz`, ` (1)`, …) advances on each `EEXIST`, bounded at `MAX_ATTEMPTS`. Mirrors `fileSecretPersistence` (`isErrnoException` verbatim, injected-`dir` seam) but **inverts** its temp-then-`rename` — that would reintroduce a clobber race and litter a `.tmp` in the user-visible downloads folder (accepted tradeoff: a partial `.tar.gz` on crash, a re-triggerable broken download not a corrupted store). Creates no dir, emits no logs, never puts `bytes` in an `Error`; `0o600` is the confidentiality control (Windows mode ignored → per-user Downloads ACL, called out not worked around). `security-sensitive`; the persistence sibling of the #71 split, consumed by #169 (#117).
- [Debug-bundle request (outbound)](features/debug-bundle-request.md) — the outbound "ask" half of the client debug-bundle download: the background process encrypts a **bare `request_debug_bundle` control envelope** (no payload struct, no `conversation_id`, no session selector — the bundle is daemon-global, so nothing is attacker-selectable → not security-sensitive) onto the live Noise session, so the daemon begins streaming the current bundle back. A pure `buildRequestDebugBundle` builder (`src/main/transport/requestDebugBundleEnvelope.ts`, a thinner sibling of `buildSendMessage` — no payload arg) plus a `requestDebugBundle()` method on `createDaemonConnection`, a structural twin of `send`: single `driver === null` no-op guard, **shares the one `nextEnvelopeId` counter** (no second counter), never throws (parity #490). "No payload" is a **present-but-empty `payload: {}`**, not an omission — the desktop's own `decodeEnvelope` requires a present payload (`codec.ts:133`) and `Envelope.payload` stays required (no wire drift); `{}` over `null` upholds the "never emit null" posture; an accepted, daemon-tolerated divergence from mobile's omission. Adds `'request_debug_bundle'` to `EnvelopeType` (matches daemon `TypeRequestDebugBundle`). No `index.ts` change — the renderer command is #118's split, #168 (contract)+#169 (wiring). Split from #71 (#115). `requestDebugBundle` now takes a `(consumer)` — see below (#116).
- [Debug-bundle reassembly (inbound)](features/debug-bundle-reassembly.md) — the **receive half**, `security-sensitive` (it parses bytes from the untrusted relay peer): the background process recognizes the daemon's streamed reply — ordered `debug_bundle_chunk`* + one `debug_bundle_done`, or a single `error` in lieu of the stream — and reassembles the opaque `.tar.gz` archive, or fails cleanly. Additive recognition in `parseInboundMessage` (three new `InboundDaemonMessage` kinds before `default`; `error` promoted from silently-dropped `inbound-unmodeled` to modeled, content-free `inbound-decoded`); a new pure, log-free `bundleReassembler.ts` (`chunk`/`done`/`fail`, `seq === chunks.length` rejects reorder/gap/duplicate in one check, `total === chunks.length` rejects truncation, exactly-one terminal via a `settled` flag); `daemonConnection` arms **one** per-connection reassembler slot per request (daemon correlates by frame type, not `id` — at most one bundle request in flight) and adds a connection-teardown net (`terminal`/`error` → `fail('connection-lost')`). `requestDebugBundle` signature changed to `(consumer: BundleConsumer)`; a not-connected request now fails the consumer explicitly instead of a silent no-op. `fakeDaemon.ts` gained a `buildReplyFrames` streaming-serve test-infra extension. Split from #71, consumer is #118's split, #169 (#116). The teardown net widened from two sites to four: a shared `failBundleStream()` (release the slot, then fail with `connection-lost`) now also fires from `relay-link-down` (a retryable close is stream-fatal too — the supervisor re-dials into a fresh, non-resumable session) and from `dial()` (a superseded driver's `terminal` is dropped by the generation fence before it can reach the old two sites) — closing a gap that could wedge the [orchestrator](debug-bundle-orchestrator.md)'s single-in-flight flag for the process lifetime after one relay hiccup or a re-pair/unpair; corrects #169's belief that a retryable close was already covered (#505, security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/505.md)).
- [Pairing-status signal](features/pairing-status-signal.md) — the launch-time "does a stored pairing exist?" answer, served renderer←main over a new request/response channel (`pyry:pairing-status`, `invoke`/`handle`) so the app shell can pick a screen before first paint (#80) instead of inferring pairing state from `daemonConnection`'s late, error-shaped `failed{ code: 'not-paired' }`. A stateless, body-free sibling of the [pairing IPC channel](features/pairing-ipc-channel.md): a channel constant + a sealed three-outcome `PairingStatus` union (`paired` / `not-paired` / `error`, no runtime logic, no request guard — the `events.ts` shape) in `src/shared/ipc/`; a stateless main handler over the already-constructed `pairedServerStore` where `null` is the *only* not-paired path and *every* throw (malformed record OR propagated decrypt failure) is `error` via one type-blind `catch` — never collapsed into not-paired (ADR 0005); a zero-argument preload wrapper. Value-free by construction (no arm carries a field), classify-don't-forward, log-free; pulled (not pushed) so it can't race the connect sequence. Renderer routing that consumes it is #80 (#79).
- [App shell (router)](features/app-shell.md) — the renderer root turned router: `App` consumes the launch-time [pairing-status signal](features/pairing-status-signal.md) (#79) to pick the initial screen and the [pairing screen](features/pairing-input-screen.md)'s `onPaired` seam (#55) to advance in-session, assembling the built screens into the runnable whole the end-to-end milestone (#13) waits on. The `conversation` route now mounts the [paired shell](features/paired-shell.md)'s `list ⇄ thread` router rather than the conversation screen directly (#140). Three parts (the composerSend/PairingView split): a pure `routeForStatus` where the **fail-safe ternary** structurally guarantees only a genuine `paired` reaches the [conversation screen](features/conversation-shell.md) — `not-paired`, `error`, and a rejected invoke all route to [welcome](features/welcome-screen.md) (AC2, ADR 0005, never masking `error` as never-paired); a pure `AppView` route→screen view with an `assertNever`-guarded switch; and the `App` container owning the route `useState` (ephemeral, ADR 0006), the one-shot cancellable launch effect, and `onPaired`→conversation (no restart). The `pending` phase renders `null` so first paint is the neutral dark canvas, never the wrong screen (AC3); `useDaemonEventBridge` stays app-level, above the branch. Renderer-only, no main-process code, no new dependency (#80). Gained a required `onUnpaired: () => void` on `AppView`, wired at `App` to `setRoute('pairing')` — the exact reverse of `onPaired`'s flip to `conversation` — fired by the conversation screen's unpair control only after the store reset and the main-side clear both complete, so the launch-status invariant holds even on an immediate relaunch (#166). [#662](codebase/662.md) relocated the unpaired launch destination from `pairing` to the [welcome screen](features/welcome-screen.md) (shipped dormant in #657), narrowed `routeForStatus`'s return type to exclude `'pairing'` as well as `'pending'` (a compile-time guard against reintroducing a launch→pairing mapping), and — since welcome-as-root finally gives it a safe destination — wired the pairing screen's previously-unwired `onCancel` seam back to `welcome`. The pinned mid-session `onUnpaired` flip (#166) stays on `pairing`, unchanged; `unpair-repair.spec.ts` staying byte-unchanged is the negative control. Security-sensitive (architect self-review PASS): the one newly-reachable sink (`SETUP_URL`'s `target="_blank"`, now hit pre-pairing) is an already-hardened `setWindowOpenHandler` path. Code review PASS with one unresolved SHOULD FIX — `smoke.spec.ts`'s Cancel round-trip asserts a vacuous `.conversation` count-0 (the `conversation` route actually renders `.channel-list`, not `.conversation`) — see [#662 codebase notes](codebase/662.md).
- [Live e2e runbook](features/live-e2e-runbook.md) — the **manual operator gate** for the Phase-1 milestone: the full **pair → connect → send → stream** round-trip against the **live** relay + the **real** `pyry` daemon on pyrybox, run only on an operator machine, never under CI. The manual sibling of the automated fake-transport [e2e harness](features/e2e-harness.md) (#40) and the desktop equivalent of mobile's live-e2e runbook (ADR 0002 shared wire). Covers the load-bearing single-host relay-allowlist prerequisite, the numbered build→pair→confirm flow, the `connected`/send/stream UI observables (composer enable + `data-message-role` bubbles), recording the outcome on the ticket, and known limitations. No production code — the deliverable is the runbook; the three ACs are operator actions on the live stack that unblock the Phase-2 hardening + automated-test batch (#35–#41) (#13). Since [#449](codebase/449.md), the runbook also names `scripts/live-drive.mjs` (built app → production relay → live daemon → vault workdir) as the **current interim operator pre-ship gate** while [#252](features/real-claude-liveness-e2e.md)'s harness spec stays red. [#479](codebase/479.md) added `npm run e2e:real:gate`, the exit-code-safe form of `e2e:real-claude`: same specs, same `playwright.real-claude.config.ts`, but a custom Playwright reporter (wired in only via the CLI `--reporter` flag, so the plain command's pipeline-load-bearing green-on-all-skip stays byte-for-byte unchanged) turns a "passed with zero tests executed" run into a non-zero exit and prints the fixture's own skip reason(s) — closing the trap where an under-provisioned operator machine looks identical to a genuine live pass. It does not supersede `live-drive.mjs` (still a local relay) and does not resolve #449's live reply-fan-out gap. [#480](codebase/480.md) fixed `scripts/live-drive.mjs` itself: it used to always exit 0, so the documented `npm run build && node scripts/live-drive.mjs .` chain could not actually stop on RED. The script now defers a non-zero exit via `process.exitCode` (never `process.exit` inside the guarded region) on RED/timeout, any thrown step, and the no-payload early exit, so the `finally` cleanup — close app, remove temp dir, `pyry pair revoke` the throwaway **live** device — always completes first; the no-payload path used to skip that revoke entirely (a live-credential leak), and now throws into the guarded region instead. `real-daemon-session-settings.spec.ts` went red on `main` from PR #982 (issue #975, which deleted the client-side model catalog backing the Model rows) until #987 re-keyed the round-trip onto the YOLO switch, restoring the `e2e:real:gate` floor to 10/10 — recorded in § Current real-claude gate state, this doc's single authoritative record.
- [Fake relay forwarder](features/fake-relay-forwarder.md) — the **test-only** plumbing half of the round-trip harness: `startFakeRelayForwarder` stands up one in-process `ws` relay on an ephemeral loopback port and splices raw frames **byte-for-byte, opcode-preserving, content-blind** between a `/v1/client` leg (the real client under test) and a `/v1/server` leg (the #91 fake daemon). Generalises the records-only `startRelay` inlined in `relayConnection.test.ts` into an importable two-leg splice (a plain `.ts`, so #91/#89 can import it). Legs routed by upgrade **path** (never frame bytes — content-blindness enforced by the import list: only `ws`); a positive `whenReady` gate closes the 101-before-registration race (the Go `WaitBinary` analog); `close()` is idempotent. No production consumer yet, no new dependency, log-free; runs unconditionally under `npm test` (no Go toolchain, no network) (#90). Now also gains a **reconnect capability**: `dropClientLeg()` terminates the current client leg (a mid-session drop, forcing a supervised client's auto-reconnect), and an identity-guarded leg-null-on-`close` lets the ensuing re-dial re-splice to the still-connected server leg instead of being terminated — both content-blind and modal-agnostic, built for the [#416](codebase/416.md) genuine-reconnect e2e (#416). [#464](codebase/464.md) adds the clean-close counterpart, `closeClientLeg(code)` — a graceful `clientLeg.close(code)` (not `terminate()`) that reuses the same leg-null-on-`close` handler; a code in the client's `DEFAULT_FATAL_CLOSE_CODES` drives a **terminal, non-retryable** failure instead of `dropClientLeg`'s retryable 1006, and is the first forwarder capability exposed through `launchPairedApp` (surfacing the `Re-pair` affordance, #167, on the fake stack).
- [Loopback relay dev affordance](features/loopback-relay-affordance.md) — a deterministic, **test/dev-only** affordance that lets the built app's main process accept and dial a loopback `ws://127.0.0.1` relay (so the [#90 fake relay forwarder](features/fake-relay-forwarder.md) can stand in for the live `wss://` relay in automated scenarios), while a **packaged build stays byte-identical to today**. The scheme+host decision is an injectable `RelayPolicy` on `parsePairingPayload` (default = production strictness); the dev relaxation + the `isPackaged`-**false-first** `selectRelayPolicy` selector are quarantined in a new `src/main/relayPolicy.ts`; `index.ts` makes the single effectful `app.isPackaged`+`process.env` choice at the composition root. Bounded on three independent axes, all required at once: `!isPackaged` AND `PYRY_ALLOW_LOOPBACK_RELAY=1` AND scheme `ws:`+host exactly `127.0.0.1` (any port; IPv4-canonical forms accepted, `localhost`/`[::1]` rejected). `relay-not-url`/`relay-has-credentials` stay in `parsePairingPayload` and apply on both paths; all eight typed reject reasons preserved; `relayConnection.ts`/renderer/preload/wire untouched. The security-sensitive seam #93 consumes but must not own (#97).
- [Fake daemon](features/fake-daemon.md) — the **test-only, content-aware** responder half of the round-trip harness (the deliberate inverse of #90's content-blindness): `startFakeDaemon` speaks the responder side of `Noise_IK_25519_ChaChaPoly_BLAKE2s` in-process — reads IK msg1 (recovering the client static + `hello`), writes msg2 carrying `hello_ack`, `Split()`s into transport ciphers, then decrypts inbound frames and streams sealed replies while keeping the session open. Dials the #90 forwarder's `/v1/server` leg; uses the exact `NOISE_PROTOCOL` + the production `codec` (`encode/decodeEnvelope`, `encode/decodeInnerFrame`, `base64Std*`) — no hand-rolled shortcut (a lax fake gives false confidence).
