The **same** `[send, recv]` Split mapping as the initiator (noise-c role-adjusts for both roles; no swap — the flynn responder's swap does not apply), pinned by a **deterministic** round-trip oracle: a crossed mapping completes the handshake but MAC-fails the first transport frame (AC4, a `(key, nonce)`-reuse guard). Fail-closed leg boundary + a cached `whenSettled()` sealed-outcome diagnostic; log-free; fresh in-process keypair, never to disk/renderer; must never enter the production graph. A co-located self-verifying test drives the real client initiator (`createNoiseSession` + `createRelayConnection` + `codec`) through the forwarder against it, proving one handshake + one sealed round-trip unconditionally under `npm test` (no Go toolchain, no network). Consumed by the assembled-client round-trip test (#89, landed) (#91). Now also gains a **rekey-INITIATOR capability** (`initiateRekey` + an `awaiting-rekey-init` state + `handleRekeyInit` + `DEFAULT_REKEY_RESUME_MESSAGE`): it seals a `rekey_request`, answers the client's fresh `noise_init` as a fresh RESPONDER handshake (empty early-data both ways, no `rekey_ack`), atomically swaps its own ciphers, and streams msg2 + a resume frame under the new keys as the client's deterministic swap signal — the responder-side twin of #111, proven e2e in #112 (#112). Now also gains a **reconnect capability** ([#416](codebase/416.md)): `onMessage` peeks the inner-frame `type` for routing only — a `transport`-state `noise_init` (a supervised client's post-drop reconnect, carrying real `hello` early-data, not empty like a rekey) routes to a new `handleReconnect` composing `handleMsg1`'s hello/`hello_ack` with `handleRekeyInit`'s atomic swap; `pushFrame` seals a server-initiated push under the current send cipher; `reconnectResendFrames` streams an ordered, test-supplied list under the new send cipher right after the reconnect `hello_ack`. Both new pieces are modal-agnostic; the module header was narrowed to reflect the type now being routing-only, still fail-closed on a hostile `noise_init` (#416). Consumed by the milestone UI e2e specs (#435): a per-run `buildReply`/`buildReplyFrames` seeds a one-row `conversations` reply so ChannelList has a clickable row, and #94 dispatches `buildReplyFrames` on the decoded inbound type (`send_message` → the interactive stream, everything else → the row seed). Closed the last `awaiting-rekey-init` routing gap ([#524](codebase/524.md)): the daemon now dispatches by inner frame **type**, not session state, so a non-`noise_init` app frame arriving in that window is served under the OLD ciphers with the state held (`transport-decrypt-failed`, leg survives, if it doesn't decrypt) instead of tearing the leg down — reproducing the interleaved-frame rekey collision **#507** needs a harness for. Required a matching `driveClient` test-fidelity fix (the client's in-session rekey msg1 was mis-tagged, invisible under the old routing) plus a new inbound-gate harness for deterministic ordering; security-sensitive, PASS (2 non-blocking SHOULD-FIX on comment/anchor accuracy, not applied). `sendNoise`, the single outbound chokepoint, now takes a defaulted `type: OutboundInnerType = 'noise_msg'`: the three IK handshake replies (initial, rekey, reconnect msg2) pass `'noise_resp'`, matching the real daemon's `TypeNoiseResp`/`TypeNoiseMsg` split; behaviour-preserving at ship time since the client still discarded the inbound tag, closing #524's last flagged outbound-fidelity gap ahead of the client-side change that would come to depend on it ([#525](codebase/525.md)). `pushFrame` — previously a no-op outside `transport` — now also serves in `awaiting-rekey-init` (the fake's send cipher stays the live OLD one throughout that window, swapped only inside `handleRekeyInit`), the harness relaxation that let a test daemon emit the one frame kind needed to reproduce the client-side interleaved-rekey desync; `driveClient.deliverFrame` was threaded to pass the decoded inner type to the real client session instead of discarding it ([#532](codebase/532.md)). Its own test file's harness now runs every one of its 13 tests under an ambient per-test wall-clock deadline (`bounded`/`armDeadline`/`closeWhenStarted`), so a stall names the step and the budget it overran instead of a bare `Test timed out in 5000ms`; introduced for one test by [#550](codebase/550.md), generalised to the file's other 11 by #931 (see fake-daemon.md § Test-file wall-clock deadline harness — the sibling `fakeRoutingRelay.test.ts` dial harness, #904, is the per-call analog).
- [Content-free diagnostic log](features/diagnostic-log.md) — the connection-lifecycle diagnostics channel for the main process: a small logger whose allowlist is the *envelope* of an event (name, static code, byte length/count, safe hostname/path, HTTP/WS status) — **never the value inside** — turning every transport module's "LOG-FREE by construction" into "content-free-log by construction," so a real bug (e.g. the relay 404) leaves a diagnosable footprint instead of a silently classified-and-dropped error. The guarantee is structural: `DiagnosticEvent` has no `token`/`key`/`headers`/`url`/`payload` field, no index signature — enforced by the type at every call site, not a runtime scrubber ([ADR 0007](decisions/0007-content-free-diagnostics-by-construction.md)). `createDiagnosticLog` stamps a monotonic `seq` + injected `ts` and serializes one JSON line to an **injected** `DiagnosticSink` (a sink throw is swallowed so the logger can't crash the transport it observes); two sinks — `stdoutSink` (dev) and `fileRotatingSink` (packaged: sync fs, `0o700` dir / `0o600` file, size-check + rename-shift rotation with bounded retention, no `existsSync`/TOCTOU). Constructed once at the composition root (`isPackaged`-selected sink) and injected into `createDaemonConnection`, keeping the #127 (relay-leg) / #128 (daemon-leg) consumers independent; #126 adds the module + both sinks + the root wiring, **no log call sites**. Electron-free, main-process only, no new dependency (#126). A third consumer, the [inbound-decode boundary](features/inbound-message-decode.md) (#130), extended the allowlist non-breaking with a `hash?` field (a one-way frame digest — the first realization of the "additive optional fields" property) and logs `inbound-decoded`/`inbound-unmodeled` at each decode outcome; unlike #127/#128 it's a decode boundary, not a lifecycle leg. The [renderer→main diagnostics channel](features/diagnostics-channel.md) (#131) then extends this same instance's reach to *renderer* surfaces across the untrusted IPC boundary (fail-closed projection, allowlist pinned to `DiagnosticEvent`). A fourth consumer, the [noise session](features/noise-session.md) / [noise relay driver](features/noise-relay-driver.md) pair of **pre-decryption** failure boundaries (#133), extended the allowlist with a **branded** `safeBytes?: SafeBytesEncoding` field (minted only by `encodeSafeBytes`) — the mirror image of #130: keeps the raw capped hex bytes instead of hashing them, safe only because the failure is *before* decryption; the brand makes "which call sites may populate this field" a compile-time property, not just "does the field exist." A fifth consumer, the once-per-session [startup banner](codebase/132.md) (#132), extended the allowlist a third time with three **plain, unbranded** `string` fields (`appVersion?`/`noiseProtocol?`/`protocolVersion?`) sourced from build-time constants — `app.getVersion()` and the shared `NOISE_PROTOCOL`/`PROTOCOL_VERSION` — via a new Electron-free `sessionBanner.ts` helper called once at the composition root, before `createDaemonConnection`, so the record lands as `seq 0`. The last of the five #125 Bucket-1 splits to ship; only a shared correlation id remains deferred on #125.
- [Renderer→main diagnostics channel](features/diagnostics-channel.md) — the one-way, **content-free** IPC pipe from the React window to the [#126 logger](features/diagnostic-log.md): `window.pyry.sendDiagnostic(record)` → `ipcRenderer.send` (preload) → `onDiagnostic` (main) → **project at the untrusted boundary** → the **same** `diagnosticLog` instance the transport writes to, so renderer-side surfaces land in the one debug bundle without ever giving the renderer a path to emit a secret. A faithful copy of the [command channel](features/command-channel.md) with **one deliberate deviation**: a single validate+project boundary function (`projectDiagnosticEvent`, fail-closed allowlist, fresh object, 128-char string cap) instead of guard+forward-raw — because the logger *spreads* its argument (`{ ...fields, seq, ts }`), so a raw-forward would spread a planted field onto the log line. `RendererDiagnosticEvent` mirrors #126's `DiagnosticEvent` field-for-field, pinned by a compile-time type-equality assertion (placed main-side to avoid the `TS6307` shared→main trap). One-way, returns void, swallows faults (can't crash the window it observes); shipped the pipe + a test emitter only, inert until its first consumer. `security-sensitive`, PASS (#131). The pin relaxed to `Equals<RendererDiagnosticEvent, Omit<DiagnosticEvent,'safeBytes'>>` once #133 added a **branded**, `src/main`-only `safeBytes?` field that structurally cannot be mirrored into `shared` — the "renderer can't invent a field" enforcement direction survives; capability-parity for a renderer-inaccessible field does not (#133). #132's once-per-session version banner extended the `Omit` list a second time with three plain, unbranded main-only fields (`appVersion?`/`noiseProtocol?`/`protocolVersion?`) — proof the escape hatch tracks "renderer cannot/must not produce this field" generally, not only branded types (#132). The [session store](features/session-store.md) is the first real consumer: an optional `dispatch`-level observer emits one `{event:'store-transition', code:action.type, count:messages.length}` record per action, using existing allowlist fields with no growth needed (#134).
- [Secret-backend dev affordance](features/secret-backend-affordance.md) — a deterministic, **test/dev-only** affordance that lets the built app's main process select a keychain-free `SecretEncryption` backend, so the pair → device-key → connect path can run headless keychain-less (#93 consumes it) without [`secureStore.set`](features/secure-store.md) failing closed — while a **packaged build stays byte-identical to today** (the real fail-closed OS-keychain backend). `SecretEncryption` is *already* an injected seam, so this needs **no edit to any chain file**: a new pure `src/main/secretBackend.ts` owns `selectSecretEncryption({ isPackaged, env, real })` + `TEST_SECRET_BACKEND_ENV_FLAG = 'PYRY_TEST_SECRET_BACKEND'`; `index.ts` makes the single effectful choice, passing `electronSecretEncryption` **uncalled** so the module stays `electron`-free. Bounded on two axes, both required: `!isPackaged` (checked **false-first**) AND the env flag `=== '1'`; the keychain-free backend is a stateless marker-byte + XOR transform with `isAvailable() === true`. The exact **twin of #97's [loopback relay affordance](features/loopback-relay-affordance.md)** for the secret chain; `secureStore.ts`/`electronSecretEncryption.ts`/persistence/renderer/preload/wire untouched. The security-sensitive seam #93 consumes but must not own (#99).
- [Debug-bundle orchestrator](features/debug-bundle-orchestrator.md) — the main-process consumer that finally ties the client debug-bundle download together: on the renderer's bare `requestDebugBundle` command, drives request ([#115](codebase/115.md)) → reassemble ([#116](codebase/116.md)) → save ([#117](codebase/117.md)) and emits progress + one terminal `DaemonEvent` ([#168](codebase/168.md)) back to the window. The only debug-bundle slice touching Electron (`app.getPath('downloads')`) and IPC. A single `active` flag, set synchronously *before* the transport call and cleared only at the three terminal-emit sites (save-resolve/save-reject/`fail`), enforces single-in-flight so a spammed command can't orphan the in-flight reassembler slot. A total exhaustive `categoryFor` switch collapses the transport's 5-value `BundleFailReason` plus a save errno onto the 3-value `DebugBundleFailure`; the save-reject handler takes no error parameter, so the fs errno is structurally unleakable (#169). Its first and only UI driver is the [conversation shell](features/conversation-shell.md)'s new Log data section (see above), which finally closes the [#71](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-desktop/issues/71) debug-bundle family end to end (#72). A destroyed main window could previously strand the `active` flag forever — its terminals emit before clearing it, and on macOS a post-close emit used to throw; [#518](codebase/518.md)'s `emitDaemonEvent` guard removes the throw (no edit to this module itself), proven by a regression test that composes the real emitter into this orchestrator's wiring. Two more connection-teardown paths could strand `active` the same way — a retryable relay drop and a superseded driver's fenced terminal during `reconnect()` — closed entirely inside [debug-bundle reassembly](debug-bundle-reassembly.md)'s teardown net with no edit to this module; verified here by composing the real `createDebugBundleDownload` over the real `connection.requestDebugBundle` and asserting a *later* `request()` reaches the wire, the only way to observe the flag actually clear (#505, security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/505.md)).
- [Screen snapshot fetch](features/screen-snapshot-fetch.md) — the on-demand data path for the Run configuration sheet's model/effort/YOLO: a `request_snapshot`→`screen_snapshot` round trip riding the `send_message` spine end to end in one ticket (no reassembler/consumer — a single fire-and-forget request, a single uncorrelated reply). ADR-025's always-available, parser-independent daemon escape hatch — **not** gated on `interactive`, so it works today even though this client withholds that capability (pyrycode #847/#848). Five additive pieces: ported `RequestSnapshotPayload`/`ScreenSnapshotPayload` wire types (all six reply fields always present, no `omitempty`); a new payload-carrying `buildRequestSnapshot` (`src/main/transport/requestSnapshotEnvelope.ts`); `requestSnapshot(payload)` on `createDaemonConnection` — the **`send` twin**, not `requestDebugBundle`'s consumer-failing twin; a fail-closed `parseScreenSnapshotPayload` + `requireBoolean` at the [inbound-decode boundary](features/inbound-message-decode.md); and the `requestSnapshot`/`snapshotReceived` sealed-union members on the existing [command](features/command-channel.md)/[event](features/daemon-event-channel.md) channels. The **load-bearing content-minimisation seam**: the reply's `text` (rendered screen) is decoded but dropped at the `daemonConnection.ts` emit site — the dedicated `snapshotReceived{model,effort,yolo}` shape has no field for it to occupy. Split A of [#156](codebase/156.md); unblocks #181, since split into [#187](codebase/187.md) (store facet + fetch-on-open, see below) and [#188](codebase/188.md) (render, see below). Security review PASS; code review PASS, no findings (#180). Extended by [#191](codebase/191.md) (transport slice of the context-window usage feature, split from #182) to carry two more always-present fields, `used_tokens`/`window_tokens` (pyrycode/pyrycode#857) — decoded via the same `requireNumber` helper the bundle `seq`/`total` use, `0` a valid value not an absence, and added to the emitted `snapshotReceived` event; the run-config store ([#187](codebase/187.md)) stayed three-field only until [#192](codebase/192.md) (the gauge render sibling) widened it by the same two fields and rendered the Context window section. #191: security + code review PASS, one informational NIT (snake_case field naming, confirmed intentional). #192: code review PASS, no corrections. **[#316](codebase/316.md) deliberately reverses the `text` drop**: `case 'snapshot'` now fires a second, dedicated `screenSnapshotReceived{text,ts}` event alongside the unchanged `snapshotReceived` — a security-reviewed widening (ADR-025: the live-screen view *is* the rendered `text`), bounded by a fresh named-field literal to exactly those two fields; unblocks the display slice #318. Security + code review PASS, one informational NIT (kept wire name `ts`, pre-adjudicated, no action). **[#620](codebase/620.md) removes the outbound half**: `requestSnapshot`/`request_snapshot`/`RequestSnapshotPayload` and `requestSnapshotEnvelope.ts` are gone from the command union, the connection method, and the wire types — a stale-renderer `requestSnapshot` command now hits the boundary guard's default-deny. **[#621](codebase/621.md) removes both `DaemonEvent` members and the `case 'snapshot'` emit that produced them**: `snapshotReceived` and `screenSnapshotReceived` are gone from the union, along with their two no-op cases in each of the three exhaustive renderer bridges (`assertNever` still terminates all three). A well-formed `screen_snapshot` frame is now decoded (`parseScreenSnapshotPayload` untouched) and silently dropped — no event, no log call added in its place (security-reviewed, confirmed by code review). 17 files, ~50 production lines removed, none added. Code review PASS, no MUST/SHOULD FIX. **[#622](codebase/622.md) then removes the inbound decode itself and the wire types** — `parseScreenSnapshotPayload`, the `snapshot` `InboundDaemonMessage` kind, and `screen_snapshot`/`ScreenSnapshotPayload` are all gone; because `Envelope.type` is an open union the removal is behaviourally inert, so the deliverable is the pin: a well-formed or malformed `screen_snapshot` now decodes to `null` via the tolerant `default` arm rather than throwing, proven by a new 5-`it` describe, and the anti-leak diagnostic-log assertion is carried onto that arm rather than deleted. Two production files, deletions only (net −77/+2, the +2 a doc rewrap). No piece of this feature survives; security review PASS, code review PASS.
- [Run configuration store](features/run-config-store.md) — the renderer's held copy of the session's Model/Effort/YOLO plus context-window usage: a dedicated Zustand vanilla store (`runConfigStore`, single `setSnapshot`, no reducer — there's exactly one mutation) mirroring the [session store](features/session-store.md)'s DI-factory→singleton→hook→selectors shape, fed by a framework-free data path (`toRunConfigSnapshot`/`requestRunConfigSnapshot`/`subscribeRunConfig`, the `composerSend`/`logDataDownload` injected-effects idiom) and a headless `RunConfigData(): null` container mounted as the sheet body's first child. Requests a fresh session-settings reply exactly once per sheet open — the container's mount **is** the open transition (the sheet body only mounts while open), guarded by a `useRef` one-shot flag so a StrictMode double-invoke still fires exactly one request; the arriving event replaces the held snapshot unconditionally, verbatim (empty `model`/`effort`, `yolo: false`, and `windowTokens: 0` all included, never coerced). Originally a `request_snapshot`→`snapshotReceived` round trip (#180); **moved onto a bare `requestSessionSettings`→`runConfigReceived` round trip at #491/#500** (screen_snapshot is refused whenever there's no terminal to photograph — always, on the stream-json interactive runner — so the sheet was inert in production; the new reply also finally carries a session id, written to the [session-id store](features/session-id-store.md) on the same frame), with `snapshotReceived`/`screenSnapshotReceived` removed outright by [#621](codebase/621.md) — see [Run configuration store § Moved off screen_snapshot](features/run-config-store.md#moved-off-screen_snapshot-491500). No visible surface of its own; [#188](codebase/188.md) renders the Model/Effort/YOLO sections from `selectSnapshot` — a pure `RunConfigView(model,effort,yolo,usedTokens,windowTokens)` (a static Model catalog matched by case-insensitive family substring, five exact-match Effort segments, a read-only `role="switch"` YOLO toggle) plus a thin `RunConfigSections` container that coalesces the store's `null` and a real all-defaults snapshot to the same render path, mounted second in the sheet body ahead of `LogDataSection`. One new token, `--color-surface-container-highest` (the switch's off-track fill). [#191](codebase/191.md) extended the `snapshotReceived` event this store consumes with two usage fields (the store required zero change at the time — explicit-copy design, not a spread); [#192](codebase/192.md) then widened both `RunConfigSnapshot` and `toRunConfigSnapshot` by `usedTokens`/`windowTokens` and added the fourth section, `ContextWindowSection` — a usage-line + `role="progressbar"` fill bar when `windowTokens > 0`, clamped to 100%, or a muted "Context usage unavailable" line when `windowTokens <= 0` (the container's null-default `windowTokens: 0` folds into the same branch as the daemon's own unavailable signal, so the division never runs). Zero new tokens (reuses `--color-success` + `--color-surface-container-highest`). [#257](codebase/257.md) then made the Model/Effort/YOLO sections **interactive**: each gains an optional handler (`onSelect`/`onSelect`/`onToggle`) whose *presence* is the whole operable-vs-inert toggle — given one, a row/segment/switch becomes `role="button"`/`role="switch"` (dropping `aria-readonly`), `tabIndex={0}`, and clickable; given none it renders byte-identical to the read-only markup above. The container now composes [`selectEffectiveSettings`](features/run-settings-write-store.md) (raw write state selected, composed in the render body — not the selector, since it returns a fresh object) over this store's snapshot, and gates the handler on `sessionIdStore`'s `session_id !== null` (AC5) both structurally (withholds the handler) and at runtime (a new `changeSetting` helper's null guard, `runSettingsControls.ts`). A rejected change's field renders a `role="alert"` `--color-error` line (new `RUN_CONFIG_ERROR_COPY` map, apostrophe-free); rollback itself needs no new code (the write store already drops the pending marker). Code review PASS with one non-blocking SHOULD FIX, not applied: the operable rows/segments/switch get a focus ring via `tabIndex` but wire only `onClick`, so Enter/Space do nothing for a keyboard-only user (a native `<button>` would get this for free) — flagged as a fast-follow, not the model list's deliberately-deferred `radiogroup` elevation. Gained a sixth section, `RunningModelSection`, rendered ahead of `ModelSection` — sources the sibling [Announced-model store](features/announced-model-store.md) via a fourth container read, not this store's snapshot; resolves the announced identifier by exact `===` equality against `MODEL_CATALOG` (deliberately not `matchedFamily`'s case-insensitive substring match, which the override row above still uses), rendering a catalog hit's display name, a miss verbatim character-for-character, an explicit not-yet-known line before any announcement arrives, and a daemon-reported cut as an independent sibling marker element (never text concatenated into the daemon-supplied value, closing the forgery risk a concatenated marker would open). The new prop is optional so all ~51 pre-existing `RunConfigView` render sites keep compiling and passing unmodified. Renderer-only, security-sensitive (architect self-review PASS), code review PASS with three comment-accuracy NITs, no MUST FIX / SHOULD FIX (#560, see [codebase notes](codebase/560.md)). **Split the feed by lifetime (#810):** the subscription that lands `runConfigReceived` moved out to a new app-level headless leaf, `RunConfigLiveData` (`runConfigLive.ts`, the `conversationListBridge` shape — React-free helpers plus the leaf, the ninth in `App.tsx`), which is now the store's *only* writer and refreshes on two daemon-event edges — a rising edge to `connected`, and each running → not-running `turnState` transition, held per conversation in a `Set` (never a plain object) so a re-asserted phase is not an edge and daemon-supplied ids cannot reach a prototype setter. `RunConfigData` keeps its per-open request, unedited, and lost only its subscribe effect. `session_settings` stayed reply-only (no wire change) — the reason a subscription alone could never have kept the figures fresh. Security-sensitive, architect self-review PASS. **#811** gave the two usage figures a second reader with no change to this store: the composer's new footer row ([conversation shell § Composer footer row](features/conversation-shell-composer.md#composer-footer-row-811)) renders a "Context: N%" reading off the same `selectSnapshot`, and the percentage math that used to be inline in `ContextWindowSection` is now one shared `contextUsagePercent(usedTokens, windowTokens): number | null` (new `contextUsage.ts`) both surfaces call — closing a `NaN`/`Infinity` gap the old inline clamp had on an overflowing daemon value via an added `Number.isFinite(windowTokens)` term. **The "bare `requestSessionSettings`" framing above describes the request only through 2026-08-19**: two daemon commits made the frame conversation-keyed on 2026-08-20, silently degrading every unnamed request (which is every request this store's callers send) to a zero-valued reply instead of the real running configuration — root-cause slice 1 of [#941](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-desktop/issues/941). [#945](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-desktop/issues/945) threads a `conversation_id` onto the wire frame through `src/main/`/`src/shared/` only, normalising an absent id to `conversation_id: ''`; this store's own callers are unchanged and still send no id, so production behaviour stays exactly as broken until [#946](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-desktop/issues/946) sources a real one from this store's conversation state. See [Run configuration store § Conversation-keyed since 2026-08-20](features/run-config-store.md#conversation-keyed-since-2026-08-20-945946). **#1020 added a seventh field, `permissionMode`** (`SessionSettingsPayload.permission_mode`, pyrycode#1687), threaded verbatim through decode/emit/IPC/snapshot as a required string — one of claude's six modes, or `''` meaning "no session was resolved" (read as a pair with `sessionId: ''`), never derived from `yolo`, no client-side allowlist (the read half deliberately carries a mode the write half's closed five refuses, #1021). No consumer yet — #682 is the first. See [Run configuration store § Permission mode](features/run-config-store.md#permission-mode-1020).
- [Thread timeline (conversation model)](features/thread-timeline.md) — a heterogeneous, ordered conversation-timeline data model, the foundation the structured event-stream render vertical builds on: a `ThreadItem` discriminated union (`assistantText`/`toolCall`/`turnBoundary`) plus a pure, exported `reduceTimeline` reducer over a renderer-local `ThreadEvent` input union, mirroring `sessionStore`'s reducer shape exactly (sealed union, `assertNever` guard, same-reference-on-no-change discipline). Introduced **alongside** `sessionStore`'s `MessagePayload[]` as a Strangler Fig — nothing cuts over, no consumer imports it, the coarse `message` path is untouched. Successive `assistantDelta`s for the same turn coalesce by tail-check into one growing bubble; a `toolResult` merges into its `toolCall` by `toolUseId`, an orphan or duplicate result resolving to a documented same-state-reference no-op (AC4) rather than a throw; `turn_state` is a scalar `phase` beside `items`, not a timeline row. Pure model + reducer only — no store, no React, no wire types, no IPC (#121). The vertical decomposed transport → store → render: [#199](codebase/199.md) (shipped) built the wire types/decode/`DaemonEvent` arms this module's `ThreadEvent` targets; [#202](codebase/202.md) (shipped) added the store + `DaemonEvent→ThreadEvent` bridge, see [Conversation timeline store](features/conversation-timeline-store.md); [#203](codebase/203.md) (shipped) renders the streamed text (see [Conversation shell](features/conversation-shell.md)), gating #179, and resolved the deferred React-key question as array index (safe given the reducer's provably append-only, tail-mutating shape); [#214](codebase/214.md) (shipped) wired the `turn_state` transport → bridge chain, giving `selectPhase` its first real source (no render yet); [#217](codebase/217.md) (shipped) wired the `tool_use` transport → bridge chain, giving `reduceTimeline`'s pre-existing `toolUse` arm its first real feed — a `toolCall` item now lands on `selectItems` in arrival order (no render yet — that's #218); [#229](codebase/229.md) (shipped) wired the `tool_result` transport → bridge chain, the vertical's last transport slice, giving `reduceTimeline`'s pre-existing `toolResult` arm its first real feed — a `toolCall`'s `result` now resolves in place via `fillResult` (no render yet — that's #230). [#286](codebase/286.md) (shipped) added a fifth `ThreadItem`/`ThreadEvent` kind, `sessionBoundary` — the `/clear`/idle-eviction/workspace-change delimiter #285 widened the `sessionTransition` `DaemonEvent` arm to carry — wired live end to end (bridge arm + render row) in one ticket, the vertical's first slice built and rendered together rather than split across transport/store/render tickets. [#317](codebase/317.md) (shipped) added a `stalled: boolean` scalar beside `phase` (not a `ThreadItem`) — a nullary `stallDetected` arm sets it, the four turn-activity arms clear it, the three non-activity arms carry it through; the two pre-existing same-reference no-op guards (`toolResult` orphan, `turnState` same-phase) widened to `&& !state.stalled` rather than gaining a branch. [#493](codebase/493.md) (shipped) added a third scalar, `apiRetry: ApiRetryStatus | null`, via a data-carrying `apiRetry` arm — the deliberate inverse of `stalled`: the four turn-activity arms now also carry `apiRetry` through unchanged (the two widened no-op guards above do **not** gain a matching clause), since the wire's `api_retry` frame clears only on its own explicit falling edge, never on turn activity. [#496](codebase/496.md) (shipped) added a fourth scalar, `compacting: boolean` — `apiRetry`'s clearing-rule inversion again but a plain flag, not a `| null` record, since the wire carries no counter to discard on clear; the arm collapses to a single same-reference-or-fresh-state ternary. [#528](codebase/528.md) (shipped) added a nullary `reset` arm — the first arm that is neither daemon- nor user-content-derived, ported verbatim from `sessionStore`'s `reset` (#166): returns the shared `initialTimelineState` constant, clearing all five fields at once, idempotent by reference, `items` un-churned. Shipped capability-only, no dispatch site; [#530](codebase/530.md) (navigation, shipped) wired the first, id-gated, and [#531](codebase/531.md) (unpair / pair-another-server, shipped) wired the second, unconditionally, via the new `clearPairingScopedState` helper alongside `activeConversationStore`/`sessionIdStore`/`sessionStore` clears. See [ADR 0008](decisions/0008-thread-timeline-model.md).
- [Conversation timeline store](features/conversation-timeline-store.md) — the renderer read/write surface over the thread-timeline model: a Zustand store (`createTimelineStore`/`timelineStore`/`useTimelineStore`) wrapping `reduceTimeline` with a real `dispatch`, mirroring `createSessionStore`'s DI-factory shape (not `runConfigStore`'s single setter); plus `translateTimelineEvent`, a second `assertNever`-guarded switch over `DaemonEvent` that owns exactly `assistantDelta`/`turnEnd`/`turnState` (the mirror image of `daemonEventBridge`'s switch, which owns the rest) and `useTimelineBridge`, its StrictMode-safe one-listener binding. Purely additive, Strangler Fig (#202). `useTimelineBridge()` now mounts in `App.tsx` beside `useDaemonEventBridge()`, and `selectItems` now has its first reader — the [conversation shell](features/conversation-shell.md)'s new `Timeline` view (#203). Gained a third owned arm, `turnState` — the first `DaemonEvent` addition since this bridge existed, so it forced a case here **and** in `daemonEventBridge` at once; `event.state` (`WireTurnState`) assigns to the `ThreadEvent` arm's `state` (`TurnPhase`) with no cast, the same literal union on both sides. `selectPhase` now has a real source; still no reader (#214). Gained a fourth owned arm, `toolUse` — the first arm whose mapping produces a durable `ThreadItem` (an appended `toolCall`, `result: null`) rather than text or a scalar; at ship time `DaemonEvent.toolUse` and `ThreadEvent.toolUse` were field-for-field identical, so the arm was a pure filter-and-copy — [#763](codebase/763.md) later widened `DaemonEvent.toolUse` with `conversationId`, dropped at the bridge, so `ThreadEvent.toolUse` stays the five-field side. `selectItems` now carries a `toolCall` item; no render yet (#217). Gained two arms added to the **inverse-filter** `null` list instead of the owned block — `modalShown`/`modalDismissed` — the first `DaemonEvent` pair since this bridge existed that it does NOT consume; a modal is neither a session action nor a timeline event, real consumer is the third, independent [modal store + bridge](features/modal-store-bridge.md) (#201, shipped #223). Gained a fifth owned arm, `toolResult` — the outcome half of `toolUse`, the vertical's last transport slice; the first arm whose mapping **resolves** an existing `ThreadItem` (a `toolCall`'s `result`, via `reduceTimeline`'s pre-existing `fillResult`, #121) rather than appending one or setting a scalar; at ship time `DaemonEvent.toolResult` and `ThreadEvent.toolResult` were field-for-field identical, so the arm was again a pure filter-and-copy — [#766](codebase/766.md) later widened `DaemonEvent.toolResult` with `conversationId` too, completing the family (all eight arms now carry it), dropped at the bridge so `ThreadEvent.toolResult` stays the four-field side. By now `modalBridge.ts` (#223) also exists, so this arm forced a case in three exhaustive `DaemonEvent` switches at once — a cost the spec's own scope self-check (written before #223 merged) undercounted by one file (#229, see [codebase notes](codebase/229.md)). [#179](codebase/179.md) also retargeted the [conversation shell](features/conversation-shell.md)'s composer echo onto this store's `dispatch` as a sixth, renderer-sourced `userText` event — the one `ThreadEvent` arm this store's `dispatch` writes directly rather than the bridge translating from a `DaemonEvent`. Gained a `sessionTransition` arm folded into the **inverse-filter** `null` list — not a timeline item, unlike `turnState`; real consumer is the not-yet-built renderer holder #259 (#254, see [codebase notes](codebase/254.md)). Gained a `sessionSettingsUpdated` arm folded into the same **inverse-filter** `null` list; real consumer is #261 / #256, not yet built (#264, see [session settings send](features/session-settings-send.md), [codebase notes](codebase/264.md)). Gained a `conversationUpdated` arm folded into the same list, beside `conversationCreated` — not a timeline item; real consumer is the list-reflect slice #275, not yet built (#273, see [conversation promote](features/conversation-promote.md)). Gained a sixth owned arm, `stallDetected` — moved out of the inverse-filter `null` list [#315](codebase/315.md) shipped it into, since a real consumer now exists: a fresh, field-identical `{type:'stallDetected'}` literal (arm-selection only, no field mapping, both sides nullary) feeding `reduceTimeline`'s new `stalled` scalar. `selectStalled` joins `selectItems`/`selectPhase` ([#317](codebase/317.md), see [codebase notes](codebase/317.md)). Gained a seventh owned arm, `apiRetry` — moved out of the inverse-filter `null` list [#492](codebase/492.md) shipped it into: at ship time a field-for-field literal (`active`/`current`/`total`, a filter-and-copy like `toolUse`/`toolResult`, not `stallDetected`'s arm-selection-only shape) feeding `reduceTimeline`'s new `apiRetry: ApiRetryStatus | null` scalar. Unlike `stalled`, this scalar does **not** clear on turn activity — it clears only on the wire's explicit falling edge (`active: false`), so the four turn-activity arms carry it through unchanged rather than joining `stalled`'s clear-set, the deliberate inversion AC4 exists to guard. `selectApiRetry` joins `selectItems`/`selectPhase`/`selectStalled` ([#493](codebase/493.md), see [codebase notes](codebase/493.md)). [#737](codebase/737.md) later widened `DaemonEvent.apiRetry` with `conversationId`, dropped at the bridge, so `ThreadEvent.apiRetry` stays the four-field side; borrowed citations of the old identity elsewhere retired by [#738](codebase/738.md). Gained an eighth owned arm, `compacting` — moved out of the inverse-filter `null` list [#495](codebase/495.md) shipped it into: a field-for-field literal (`active` only, a filter-and-copy like `apiRetry`) feeding `reduceTimeline`'s new `compacting: boolean` scalar — `apiRetry`'s clearing-rule inversion again, but a plain flag since there's no counter on the wire to discard on clear. `selectCompacting` joins `selectItems`/`selectPhase`/`selectStalled`/`selectApiRetry` ([#496](codebase/496.md), see [codebase notes](codebase/496.md)). [#756](codebase/756.md) adds no new arm and no field — `translateTimelineEvent` is byte-identical before/after — but gives the [conversation timeline holder](features/conversation-timeline-holder.md) (#755) its first writer: a new sibling pure function `timelineTargetFor(event)` attributes each of the eight id-carrying owned arms to its own `conversationId`; `subscribeTimeline`'s injected `dispatch` widens by **arity**, `(event) => void` to `(event, conversationId: string | null) => void`, so all 20 existing call sites compile and pass unedited; `useTimelineBridge` fans out to the flat store (unconditional, first) and then the keyed holder (guarded on non-null id) — a verified no-op since nothing reads the holder yet (#758). [#784](codebase/784.md), the first widening to land after `timelineTargetFor` existed, moved `unrecognizedMessage`'s `case` from the id-less group into the id-carrying one (a label move, not a new branch) — nine id-carrying owned arms now, `sessionTransition`/`connected` the only two left with nothing to attribute to; the translator's returned `ThreadEvent` still omits the id, so the id-carrying move only changes which conversation's retained slice the row lands in, not what the row looks like. [#785](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-desktop/issues/785) gives the keyed holder its first write for the two arms that still have no key of their own — `sessionTransition`→`sessionBoundary` and `connected`→`reconnected` — via a new sibling pure function, `timelineWriteTarget(event, conversationId, getOpenConversationId)`: the event's own attribution wins if present, and only for those two named arms does it fall back to an injected `getOpenConversationId` getter (never an import, keeping this module's import-ban literally true) resolving to the conversation on screen, or drops the event from the keyed path with no key invented when none is open. `timelineTargetFor` and `subscribeTimeline` stay byte-identical; `useTimelineBridge` gains one parameter, threaded from a new module-level constant in `App.tsx` (its one production call site). Ships as a verified no-op on the flat store the screen still reads, same as #756.
- [Conversation list fetch](features/conversation-list-fetch.md) — the transport data path for the daemon's live conversation list (mirror mobile #312): a bare `list_conversations` v2 request → an ordered `conversations` reply, decoded field-for-field into `ConversationSummary[]` (`id`/`name: string|null`/`is_promoted`/`is_archived`/`cwd`/`last_message_ts`/`last_used_at`, all always-present) and emitted as one `conversationsReceived` `DaemonEvent`, reused verbatim (no content-minimisation — nothing is secret). An explicitly verified clone of the [screen snapshot fetch](features/screen-snapshot-fetch.md) vertical, needing both the inbound decode chain and the outbound `requestConversations` `RendererCommand` + boundary guard (8 files, like #180). `name: null` decodes to a distinct "unnamed" value via a new `requireStringOrNull` helper — the codec's first nullable-field checker. No UI, no store, no request trigger yet — those are the sibling [conversation list store](features/conversation-list-store.md) (#208, shipped), unblocking #141/#142's list UI (#139).
- [Conversation create](features/conversation-create.md) — the write-side twin of conversation-list-fetch: a `create_conversation{is_promoted,name,cwd}` v2 request (all three server-defaultable, `null`-and-**present**, never optional — the daemon's `*T` fields carry no `omitempty`) → a `conversation_created` reply, decoded into its own 5-field `ConversationCreatedPayload` (deliberately not a reuse of the 7-field `ConversationSummary`) and emitted as one `conversationCreated` `DaemonEvent`. The connection method builds a **fresh literal** naming exactly the three modeled fields before encrypting — the deterministic net bounding the outbound wire regardless of what the structural-minimum `isCreateConversationPayload` guard let through. Ten production files, one new (`createConversationEnvelope.ts`); by this ticket a new `DaemonEvent` arm costs a case in all three exhaustive bridges (session, timeline, modal), not two. The sibling render ticket, [the new-discussion FAB](features/new-discussion-fab.md), has since shipped — it fires the command and opens the new thread (#241, #242). Gained a direct transport twin, [conversation promote](features/conversation-promote.md) — see below.
- [Conversation promote](features/conversation-promote.md) — the direct twin of conversation-create, split from #143: a `promote_conversation{conversation_id,name,cwd}` request (all three **required** strings — the deliberate opposite of create's nullable trio) asks the daemon to flip an existing discussion's `is_promoted` bit; the daemon confirms by **broadcasting** `conversation_updated{id,is_promoted,name,cwd,last_used_at}` to every client on the server-id, not via `in_reply_to` — the `assistant_delta` unsolicited-event pattern, so this slice adds **no** outstanding-request correlation memory (unlike the `pendingSettings`-correlated session-settings paths). `ConversationUpdatedPayload` orders `name` **before** `cwd`, the intentional reordering vs. `ConversationCreatedPayload` (spec #274). The connection method builds the same fresh-literal security net as `createConversation`. Ten production files, one new (`promoteConversationEnvelope.ts`); a new `DaemonEvent` arm still costs a case in all three exhaustive bridges. The renderer-facing [Save-as-channel dialog](features/save-as-channel-dialog.md) (#274, shipped) and the live-list reflection ([#275](features/conversation-list-store.md), shipped) both consume this slice, which they were blocked by (#273).
- [Conversation list store](features/conversation-list-store.md) — the renderer's held copy of the daemon's live conversation list: a dedicated Zustand store (`createConversationListStore`/`conversationListStore`/`useConversationListStore`, single `setConversations` setter, `null` not-loaded sentinel, whole-list replace — the `runConfigStore` shape, not `timelineStore`'s reducer) fed by `conversationListBridge.ts` (`translateConversationsEvent`, a soft `default: null` filter mirroring `toRunConfigSnapshot`; `requestConversationList`; `subscribeConversations`) and mounted app-level as the headless `ConversationListData(): null` leaf in `App.tsx`, alongside `useDaemonEventBridge()`. Rows held **verbatim in wire snake_case** — no camelCase remap, no derivations (`is_promoted`/`name===null` interpreted downstream by the [Channel List screen](features/channel-list.md), #141, shipped). Requests the list once per connection episode on the rising edge to `connected` (`useRef` one-shot guard, resets on disconnect so a reconnect re-requests); the whole-list-replace setter makes each re-request idempotent. A **component**, not a hook in `App`, so the connected-gate `useSessionStore` read doesn't cascade a re-render into `ConversationScreen` via `App`'s unstable inline `onPaired`/`onUnpaired` arrows. Not security-sensitive, no main/IPC/wire code (that's #139, already shipped). Code review PASS, no findings (#208). Gained a required third `subscribeConversations` param, `refreshOnChange: () => void`, and a new pure boolean predicate `isConversationUpdated` — on a `conversationUpdated` broadcast (a promote/rename/archive, #273) the listener re-requests the list instead of writing rows, so the affected row's `is_promoted` flip lands via the daemon's authoritative reply without a reconnect, keeping the once-deferred "richer refresh policy" resolved and the [Channel List](features/channel-list.md)'s `partitionByPromotion` tier move automatic with no view change ([#275](codebase/275.md)). `isConversationUpdated` renamed to `shouldRefreshList` and widened to also match `conversationDeleted{id}` (#375's correlated permanent-delete reply, no broadcast) — one occurrence-only predicate covering both "a conversation changed" arms rather than a second `isX` predicate OR'd at the call site; still id-blind, still fires no store write of its own, so a deleted row leaves the list only once the daemon's fresh re-list omits it ([#376](codebase/376.md)). `shouldRefreshList` widened to a third arm, `conversationCreated` — the daemon sends no broadcast on a plain create either (only a correlated reply to the requester), so a FAB-created discussion previously stayed absent from the Channel List until a reconnect or an unrelated mutation ("Gap A", documented by the e2e suite at #440/#451/#452). No optimistic insert: the 5-field `ConversationCreatedPayload` lacks `is_archived`/`last_message_ts`, so the fix rides the same re-request seam, creator-only (a second client's list stays stale, unfixable renderer-side, out of scope). Ten stale comment sites rewritten, zero e2e assertions changed. Code review PASS, 0 MUST/SHOULD, 2 non-blocking NITs ([#515](codebase/515.md)).
- [Paired shell (list ⇄ thread ⇄ settings ⇄ archive router)](features/paired-shell.md) — the second-level router under the app shell's `conversation` route, mirroring the `appRoute.ts`/`AppView` split: a pure `PairedRoute = 'list' | 'thread' | 'settings' | 'pairServer' | 'archive'` model + `nextPairedRoute` transition (`pairedRoute.ts`) and a `PairedShellView`/`PairedShell` pure-view+`useReducer`-container pair (`PairedShell.tsx`, ADR 0006). The paired region enters at a `list` view (initially a throwaway placeholder; now the real [Channel List](features/channel-list.md), #141) instead of dropping straight into the thread; opening it mounts the existing [conversation shell](features/conversation-shell.md), which gained an optional `onBack?` back affordance (Figma 16-9 `arrow_back`, the `onUnpaired?` precedent). The `open` transition gained a second trigger — the [new-discussion FAB](features/new-discussion-fab.md)'s `useConversationCreatedNav` hook, mounted in the `PairedShell` container, dispatches it on a daemon-confirmed `conversationCreated` event (#242). That callback's `created` payload — previously received and discarded — is now also snapshotted into a new `activeConversationStore` before the `open` dispatch, feeding the [conversation shell](features/conversation-shell.md)'s workspace chip; no new subscription, no new nav arm (#278, see [codebase notes](codebase/278.md)). #333 added the third route, `settings` (reached via a new `openSettings` nav arm and a new Settings entry button on the Channel List), reusing the pre-existing absolute `back` arm unchanged for the return trip — see the [Settings screen](features/settings-screen.md) doc. Not security-sensitive. Code review PASS, no findings (#140, #333). [#152](codebase/152.md) added a fourth route, `pairServer`, reached from a new "Pair another server" row in Settings; it renders the pre-existing [pairing screen](features/pairing-input-screen.md) with its production `window.pyry` default, and — unlike every prior route — has **two** distinct exit arms rather than reusing `back`: `pairServerCancelled` returns to `settings` (non-destructive, current server stays paired) and `pairServerPaired` goes to `list` (the freshly-paired server's home), since the two exits' destinations only coincidentally match `back`'s today and would diverge under a future stack-aware `back`. Security-sensitive (architect self-review PASS): no new IPC/store/crypto, navigation only, over the already-vetted #54/#55 pairing surface. Code review PASS, no findings (#152). [#347](codebase/347.md) added a fifth route, `archive` — a chrome-only scaffold reached from a new entry button sharing the Channel List's top-right actions cluster with Settings — reusing the pre-existing absolute `back` arm unchanged for the return trip, the same #333-proven economy (adding a route needs no matching new `back` case when `back` never inspects `current`); both tab bodies shipped empty as [#348](codebase/348.md)'s mount point. Not security-sensitive, no new store/IPC/wire. Code review PASS, no findings (#347). [#348](codebase/348.md) then filled both tab bodies with live per-tab archived counts and restore rows, reading the already-live [conversation list store](features/conversation-list-store.md) and dispatching #346's previously-dormant `unarchiveConversation` command on restore, its first caller. See the [Archive screen](features/archive-screen.md) doc. Not security-sensitive. Code review PASS, no findings (#348). [#393](codebase/393.md) added a **third** `open` trigger, sibling to #242's: `useNotificationActivatedNav`, mounted beside `useConversationCreatedNav`, dispatches `open` on a clicked [push notification](features/push-notifications.md#clicking-the-notification-393) (a new, main-local, nullary `notificationActivated` `DaemonEvent`) — no new route or arm needed, since `open` was already absolute; unlike the FAB trigger, no `setActiveConversation` call (the click carries no payload). Not security-sensitive, not UI-visible. Code review PASS, no findings (#393). [#392](codebase/392.md) mounted a fourth, headless daemon-event subscriber alongside these three nav-dispatching ones — `usePushNotify` (see [Push notifications](features/push-notifications.md)) — which dispatches no nav and holds no route; it only sends a command, so `PairedRoute`/`nextPairedRoute`/`PairedShellView` are all untouched. Not security-sensitive, not UI-visible. Code review PASS, no findings (#392).
- [Channel List home screen](features/channel-list.md) — the paired shell's real `list` view (mirror mobile #312), replacing the #140 `PlaceholderList`: a pure render slice over the already-shipped [conversation list store](features/conversation-list-store.md), splitting rows by `is_promoted` into **Channels** above **Chats** (labelled "Recent discussions"
until the desktop-design relabel, [#709](codebase/709.md)), each showing a title (`'Untitled'` fallback for `null`/blank `name`) and a last-activity relative time bucketed from `last_message_ts` (the wire carries no message text, so both Figma row shapes collapse to one title+time row — flagged to @Juhana as needing a daemon+wire change to add previews). Pure `channelListViewModel.ts` (`titleFor`/`partitionByPromotion`/`formatLastActivity`, injected `now`) + a container/pure-view split (`ChannelList`/`ChannelListView`, the #203/#218 pattern) + token-only `channels.css`, adding a new `--text-title-medium-*` token. `.channel-list` uses `height: 100%` rather than the spec's `flex: 1 1 auto` — a documented, code-review-verified deviation, since it mounts directly under `#root` with no flex wrapper. Every row opens the shell's single active conversation (per-row select-and-load needs a transport path that doesn't exist yet). Not security-sensitive, no new transport/store/wire code. Code review PASS, one non-blocking a11y NIT (#141). Each Recent row gained a trailing [Save-as-channel](features/save-as-channel-dialog.md) affordance (absent on saved Channel rows); `Row` was restructured from a single `<button>` into a `.channel-list__row` flex wrapper around two sibling buttons (`.channel-list__row-open` + the optional `.channel-list__save`), since an interactive control can't nest inside a button (#274). A pinned top-right gear-glyph Settings entry button was added as a further sibling of the rows/FAB, present in all three list states (#333). [#347](codebase/347.md) added a second, leading Archive entry button (Material `archive`-box glyph, `aria-label="Archive"`) alongside it; since two independent sticky top-right children would have stacked awkwardly, both buttons now share one sticky flex-row `.channel-list__actions` cluster (Archive leading, Settings trailing — conventional gear-rightmost). See the [Archive screen](features/archive-screen.md) doc. Each saved Channel row gained a trailing [Rename](features/rename-conversation-dialog.md) affordance (`.channel-list__rename`, absent on Recent rows — the symmetric counterpart of Save-as-channel), a second optional sibling on the same `Row` (#360). [#469](codebase/469.md) fixed a latent #366 regression ("Gap B", first documented by [#440](codebase/440.md)/[#452](codebase/452.md)): `renderBody` never filtered `is_archived`, so archived conversations rendered in both this list and the [Archive screen](features/archive-screen.md) at once. New `partitionActive` (the dual of `archiveViewModel.partitionArchived`) drops archived rows before the promotion split; the empty-state guard moved to key off the post-filter partition, so an all-archived store shows "No conversations yet" instead of a blank body. Renderer-only, no store/transport/wire change. No code-review findings (#469). Each present tree
now heads with a **host row** — the file's sixth inline-glyph idiom instance — rendered once per
tree *inside* the same `length > 0` gate that decides the section header, so a zero-row tree still
renders neither; the row repeats in both trees on purpose (operator-confirmed, not deduplicated).
Shipped in #710 showing a client-owned `'Server'` placeholder label, never `serverInfoStore`'s
`serverId` and never the Figma node's own placeholder machine name ("Pyrybox"). Its class names
(`channel-list__host`/`__host-icon`/`__host-label`) share no token or substring with
`channel-list__row`/`__row-open`/`__section-header`, and the label contains neither "Channels"
nor "Chats" — closing by construction the two Playwright strict-mode hazards the ticket exists to
avoid (the unfiltered `.channel-list__row-open` click 28 specs ride at fixture launch, and the two
promote specs' `.channel-list__section-header` + `hasText` locators). Zero `e2e/` files edited; the
40/40 default-tier suite passing unchanged is the proof. Renderer-only, no store/IPC/wire. Code
review PASS, one non-blocking NIT (#710, see [codebase notes](codebase/710.md)).
[#834](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-desktop/issues/834) put the operator's stored label on
the row in place of that placeholder: `HostRow` became an exported pure view (`{ label: string }`
prop) with a thin `HostRowControl` container beside it (the `HostConnectionDots` split, reused for
the reason that comment already states — a zustand singleton seeded before `renderToStaticMarkup`
is invisible to it), and a new exported `hostRowLabel(value: HostLabelValue): string` collapses the
[host-label window store](features/host-label-window-store.md)'s four arms — `loading`,
`not-stored`, `error`, and a `stored` label with no non-whitespace content — onto the renamed
`HOST_ROW_FALLBACK_LABEL = 'Server'`; a `loading` value falls back to that same word rather than a
"Loading…" placeholder, since this is a name slot and a placeholder in it would read as the
machine's name. `<HostLabelData />` (shipped dormant by #833) now mounts in the `ChannelList`
container, giving one read per sidebar mount with a re-read on remount from
`settings`/`archive`/`pairServer` — the mount-site question #833 left open, resolved here rather than
app-level (would predate pairing, never re-run) or in Settings (which the AC forbids visiting
first). `.channel-list__host-label` gained an ellipsize treatment (three of
`.channel-list__workspace-label`'s four declarations, deliberately minus `flex-grow` so
`.channel-list__host-status`'s `margin-left: auto` keeps pinning the connection dots), bounded at
`MAX_HOST_LABEL_LENGTH` (128). A companion fix, landed as its own commit (d6fdc3a) touching
`pairedShell.css` rather than this screen's own files: `.paired-shell__sidebar` was missing the
`min-width: 0` its neighbour pane already carried, so a 128-character `nowrap` label's min-content
width (measured ~1063px) grew the sidebar itself before the label ever got to ellipsize — see
[Paired shell](features/paired-shell.md#the-two-pane-desktop-shell-pairedshellcss-srcmainindexts-670).
Not security-sensitive per se, but security-reviewed as the ticket carried `security-sensitive`:
label reaches the DOM only as an escaped React text child, no `title`/`aria-label`/`id`/log built
from it. Code review / PR #837, no MUST FIX. Below each host
row, rows now group by **workspace** — one group per distinct `cwd` (a workspace *is* a
conversation's cwd, the daemon has no separate concept), each headed by a `WorkspaceRow` one
indent deeper than the host row (Figma `106:3098`); both trees group independently. Two new pure
exports carry it: `workspaceLabelFor` (last non-blank `/`-segment of `cwd`, walked from the end,
`null` only when every segment is blank — `\` deliberately not treated as a separator) and
`groupByWorkspace` (`Map`-accumulated, never a plain object, since integer-like string keys on an
object jump to the front regardless of insertion order — the `Object.entries` trap #706 hit);
unusable `cwd`s collapse into one `''`-keyed fallback group labelled `'Unknown workspace'`, a
sentinel collision-proof by construction rather than by comparing the label against the fallback
constant. `renderBody` wraps each tree's map in a keyed `Fragment` inside the same `length > 0`
gate, so the DOM stays a flat sibling sequence and every `.channel-list__row`'s ancestry is
unchanged. Renderer-only, no store/IPC/wire; the text half of the selector-hazard guard is
fixture-decided here rather than constant-decided, since the label is daemon-derived. Code review
PASS, 3 non-blocking NITs (#703, see [codebase notes](codebase/703.md)). [#704](codebase/704.md)
then turned that `WorkspaceRow` into its group's disclosure control: a real `<button>` carrying
`aria-expanded`, replacing the keyed `Fragment` with a new exported `CollapsibleWorkspaceGroup`
that holds one `useState` boolean per rendered group and — like the `Fragment` it replaces — emits
no element of its own, so the flat sibling sequence and every row locator's ancestry stay
unchanged. Collapse is per tree and per group for free (Channels and Chats are separate sibling
lists, so React gives the same `cwd` two distinct component instances); state is renderer-only and
unpersisted, surviving a conversation switch only because `PairedShell` mounts `ChannelList` at the
same element position on both routes (ADR 0006), and dying with the renderer on app start. Four
attribute sinks the new control invites — `aria-label`, `aria-controls`/`id`, `title`, a log line —
are declined by name, sourced from the daemon-derived label and from `group.key`; the `<div>` →
`<button>` swap needed two inheritance repairs (`font-family`, and `color` for the
`fill="currentColor"` glyph) invisible until tested against the dark scheme. Zero `e2e/` files
edited; the pre-existing 40-spec default tier passed unchanged, plus one new spec. Security-
sensitive gate satisfied (architect self-review + code review both PASS). Code review PASS, one
non-blocking NIT (#704, see [codebase notes](codebase/704.md)). [#718](codebase/718.md) (split from #672; sibling #719 shipped separately, see below) ended each host row with two 6px, label-less connection dots at its trailing edge — host leg first, relay leg second — reusing [#330's shipped `relayLeg`/`daemonLeg`/`ConnectionLeg` mapping](features/conversation-shell-chrome.md#two-dot-relaypyrycode-connection-status-indicator-330) across screens rather than growing a second copy of it, at both the TypeScript level (cross-screen import) and the CSS level (the sidebar dot wears `.conn-dot--up/--in-progress/--down` without the status row's 8px `.conn-dot` base, so the category → colour binding stays a single copy in the renderer). Leg order is host-first, the reverse of the status row's `ConnectionStatusIndicator(relay, daemon)` call — pinned by a test, since both props share one type and a copied call site would swap them silently. Each dot is a `role="img"` span named by the leg's existing client-owned label (no daemon text reaches it); the wrapper carries no group role, since the host row renders twice and #670's two-pane layout shows the labelled status-row indicator at the same time. Zero `e2e/` files touched, 41/41 unchanged. Renderer-only, no store/IPC/wire. Code review PASS, two non-blocking NITs (#718, see [codebase notes](codebase/718.md)). [#719](codebase/719.md) then gave the relay leg's `null` sentinel — "no status has arrived yet" — its own fourth `LegCategory`, `unknown`/`Relay Unknown`, instead of #330's original collapse into `down`/`Relay Offline`: since #718 put these dots on the sidebar from the first frame of every launch, that collapse made a cold start look like an outage. One CSS rule (`.conn-dot--unknown`, reusing `--color-outline`, declared flat beside its three siblings so it reaches both the 8px and 6px dot from a single declaration) and one label string satisfy both surfaces with no code change to either consumer; `daemonLeg` is untouched and never produces the new category. Code review PASS, one non-blocking SHOULD FIX left open (a stale doc comment; see [codebase notes](codebase/719.md)).
- [Welcome screen](features/welcome-screen.md) — desktop's own first-run explanation screen (Figma `103-744`, a horizontal hero unlike every other screen's mobile-frame-stretched layout): the pyrycode mark, title/subtitle/body copy, a primary "I already have pyrycode" pill CTA (an `onPair?` prop, `PairingScreen.tsx:163`'s posture), a secondary "Set up pyrycode first" anchor (`target="_blank"`, opens `https://pyryco.de/setup` via the existing `setWindowOpenHandler`→`shell.openExternal` path, never the in-app window), and a non-interactive footer line. **Shipped dormant in [#657](codebase/657.md)** — nothing mounted it; #658 was later split into #661 (e2e scaffolding) and [#662](codebase/662.md), which made it the [app shell](features/app-shell.md)'s launch destination for every non-paired outcome — the screen is live, no longer dormant, and its `onPair` prop is wired to navigate into pairing. Adds `--color-on-primary`, a `display-large` type tier, and `--space-7/8/16` to `theme/tokens.css`. Split from #612 alongside #658. Security-sensitive (architect + code-review both PASS): no daemon-derived data, no bridge, no new IPC. Code review PASS with one non-blocking SHOULD FIX (footer opacity `.55` a WCAG AA near-miss, kept for frame fidelity) and two NITs (a now-stale "no on-primary token" comment left in `settings.css`/`conversation.css`; an independently-reverified `scaleY(-1)` mark flip, confirmed genuine Figma intent on both the desktop and mobile frames, not a bug). [#662](codebase/662.md) (security-sensitive, architect self-review PASS, code review PASS): relocated the app root here, wiring `onPair` and, on the pairing screen, `onCancel` back to this screen — see the [App shell](features/app-shell.md) entry for the routing-side detail. [#796](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-desktop/issues/796) moved the snowflake mark itself out of this screen's module-private `PyrycodeMark` into a shared `theme/PyryMark({ className, width, height })`, once the [conversation shell](features/conversation-shell-composer.md#composer-status-row-796)'s new composer status row needed the same 12 KB path — one drifted copy being a real risk. This screen's own call site (`<PyryMark className="welcome__mark" width={184} height={208} />`) reproduces the prior markup byte-for-byte, `className` first in attribute order since `WelcomeScreen.test.tsx:33` asserts that literal string, and its `scaleY(-1)` flip stays exactly where it was, on the consuming CSS class rather than inside the shared component.

Adds one `PairedRoute` member (`'settings'`) and one `PairedNav` arm (`{ type: 'openSettings' }`); both `assertNever` guards (`nextPairedRoute`, `PairedShellView`) force the additions. Back reuses the pre-existing absolute `back` arm unchanged — `nextPairedRoute`'s `current` parameter was already unreferenced, so no new reverse-transition logic was needed. Mirrors mobile #390/#398. Not security-sensitive, no new store/IPC/wire (#333, code review PASS, no findings). [#334](codebase/334.md) then filled the section-body: a new `ServerRow.tsx` (pure `ServerRow({serverInfo})` view + store-bound `ServerRowControl`, the #330 view/container split) mounted alongside the previously-dormant [server-info store](features/server-info-store.md)'s `<ServerInfoData />` loader, so opening Settings now fires the one-shot `serverId`/`relayUrl` fetch and renders it (or a `Loading…` placeholder pre-resolve), plus an empty two-dot host slot with deliberately no `aria-label="Connection status"` (avoids colliding with #330's `thread`-view marker). Scaffold child of the #150 → #332 (data path, merged: [#339](codebase/339.md) IPC + [#340](codebase/340.md) store) / #333 (scaffold) / #334 (Server row) split; #151/#152 remain blocked-by #333. Code review PASS, no findings (#333, #334). [#350](codebase/350.md) (a #151 split sibling of #351/#352/#353) then appended a second "About" section reading `Version X.Y.Z` — the running app's `package.json` `version` sourced as a **build-time** `__APP_VERSION__` Vite `define`, deliberately not IPC (static, non-secret, no daemon round-trip, unlike `serverInfo`'s async path above); the `define` is mirrored into both `electron.vite.config.ts` and the separate `vitest.config.ts` (the latter load-bearing — otherwise `npm test` throws `ReferenceError` at transform), and a new ambient `src/renderer/src/version.d.ts` types the global. Inline in `SettingsScreen.tsx`, no new component file (no store/matrix to isolate, unlike `ServerRow`). No `src/main`/`src/preload`/`src/shared/ipc` edit. The build-hash sub-line (Figma 17-109) is out of scope, deferred to a follow-up. Not security-sensitive, no code-review findings (#350). [#351](codebase/351.md) (another #151 split sibling) then inserted a third "Storage" section **between** Connection and About (preserving Figma's relative vertical order): a new `ArchivedCountRow.tsx` (the same `ServerRow`/`ServerRowControl` pure-view/store-bound-container split) reading a new `selectArchivedCount` selector added to the existing [conversation list store](features/conversation-list-store.md) — `null` (list not yet loaded) passes through as `null` (neutral "—" placeholder) rather than collapsing to a spurious "0 archived"; a loaded list resolves to the count of `is_archived === true` rows, rendered uniformly as `` `${n} archived` `` for every `n` (no singular/plural branch — "archived" is a past-participle state, not a countable noun). No new store, no new wire message, no navigation — the count is a pure derived read, kept live for free by the already-shipped `ConversationListData` re-list-on-`conversationUpdated` bridge. Not security-sensitive, no code-review findings (#351). [#152](codebase/152.md), the last open #150-line follow-up, then added a "Pair another server" row directly below the Server row — label + a **kept** trailing chevron (unlike the Server/Storage rows, which both omit theirs, since this row is a genuine forward-nav affordance) — that dispatches `openPairServer` on the [paired shell](features/paired-shell.md), reopening the existing pairing screen in place to switch daemons; confirming overwrites the single stored server record and the transport reconnects automatically (inherited from `pairingHandler`, no transport change here), cancelling is non-destructive. Security-sensitive (architect self-review PASS), code review PASS, no findings (#152). [#404](codebase/404.md) (a #352 split sibling of #403/#405) then inserted a "Defaults for new conversations" section **between** Connection and Storage: a new `DefaultWorkspaceRow.tsx` following the #152 *interactive*-row idiom (native `<button>`, no `aria-label`, kept trailing chevron — unlike the static Server/Storage rows) whose `DefaultWorkspaceRowControl` reads the [default-workspace store](features/default-workspace-store.md) (#403) and, on activation, mounts an in-file `DefaultWorkspacePickerSheet` (the #383 `WorkspacePickerSheet` container analog, minus its conversation coupling) that reuses `WorkspacePickerSheetView` (#383) + `RecentWorkspacesData` (#382) fresh per open; choosing a row calls `setDefaultWorkspace(path)` and closes — no daemon command, since a workspace path is not a secret and this is a client pref, not a live conversation's workspace. No `onCreateFolder` supplied (#398's create-folder dialog is conversation-scoped, out of scope), so that picker entry renders disabled. Not security-sensitive, no new wire/IPC. Code review PASS, no findings (#404). [#409](codebase/409.md) (the write half of the #353 push-toggle split, #408 the data half) then inserted a "Notifications" section **between** Defaults and Storage: a new `PushNotificationRow.tsx` following the static `ServerRow`/`ArchivedCountRow` idiom (pure view + store-bound Control, plus one `onToggle` callback prop) whose `PushNotificationRowControl` reads the [push-notification preference store](features/push-notification-preference-store.md) (#408) and, on toggle, calls `setPushNotificationsEnabled(next)` — no daemon command, no picker, a direct on/off write. The switch is a native `<button role="switch">`, not a clone of the pre-existing `run-config__switch` `<span>`: the keyboard AC needed real Space/Enter activation, which a `<span onClick>` can't give without an added `onKeyDown`, so the CSS clone (`.settings__switch*` in `settings.css`) added a button reset instead. `aria-label` mirrors the visible label via one shared constant, since the switch is a sibling of the label `<p>` (not its parent) and `role="switch"` computes its name from the author. Not security-sensitive, no new store/IPC/wire. No code-review pass required (confirmed not security-sensitive at spec time).
- [Archive screen](features/archive-screen.md) — the paired shell's fifth view, `archive`, reachable via a new Archive entry button sharing the top-right actions cluster with the Settings entry on the [Channel List](features/channel-list.md). Adds one `PairedRoute` member (`'archive'`) and one `PairedNav` arm (`{ type: 'openArchive' }`); both `assertNever` guards force the additions. Back reuses the pre-existing absolute `back` arm unchanged, the same #333-proven economy. Container/pure-view split (`ArchiveScreen`/`ArchiveScreenView`, the #203/#218 pattern); `selectedTab` is screen-local `useState` (ADR 0006). Mirrors mobile's Archive design (Figma 18-2). Not security-sensitive, no new store/IPC/wire (#347, code review PASS, no findings). [#348](codebase/348.md) then filled both tab bodies: a new framework-free `archiveViewModel.ts` (`partitionArchived` — filters to `is_archived` then delegates to the existing `partitionByPromotion`, keyed exactly by the `ArchiveTab` union so a tab's count and body index the same partition slot; `archivedSubtitle` — composes `"Archived " + formatLastActivity(...)` without doubling "ago", collapsing to bare `"Archived"` for a non-parseable timestamp; `tabCountLabel` — `null` count → bare label, else `` `Channels (N)` `` including `(0)`). The container additionally reads `useConversationListStore(selectConversations)` + `Date.now()`; each row (title + "Archived …" subtitle + an icon-only restore control) dispatches the previously-dormant `unarchiveConversation` command (#346) on restore, fire-and-forget — the row's departure and both tab counts' recompute arrive free via the existing `conversation_updated` → re-list bridge, no new store/subscription/wiring. Not security-sensitive, no new store/IPC/wire. Code review PASS, no findings (#348). Split from #153, all three children ([#346](codebase/346.md) transport, #347 scaffold, #348 counts+restore) now shipped.
- [New-discussion FAB](features/new-discussion-fab.md) — the `+` affordance on the Channel List, rendered as a sibling of `renderBody` so it shows in all three list states (mirror mobile #347). A click fires `requestNewConversation` (inline `createConversation` command, `{is_promoted:false,name:null,cwd:null}`, fire-and-forget); new `conversationCreatedBridge.ts` is the twin of `conversationListBridge.ts` for the create/created pair (`translateConversationCreated` soft filter, `subscribeConversationCreated` injected off-handle, `useConversationCreatedNav` React glue subscribing once via a latest-`useRef`). `PairedShell` wires the hook to `dispatch({type:'open'})`, reusing the existing list→thread transition with no new route/nav arm/store slice — navigation is decoupled and event-driven, firing only on a daemon-confirmed `conversationCreated`, never synchronously from the click. CSS pins the FAB bottom-right in every list state by composing `margin-top: auto`/`align-self: flex-end` (short-content case) with `position: sticky; bottom` (overflow case) — sticky alone only offsets during scroll overflow. Two documented token-mapping deviations: `--radius-md` (20px) for Figma's 16px corner, and an inlined M3 two-layer `box-shadow` literal as the codebase's first elevation consumer (deferred `--elevation-*` token until a second consumer exists). Renderer-only, not security-sensitive. Code review PASS (#242). `requestNewConversation` gained a required `defaultCwd` parameter: `cwd` is now the [default-workspace store](features/default-workspace-store.md)'s saved value (or `null`, unchanged daemon-default behavior) instead of a hardcoded `null` (#403, see [codebase notes](codebase/403.md)).
- [Save-as-channel dialog](features/save-as-channel-dialog.md) — Figma 19:24, built across two tickets: a per-row "Save as channel" affordance on Recent (unpromoted) Channel List rows opens a `role="dialog"` modal (mirroring `PermissionModalView`'s overlay+scrim+panel chrome) with a Name field prefilled via `titleFor(row.name)` and a trailing Cancel/Save pair, Save disabled while the name is blank (empty or whitespace-only). #274 shipped the naming half: confirming dispatched the already-shipped [`promoteConversation`](features/conversation-promote.md) command with `cwd: row.cwd`, keeping the discussion in its current workspace; the dialog collects input and dispatches only, never mutating the list itself (the row's Recent→Channels move is [#275](codebase/275.md)'s `conversation_updated` re-request). New `SaveAsChannelDialog.tsx` (pure view + a co-located `requestPromoteConversation` dispatch helper, not a `store/*Bridge.ts` module, since promote's subscription half already lives in `conversationListBridge.ts`); `window.pyry` dereferenced only inside interaction callbacks, dialog absent on first paint, so the SSR smoke stays green. Dialog overlay is `position: fixed; z-index: 2` (not `absolute`) since `.channel-list` is itself a scroll container and the FAB sits at `z-index: 1`; the action row is `justify-end` (both Cancel and Save trail), correcting a spec CSS note that had drifted from Figma node 19:39. Renderer-only, not security-sensitive. Code review PASS (#274). [#288](codebase/288.md) then added the node's remaining location-choice radios + auto-slug preview: a "Move to dedicated channel folder" (default) vs "Keep in scratch" radio group, `location`/`roundTrip` (#397) now injected props on the view, and a new in-file `SaveAsChannelDialog` container cloning [#398](codebase/398.md)'s create→observe→act shape — dedicated dispatches `createRequested` + `createWorkspaceFolder{parent:'~/pyry-workspace/channels', name: slugForChannel(name)}`, then an effect keyed on the daemon's `workspaceFolderCreated{path}` promotes with that **returned path verbatim** (never the previewed string, since promote's `cwd` is `EvalSymlinks`-resolved server-side and rejects a non-existent client-templated path); `workspaceFolderRejected` keeps the dialog open with a generic apostrophe-free line and fires no promote. `requestPromoteConversation` widened to take an explicit `cwd` param instead of reading `row.cwd` internally, so both branches share one helper. No new wire verb or command — a pure consumer of #381/#396/#397. Renderer-only, not security-sensitive. Code review PASS, two non-blocking NITs (missing `aria-live` on the error line, mirroring #398's same pre-existing gap; the documented `overflow-wrap: anywhere` Figma deviation) (#288).
- [Question-batch model](features/question-batch-model.md) — the question vertical's counterpart to [modal-prompt model](features/modal-prompt-model.md): a pure, framework-free `reduceQuestionBatches` reducer over an id-addressed, ordered `QuestionBatch[]` held in `QuestionBatchState`, correlated by the daemon's one-time `questionBatchId` nonce, over a sealed renderer-local `QuestionBatchEvent` union (`shown`/`dismissed`/`reconnected`). Reuses [ADR 0009](decisions/0009-modal-prompt-model.md) rather than minting a sibling ADR — same discipline (camelCase types, `assertNever`-guarded switch, same-reference-on-no-op, ordered array scanned by id, never a keyed container). Three deliberate departures from `ModalPrompt`: two nesting levels (`options` hangs off each `Question`, not the batch); no `id` on `QuestionOption` and no `defaultOptionId` (claude's answer protocol selects by `label`, so the label is the identity, and a clarifying question has no fail-safe deny default); and **no `resolved` id-memory** — that slice exists in the modal model only because the modal client answers optimistically; this vertical predicted the same slice would arrive with its own answer frame, but #921 (below) landed a local, optimistic dismissal (Cancel refusing a batch) without it, so that prediction is withdrawn — the daemon re-asserts a batch only at connect time and both this store and `questionPicksStore` reset on `reconnected` before the reconcile lands, so the failure `resolved`/[#510](codebase/510.md) defends still cannot occur here. A fourth, ticket-settled rule: an empty `questions` array on `shown` installs nothing (same-state-reference no-op, checked *before* the re-delivery match so a live batch is never replaced by an unanswerable one) — a producer bug the transport lets through unchanged since it polices type, not membership. `dismissed` clears on any `outcome`/`source` value without reading either, satisfying the never-read-an-unrecognised-`source`-as-an-answer rule at this layer; `reconnected` clears the whole set so the daemon's connect-time re-send is the sole repopulation truth. `selectBatchFor(conversationId)` is a selector factory answering the first match in insertion order — the reducer does not enforce one batch per conversation, since nothing observed shows the daemon raising two concurrently. Security review PASS: the array-scanned-by-id shape structurally forecloses the family's two named failure modes (keying a tab by `header`, memoising by `label`), and the module makes no logger call anywhere, since `questionBatchId` and the four claude-authored strings must never reach one.
