No React/Zustand/IPC/transport import, and a test pins the module's import set as empty. 32 tests — split from #850 (#898, shipped). **The Zustand container has since shipped**: `questionBatchStore.ts` (`createQuestionBatchStore`/`questionBatchStore`/`useQuestionBatchStore`) wraps `reduceQuestionBatches` with `dispatch` as the sole write path, a near-verbatim clone of [modal store + bridge](features/modal-store-bridge.md)'s `modalStore.ts` DI-factory → singleton → hook → selectors shape; re-exports `selectOutstandingBatches`/`selectBatchFor` rather than redefining them, and mints no third selector. No `observe?` param (matching `createModalStore` — an observer would be the easiest way to leak the `questionBatchId` nonce and the four claude-authored strings to a sink), and the singleton must never be attached to `window`. Write serialisation is by construction: `dispatch` is synchronous with no `await`, so no check-then-act race; the one named-but-unreachable hazard is re-entrancy via zustand's synchronous subscriber notification inside `setState`, foreclosed as designed since #900's bridge dispatches from the preload callback and the panel slices only read. The DI factory is load-bearing, not test sugar — seeding the app singleton is invisible to `renderToStaticMarkup` (the server renderer reads `getServerSnapshot()`, wired to the state captured at store creation), so the still-unbuilt panel slices (#851, #853) need a per-file instance to seed. Headless: no existing store or bridge touched, no consumer yet. Security review PASS, no findings. (#899, shipped). **The `DaemonEvent → QuestionBatchEvent` bridge has since shipped**: `questionBridge.ts` (`translateQuestionEvent`/`subscribeQuestionBatches`/`useQuestionBridge`) is a **fourth** independent subscriber on the `onDaemonEvent` channel — the `announcedModelBridge` posture, owning exactly `questionShown`/`questionDismissed`/`connected` and returning `null` for the other 38 arms via explicit fall-through cases closed by a local `assertNever`, never a catch-all `default` — cloning [modal store + bridge](features/modal-store-bridge.md)'s `modalBridge.ts` shape but diverging where that one only filters: this bridge *rebuilds* each question row, renaming the family's one snake_case field (`multi_select` → `multiSelect`), while `options` passes through by reference at both the wire→bridge and bridge→reducer hops since `WireQuestionOption`/`QuestionOption` are structurally identical with no rename. `connected` maps to a payload-free `reconnected`. The three pre-existing bridges (`daemonEventBridge`/`timelineBridge`/`modalBridge`) keep their permanent `null` arms for both question types unedited. A shape boundary, not a trust boundary — the four claude-authored strings leave it exactly as untrusted as they arrive, and the render slice still owes the escaping. Shipped dormant, mirroring how #223 left `useModalBridge` unmounted for #224; **mounted at App level by #906**, which also gave the store its first reader — see [Conversation shell — modals](features/conversation-shell-modals.md) for the render vertical (frame only; #907/#908/#853 still to come). The AC5 StrictMode double-mount proof needed a listener-**set** fake (per-subscription off handles) rather than `modalBridge.test.ts`'s single-captured-listener fake, since only a set can distinguish "cleanup ran" from "the second mount overwrote the first." Security review PASS, no findings. (#900, shipped). **A `questionPicksStore.ts` has since shipped**, holding what the *operator* has picked so far in an open batch, deliberately separate from the held-batch model above — keyed `questionBatchId → questionIndex → QuestionSelection` (`ReadonlyMap` of `ReadonlyMap`s, the `conversationLastReadStore` precedent, and this family's one departure from ADR 0009's ordered-array-scanned-by-id, since this is a pure lookup with no order of its own). A pick is the option's **position**, never its label — `QuestionOption` carries no `id` by design (#898) — so the store is structurally incapable of holding claude-authored text: a `QuestionSelection` is two numbers, a boolean, and one operator-typed string. Seven arms named for behaviour rather than a `multiSelect` boolean (`optionPicked`/`otherPicked` single-select-replace, `optionToggled`/`otherToggled` multi-select-accumulate, `otherTextChanged`, `dismissed`, `reconnected`); no `shown` arm, since a batch's picks come into being only on the operator's first pick. Fed through the existing bridge with no fifth channel subscription: `subscribeQuestionBatches` gained a required `dispatchPicks` parameter and a new `translateQuestionPickEvent` reshapes the already-translated `QuestionBatchEvent`'s two clearing arms — rebuilt by name rather than `return event`, since `dismissed` is structurally assignable across the two unions and would otherwise silently carry `outcome`/`source` into a store that must never hold them. The picks dispatch runs **first**, before the batch dispatch, since zustand notifies subscribers synchronously inside `setState` and picks-first keeps the observable intermediate state indistinguishable from an untouched batch. Both new exhaustiveness guards throw content-free messages rather than the family's usual `${JSON.stringify(event)}`, since that form would put the nonce and operator-typed text into an `Error` message. Security review PASS, with one accepted gap: a compromised daemon re-delivering `question_shown` for a still-outstanding id *mid-connection* (not covered by `reconnected`) can leave a held position addressing a different option — **resolved by #922's `resolveQuestionAnswers`**, which skips a position that no longer resolves against the currently held options rather than falling back to a neighbour. Landed headless; #912 has since wired the panel to it, making the option rows and the Other field live and proving picks survive a conversation switch. (#911, split from #908/#851, shipped; #912 shipped). **#921 gave this arm its first local caller**: `refuseQuestionBatch` dispatches `dismissed` to both this store and `questionPicksStore`, picks-first, when the operator hits Cancel — see [Question panel — Cancel refuses the batch](features/question-panel-cancel-refusal.md). **#922 gave it a second**: `answerQuestionBatch`, the same shape over the renamed `QuestionResolveDeps` — see [Question panel — Continue answers the batch](features/question-panel-continue-answer.md).
- [Modal-prompt model](features/modal-prompt-model.md) — the renderer-side foundation for permission/trust prompts: an id-addressed ordered `ModalPrompt[]` held in `ModalState`, plus a pure `reduceModal` reducer over a renderer-local `ModalEvent` union (`shown`/`dismissed`), mirroring `threadTimeline`'s shape exactly (sealed union, `assertNever` guard, same-reference-on-no-change discipline). Introduced **alongside** `sessionStore`/`threadTimeline` as a Strangler Fig — nothing cuts over, no consumer imports it. `shown` appends by `modalId` (no re-delivery dedup — that's #195); `dismissed` removes by `modalId`, an unknown/already-dismissed id resolving to a same-state-reference no-op (AC4). `class` is `permission | trust` only — no `destructive` wire class exists. Pure model + reducer only — no store, no React, no wire types, no IPC (#122). The vertical's transport slice has since shipped: wire types (`WireModalClass`/`WireModalSource`/`WireModalOption`/`ModalShownPayload`/`ModalDismissedPayload`), fail-closed decode (two closed-enum checks + a per-option narrower over the ordered array), and the `modalShown`/`modalDismissed` `DaemonEvent` arms field-mirroring this module's `ModalEvent` — module itself untouched, both existing renderer bridges still discard the arms as `null`, real consumer shipped in #223 (#201, see [codebase notes](codebase/201.md)). The interactive render (`PermissionModal.tsx`, read-only inert buttons) shipped in #224. The answer/cancel path (#225) has since split 3-way by transport/render layer: [outbound wire types + fail-closed builders](features/modal-resolution-envelope.md) — `modal_answer{modal_id,option_id,answer_token}` / `modal_cancel{modal_id}`, a structural clone of `requestSnapshotEnvelope.ts`, no consumer yet (#235, see [codebase notes](codebase/235.md)) — followed by main-command wiring that mints `answer_token` (#236) and the renderer buttons that call it (#237). Gained two more `ModalEvent` arms, `rejected`/`rejectionDismissed`, and an `outstanding`-orthogonal `rejections: readonly string[]` slice + `selectRejections` — the surface-rejection render half; `shown`/`dismissed` were changed to spread `state` so they stop silently dropping the new field (#249, see [codebase notes](codebase/249.md)). Reconnect-reconcile ([#415](codebase/415.md)) added a `reconnected` arm clearing `outstanding` on every supervisor (re)handshake. **[#510](codebase/510.md) (security-sensitive, PASS) deliberately reverses #415 AC3**: the `reconnected` arm now clears `resolved` too — it is per-connection memory, not permanent — because the daemon's connect-time reconcile re-sends only still-outstanding modals, so retaining `resolved` across a reset suppressed a genuine re-delivery rather than guarding a real double-show; a prompt answered while the transport was disconnected (the send silently swallowed by a null driver) now re-surfaces instead of decaying into a deny-on-timeout. See [ADR 0009](decisions/0009-modal-prompt-model.md).
- [Modal store + bridge](features/modal-store-bridge.md) — the missing middle of the modal vertical: a Zustand `modalStore` (`createModalStore`/`modalStore`/`useModalStore`) wrapping `reduceModal` with a real `dispatch`, mirroring `createTimelineStore`'s DI-factory shape; plus `translateModalEvent`, a **third** independent `assertNever`-guarded switch over `DaemonEvent` that owns exactly `modalShown`/`modalDismissed` (the two arms both `daemonEventBridge` and `timelineBridge` null) and `useModalBridge`, its StrictMode-safe one-listener binding (dormant, not yet mounted). Unlike every prior bridge pair, the discriminant **tag itself renames** across the boundary (`modalShown`→`type:'shown'`, `modalDismissed`→`type:'dismissed'`) even though field names stay camelCase-identical — the one detail that breaks a blind clone of `timelineBridge`. Purely additive, Strangler Fig, headless — 2 new files, 0 existing touched (#223, see [codebase notes](codebase/223.md)). Gained a seventh no-op case, `toolResult` — [#229](codebase/229.md) added the vertical's last transport arm, and since this file is now a third exhaustive `DaemonEvent` subscriber, a spec written before this file existed undercounted the new arm's touchpoint floor by one; caught by `npm run build`, not `npm test` (esbuild strips the TS exhaustiveness error). `useModalBridge` is now mounted — [#224](codebase/224.md)'s App-level wiring (beside `useDaemonEventBridge`/`useTimelineBridge`) closes the store→UI path, so a live `modalShown`/`modalDismissed` frame now reaches `selectOutstanding` and the new [`PermissionModal`](codebase/224.md) render slice. [#179](codebase/179.md) flipped the `interactive` capability gate — a real `modal_shown` frame now reaches this store in production and the modal is answerable end to end. Gained another no-op case, `sessionTransition` — folds alongside `toolResult`/`conversationCreated`; real consumer is the not-yet-built renderer holder #259 (#254, see [codebase notes](codebase/254.md)). Gained another no-op case, `sessionSettingsUpdated` — folds alongside `sessionTransition`; real consumer is #261 / #256, not yet built (#264, see [codebase notes](codebase/264.md)). Gained a third owned arm, `modalAnswerRejected` — kept as a **distinct** dormant case (not folded into the null group) so ownership reads clearly; returns `null` this slice (no `ModalEvent`, no `reduceModal` arm); producer is a new main-side FIFO correlation window in [daemon connection](features/daemon-connection.md) attributing a content-free daemon `error` to the `modal_id` it answered; render slice is #249 (#248, security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/248.md)). The `modalAnswerRejected` case is now flipped live — a fresh `{ type: 'rejected', modalId }` literal, matching the `modalShown`/`modalDismissed` reconstruction — feeding the new `rejections` slice and a `RejectionSurfaceView` banner stack at the modal host; not security-sensitive (#249, see [codebase notes](codebase/249.md)). Gained another no-op case, `conversationUpdated` — folds alongside `conversationCreated`; real consumer is the list-reflect slice #275, not yet built (#273, see [conversation promote](features/conversation-promote.md)).
- [Modal resolution envelope](features/modal-resolution-envelope.md) — the **outbound** half of the modal vertical: `EnvelopeType` gains `'modal_answer'`/`'modal_cancel'` beside the inbound `'modal_shown'`/`'modal_dismissed'`; `ModalAnswerPayload{modal_id,option_id,answer_token}` (single `option_id`, not the stale daemon-repo ADR-025 `option_ids[]`) / `ModalCancelPayload{modal_id}` (no `conversation_id` — the sole correlation key, ADR 0009) join `types.ts`; two pure fail-closed builders, `buildModalAnswer`/`buildModalCancel` (one new file, `modalResolutionEnvelope.ts` — two halves of one concern, no barrel, MAIN-PROCESS ONLY), each a structural clone of `buildRequestSnapshot` wrapping a caller-supplied payload into a typed `Envelope` → `encodeEnvelope`, MAY throw `WireEncodeError` on over-cap. Zero `EnvelopeType` consumer cascade (no production code exhaustively switches over it). A regression test pins `not.toHaveProperty('conversation_id'/'option_ids')` against the stale ADR-025 shape leaking back in. Base slice of the answer/cancel path, split 3-way from #225 by transport/render layer (#235). **Consumer landed**: [#236](codebase/236.md) wires `daemonConnection.answerModal`/`cancelModal` to call both builders — `answerModal` mints `answer_token` main-side via a new `mintToken` DI seam (default `crypto.randomUUID`) into a **fresh object literal** (never a spread of the caller's payload, the deterministic net against a renderer-smuggled token), routed from two new [command channel](features/command-channel.md) members; `security-sensitive`, security review PASS. #237 (next) wires the renderer buttons that dispatch those commands.
- [Session settings send](features/session-settings-send.md) — the outbound send half of a per-session model/reasoning-effort/YOLO change (pyrycode/pyrycode#844/#845), the write-side counterpart of the [screen snapshot fetch](features/screen-snapshot-fetch.md): `EnvelopeType` gains `'set_session_settings'`, `SetSessionSettingsPayload{session_id,model?,effort?,yolo?}` joins `types.ts`, a new main-only builder (`setSessionSettingsEnvelope.ts`) owns the **omitempty presence contract** — an absent optional means "leave unchanged," a present zero value (`''`/`false`) means "set to this value," enforced by an explicit `!== undefined` check (never truthiness) on a fresh, conditionally-keyed literal that doubles as the anti-smuggling net — and `daemonConnection.setSessionSettings(payload)` is a faithful `requestSnapshot` twin passing the payload straight through. The eighth [command channel](features/command-channel.md) member, `setSessionSettings`, is guarded by the first optional-absent (not nullable-present) boundary check, `isSetSessionSettingsPayload`. `security-sensitive`, architect self-review PASS, code review PASS, no findings. Split from #255 (#263, see [codebase notes](codebase/263.md)). The reply decode arm, [#264](codebase/264.md), shipped next (a `sessionSettingsUpdated{sessionId}` `DaemonEvent`, no-op in every bridge). **The confirmed-round-trip correlation, [#261](codebase/261.md), has since shipped too**: the renderer mints a client-internal `changeId` riding IPC-only alongside the command's `payload`; `daemonConnection` remembers `envelopeId → changeId` in a new `pendingSettings` map after a successful send, matches a reply by `Envelope.in_reply_to` (propagated but not re-decoded from the already-parsed envelope), and emits a fail-closed, correlation-gated `sessionSettingsUpdated{sessionId,changeId}` — a reply matching no pending entry (stale, or a hostile daemon forging a confirmation) is silently ignored; `dial()` clears the map. `security-sensitive`, PASS, no findings. **The rejected-round-trip correlation, [#269](codebase/269.md), has since shipped too**: reusing the exact same `pendingSettings` map + `changeId` key, keyed off `case 'daemon-error':` instead — the daemon's content-free `error` reply (#116) widens with the same propagated `Envelope.in_reply_to`, and a match emits `sessionSettingsRejected{changeId}` while consuming the frame entirely (skipping both the #116 bundle reassembler and the #248 modal-answer FIFO, since a per-request-correlated error can be neither); a miss falls through to those two exactly as before. Also closes the orphan #261 left open: a rejected change's `pendingSettings` entry is now `delete`d on correlation rather than surviving until the next `dial()`. `security-sensitive`, code review PASS, no findings. **The pending→confirm/reject store, [#256](codebase/256.md), has since shipped too** — see [Run configuration write store](features/run-settings-write-store.md), below. **The interactive Run-config controls, [#257](codebase/257.md), have since shipped too** — the live dispatch site, mirroring how #236 shipped ahead of its render consumer; #183's whole interactive Run-configuration write path is now complete end to end.

- [Run configuration write store](features/run-settings-write-store.md) — the renderer's pending-write state machine for a Model/Effort/YOLO change: an **adjacent** dedicated store (not a `runConfigStore` facet, same App-level-always-listening + orthogonal-shape reasoning as [session-id store](features/session-id-store.md)) — a **reducer** (unlike that store's named setters, since this one's three transitions are correlated and each reads prior state, where the sibling's set/clear ([#529](codebase/529.md)) are independent whole-value writes) over the sealed `changeDispatched | settingsConfirmed | settingsRejected` event union: `pending: ReadonlyMap<changeId, SettingsChange>`, sparse `confirmed: {model?; effort?; yolo?}` overrides, and `error: field | null`. `changeDispatched` records the pending change and clears `error`; `settingsConfirmed` commits **the value the client sent** (the daemon reply carries none) and deletes the marker, or no-ops on a non-matching `changeId` (fail-closed); `settingsRejected` deletes the marker (the view rolls back automatically — no explicit rollback mutation exists) and sets `error` to the field, or no-ops on no match. `selectEffectiveSettings(snapshot, state)` composes the display per field: pending overlay (last-write-wins by `Map` insertion order) > confirmed override > `runConfigStore` snapshot base, `??`-on-`undefined` only (empty-string/`false` held verbatim). The bidirectional `runSettingsWriteBridge.ts`: inbound `translateWriteEvent`/`subscribeRunSettingsWrite` fold `sessionSettingsUpdated`/`sessionSettingsRejected` (#261/#269) into the reducer as a **fourth, independent** App-level subscriber on the daemon-event channel (not a change to the three existing exhaustive bridges); outbound `submitSettingsChange` mints one `changeId` (`crypto.randomUUID()`, injectable), **dispatches before sending** (record-before-send), then sends `{setSessionSettings, payload, changeId}` — the shared `changeId` is the correlation invariant. The headless `RunSettingsWriteData(): null` mounts unconditionally in `App.tsx` alongside `ConversationListData`/`SessionIdData`, since a confirm/reject reply can arrive after the Run config sheet closes. Not security-sensitive; no Figma of its own (delivers state only; [#257](codebase/257.md) built the interactive controls that dispatch onto and read it — the render surface lives on that entry, above). Code review PASS with one non-blocking NIT: two same-field outstanding changes resolve by *confirmation* order, not *dispatch* order — benign under the single-client, sequential-daemon-reply model, self-heals on the next snapshot, violates no AC ([#256](codebase/256.md)). **Gained a fourth, uncorrelated `reconnected` arm ([#539](codebase/539.md), `security-sensitive`, split from #509, sibling of [#538](codebase/538.md))**: clears every stranded `pending` entry on the daemon's `connected` (re)handshake edge — main abandons its envelope-id → `changeId` correlation on each re-dial and emits no rejection in its place, so a change in flight during a drop previously stranded forever and, since `pending` beats `confirmed` in `selectEffectiveSettings`, could permanently shadow a later confirmed change on the same field. `confirmed`/`error` are preserved; same-reference no-op when nothing is pending (load-bearing — #257's container selects the whole raw state as its zustand selector). Spreads `state` rather than the initial constant, the mirror image of #538's spread choice for the same underlying reason (see #539's codebase notes). Two accepted residuals, both named rather than engineered around: the outage window itself (the clear fires on reconnect, not on the drop, since `disconnected` is never emitted anywhere in `src/main`), and a single-IPC-hop window between the `connected` emit and its renderer receipt. Code review PASS, 0 blocking, 2 non-blocking NITs.

- [Queue store](features/queue-store.md) — the renderer's held copy of each open conversation's queued-message backlog, keyed by `conversationId`: a dedicated Zustand store (`createQueueStore`/`queueStore`/`useQueueStore`) plus a reactive-only headless bridge (`translateQueueState`/`subscribeQueue`/`QueueData`) that observes the [daemon-event channel](features/daemon-event-channel.md)'s `queueState` arm (#292) and lands each snapshot via a single `setBacklog` copy-on-write setter — field-for-field mirroring [session-id store](features/session-id-store.md)'s DI-factory → singleton → hook → selector shape and [session settings send](features/session-settings-send.md)'s `ReadonlyMap` copy-on-write idiom, but the first store in this codebase keyed by an external id rather than holding one flat value. Keyed (not flat) because the `queueState` arm carries `conversationId` specifically so this store can, and because daemon #878/#879 unicasts one `queue_state` per non-empty conversation on reconnect — several snapshots for different conversations can arrive back-to-back, and a flat "last snapshot" slot would let one clobber another. `queue_state` is **replacement-truth per key**: the latest snapshot for a conversation replaces its held backlog wholesale (removed entries disappear, an empty `queued: []` clears to `[]` without deleting the key), never merged or appended — deliberately **not** folded into the [conversation timeline store](features/conversation-timeline-store.md), since `queue_state` is daemon *state* (SSOT pyrycode #720), not part of claude's turn stream. `selectBacklogFor(conversationId)` is a selector *factory* (this codebase's first) rather than a bare selector, returning a stable `EMPTY_BACKLOG` reference before any snapshot arrives and preserving reference-equality for one key across a write to another; `selectBacklogs` exposes the whole map, added "for #197's reconcile-on-connect pass" but ending up with no production caller once that reset shipped as a wholesale clear instead (see below). `QueueData` mounts as a fourth headless App-level sibling alongside `ConversationListData`/`SessionIdData`/`RunSettingsWriteData`, reactive-only like `SessionIdData` (the daemon pushes `queue_state` unsolicited, so there is no request half). Not security-sensitive (pure renderer state over an already-decoded event); no Figma (headless, no render surface); the queue render slice (#294, shipped, see [conversation shell](features/conversation-shell.md) above) and the drop affordance (#296, shipped, dispatches removal but never mutates this store directly — the row leaves only via the next `queue_state` snapshot) are its consumers. Split from #145 alongside #292 (transport, shipped first) (#293, code review PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/293.md)). Gained a second setter, `resetBacklogs` — clears the whole `backlogs` map on the daemon's `connected` (re)handshake edge (same-reference no-op if already empty), letting the daemon's connect-time `queue_state` re-sends repopulate through the unchanged `setBacklog` path; wired as a leading branch in `subscribeQueue` (widened to a third `resetBacklogs` param) rather than folded into the pure `translateQueueState`, since the queue bridge has no event union to route a `reconnected`-style arm through the way [modal store + bridge](features/modal-store-bridge.md)'s twin ([#415](codebase/415.md)) does. Closes the two reconnect-recovery bugs #196 split off: a conversation that fully drained while disconnected (no re-send, since the daemon only unicasts non-empty backlogs) no longer shows its stale pre-drop backlog, and a message queued while disconnected still appears once its re-sent snapshot lands. Not security-sensitive, no Figma. Code review PASS with two non-blocking NITs (the now-unused `selectBacklogs` export and its stale doc comment, left alone per the don't-touch-adjacent-code rule) (#197, split-child B of #196, see [codebase notes](codebase/197.md)).
- [Background-task roster store](features/background-task-roster-store.md) — the `queueStore`/`queueBridge`
  precedent applied to the [daemon-event channel](features/daemon-event-channel.md)'s `backgroundTaskRoster`
  arm (#566): a dedicated Zustand store (`createBackgroundTaskRosterStore`/`backgroundTaskRosterStore`/
  `useBackgroundTaskRosterStore`) keyed by `conversationId`, plus a reactive-only headless bridge
  (`subscribeBackgroundTaskRoster`/`BackgroundTaskRosterData`, the seventh App-level headless leaf). The one
  deliberate divergence from the `queueStore` precedent: `selectRosterFor` returns `?? null`, not
  `queueStore`'s `?? EMPTY_BACKLOG` — the collapse is correct for `queue_state` but would have silently
  erased the distinction between "no frame has ever arrived" and "observed, nothing alive" (AC5, originally
  AC4), with no compiler error and no failing test unless one was written for it; `null` needs no hoisted
  `EMPTY_*` constant, being a stable reference for free. The `connected` (re)handshake edge clears every
  conversation's held entry wholesale (same-reference no-op if already empty) and is the **sole
  enforcement** of a security requirement: without it, a previous pairing's literal shell-command
  `description` text would survive into a new pairing. Deliberately does not repopulate after a reconnect —
  neither frame is in the daemon's reconcile-on-connect set and this app sends no `last_event_id` (#569,
  open, needs a daemon change). Split from #567 (#573, security-sensitive, PASS, code review PASS with zero
  findings, see [codebase notes](codebase/573.md)); shipped **holding wire `BackgroundTask` rows verbatim
  by reference (snake_case)**, with the two scalar arms (`backgroundTaskStarted`/`backgroundTaskUpdated`)
  dormant, awaiting #574 (open at the time, since split).
  **#576 reshaped the held value from a wire-row array to a new per-task `HeldBackgroundTask` type
  (`{taskId, toolCallId: string | null, taskType, description, truncatedFields}`), keyed within the entry
  by `taskId` in a `ReadonlyMap` built at write time, and joins the `backgroundTaskStarted` arm (#564) onto
  it via a new `setStartedTask` setter and a sibling bridge translator, `translateBackgroundTaskStarted`.**
  Provenance is derived, never stored: a task is started-sourced exactly when `toolCallId !== null`, tested
  `!== null` and never for truthiness since `requireString` admits `''`. A roster keeps a started-sourced
  record unchanged (its label is authoritative and never repeats) and rebuilds every other row fresh, while
  remaining replacement truth for membership regardless of provenance; `setStartedTask` upserts in place,
  preserving `droppedTasks` and roster order. This falsified #573's "holds BY CONSTRUCTION" null-
  preservation claim (a per-row mapping now exists) — the header was rewritten to name the straight
  assignment plus one test per write path as the defence instead. Both predicted "just widen the entry with
  optional `toolCallId`/`patch`" shapes were closed by the spec: the entry is per-conversation (one
  `toolCallId` can't hang off it) and the wire `BackgroundTask` type is pinned tool-call-id-free by a
  shipped test. Still ships **populated and unread** — the panel (#568, open) is still the first reader;
  the remaining scalar arm, `backgroundTaskUpdated`/`patch`, is #577's (split from #574, open). Size S, code
  review PASS with one non-blocking SHOULD FIX (the roster-rebuild branch's negative half — a changed
  roster-sourced row on a second roster — has no dedicated mutation-control test), see [codebase
  notes](codebase/576.md).
  **#577 joins the third and last arm, `backgroundTaskUpdated`: `HeldBackgroundTask` gains
  `latestUpdate: HeldBackgroundTaskUpdate | null`** (`{patch: string; truncatedFields: readonly string[] |
  null}`, required-and-nullable, one nested record so a cut report without a patch is unrepresentable), a
  new `setUpdatedTask` setter that joins on `conversationId`+`taskId` and misses silently (returns state
  unchanged) on either an unknown conversation or an unknown task, and a third sibling bridge translator +
  fifth `subscribeBackgroundTaskRoster` writer. `latestUpdate` is latest-wins, never a history — the
  daemon's per-frame cap (`maxTaskPatch`, 4 KiB) is per frame, not per task. The `setRoster` rebuild branch
  was reshaped to carry `latestUpdate` across **individually** (`held?.latestUpdate ?? null`) rather than
  joining the `toolCallId !== null` provenance predicate that gates keeping a record whole — the ticket's
  central trap: the shipped `HeldBackgroundTask` docstring said any field a roster row can't report must
  join that predicate, and following it literally would freeze a patched roster-sourced task's label
  forever, while leaving the branch unchanged (as #576 shipped it) would drop the patch at the next roster.
  Both compile clean and break no pre-existing test; the mutation-control test this ticket adds
  (`backgroundTaskRosterStore.test.ts:396-420`) discriminates all three ways the branch can go wrong,
  closing the SHOULD FIX #576's review left open. `setStartedTask` also gained the same carry-over — an
  undocumented-in-spec deviation, since a started frame reporting no patch must not erase one recorded
  before it arrives. Finally corrects #573's now-sole-surviving stale prediction (widening the
  per-**conversation** entry with an optional field) — see [#577 codebase notes](codebase/577.md) §
  "Correcting a stale prediction still on disk". Still ships **populated and unread**; #568 remains the
  first reader. Size S, code review PASS with two non-blocking NITs (both comment-only — a spliced-in
  docstring line and one unwrapped multi-arg call site), see [codebase notes](codebase/577.md).
  **#581 is the first reader, shipped:** the background-task panel — split three ways from #568 into
  #581 (this slice) → #582 → #583 — reads `selectRosterFor(conversationId)` and renders exactly
  `description` + `taskType` per held task; `droppedTasks`/`truncatedFields` remain unread until #582
  and `latestUpdate`/`patch` until #583. See [Conversation shell — Background-task
  panel](features/conversation-shell-turn-status.md#background-task-panel-581-cap-and-cut-display-since-582-latest-patch-since-583) and
  [#581 codebase notes](codebase/581.md).
  **#582 shipped, reading both remaining bounds off the same `entry` prop:** a partial-list notice
  (`entry.droppedTasks > 0`, a sibling of #581's three-way branch so it shows under whichever arm the
  list itself takes) and a per-field cut marker matched against **wire** field names
  (`task_type`, not the held `taskType` — the array's contents cross IPC unconverted, only the field key
  is camelCased) rather than the held property names, for the two fields the panel renders
  (`description`, `taskType`). `latestUpdate`/`patch` and its own differently-vocabularied cut report
  remain #583's. No prop, store, bridge or wire change — both seams were already reachable on `entry`.
  Size S, code review PASS with zero findings, see [#582 codebase notes](codebase/582.md).
  **#583 shipped, closing out the split:** the held `latestUpdate` pair now renders as a third row
  element — a never-updated task shows nothing, a recorded empty patch shows a distinct client-owned
  sentence (`patch: ''` is a value, not an absence), and a non-empty patch renders as inert, never-parsed
  escaped text, with its own `.background-task-panel__cut-patch` marker read from **the update's own**
  `truncatedFields` (`task_id`/`patch` vocabulary) and never crossed with the task's own list
  (`task_id`/`task_type`/`description`). No prop, store, bridge or wire change. Size S, code review PASS
  with two non-blocking SHOULD FIX (both test-coverage gaps — the marker-placement decision and an
  escaping-net comment, neither a production defect), see [#583 codebase notes](codebase/583.md).

- [Relay-link store](features/relay-link-store.md) — the renderer's held copy of the relay-**socket** leg's link status, split from #149 alongside #328 (transport, shipped) → #330 (two-dot render, shipped): a dedicated `relayLinkStore` (`RelayLinkStatus | null`, `null` = not-connected-yet sentinel, single unconditional `setRelayLinkStatus` — most-recent-category-wins) plus a reactive-only headless bridge (`translateRelayLink`/`subscribeRelayLink`/`RelayLinkData`) that observes the [daemon-event channel](features/daemon-event-channel.md)'s `relayLinkChanged` arm (#328) — the arm's first real consumer, all three exhaustive bridges keep no-op'ing it. Near-verbatim clone of [session-id store](features/session-id-store.md)'s DI-factory → singleton → hook → selector shape (not `conversationListStore`'s request-gated shape — the arm is an unsolicited push). Deliberately does not model the daemon-session leg, which stays in [session store](features/session-store.md)'s `ConnectionStatus`; #330 combines both at render time (`relayLeg`/`daemonLeg`) and decided **not** to reconcile relay-dot presentation after a fatal session close — the leg is left stale, not cleared, per #328's forward note, and #330 renders that staleness honestly rather than suppressing it. `RelayLinkData` mounts as a sixth headless App-level sibling alongside `ConversationListData`/`SessionIdData`/`QueueData`/`ScreenSnapshotData`. Not security-sensitive; no Figma (headless, no render surface). Shipped dormant (#329, code review PASS, no findings, see [codebase notes](codebase/329.md)); gained its first reader in #330 (see [codebase notes](codebase/330.md)).
- [Screen-snapshot store](features/screen-snapshot-store.md) — **removed.** The renderer's held copy of the daemon's latest rendered-screen `text`, split from #318 (itself split from #147): a dedicated `screenSnapshotStore` (`{text,ts} | null`, `null` = not yet received, single unconditional `setSnapshot` — most-recent-wins, empty `text` held verbatim not dropped) plus a reactive-only headless bridge (`translateScreenSnapshot`/`subscribeScreenSnapshot`/`ScreenSnapshotData`) that observed the [daemon-event channel](features/daemon-event-channel.md)'s `screenSnapshotReceived` arm (#316). Structurally closest to [session-id store](features/session-id-store.md) (#259) — reactive-only, no request half at all. Shipped dormant at #323 (code review PASS, [codebase notes](codebase/323.md)); #324 was its sole reader until [#618](codebase/618.md) removed it, leaving it reader-less; [#619](codebase/619.md) then deleted the store and bridge outright (396 lines across four files). The event and inbound wire types are unaffected — see [screen snapshot fetch](features/screen-snapshot-fetch.md) — but the outbound `requestSnapshot` command and its `RequestSnapshotPayload` wire type are now also gone, removed by [#620](codebase/620.md); **[#621](codebase/621.md) then removed the `screenSnapshotReceived` event itself** (and its sibling `snapshotReceived`), along with the three bridges' no-op arms for both — **[#622](codebase/622.md) took the remaining inbound decode and the `screen_snapshot`/`ScreenSnapshotPayload` wire types out last**, the fifth and final removal slice. [#637](codebase/637.md) closed the series' comment debt: 23 transport-module comments that cited the deleted `requestSnapshot` as their design precedent, re-anchored onto `buildSendMessage`/`sendMessageEnvelope` where the cited property still holds, deleted (not swapped) at the two sites where it didn't — zero behavior change, proved by a comment-stripped residue diff. [#635](codebase/635.md) closed the remaining comment debt from the #618–#622 store/bridge/wire removal series itself (a disjoint site set from #637's #620-series sweep): 18 sites across 9 files re-anchored onto `runConfigStore`/`queueBridge`/`sessionIdBridge`/`QueueData` where a live co-exemplar or property-restatement was available, corrected where a comment asserted a now-false fact about this client (`announcedModelBridge.ts`'s `App.tsx` mount list, `runConfigSnapshot.ts`'s stale live-screen claim), and left two sites byte-identical where the comment described true daemon behaviour or history rather than this client's modeling — zero behavior change, same comment-stripped residue-diff proof.
- [Dequeue message envelope](features/dequeue-message-envelope.md) — the **outbound** queue-drop path, split from #295 (itself split from #145) along the #235/#236 seam after #295 tripped the ≥5-file gate. `EnvelopeType` gains `'dequeue_message'` beside the inbound `'queue_state'`; `DequeueMessagePayload{conversation_id,queued_msg_id}` (both always-present, `queued_msg_id` symmetric with the inbound `QueuedItem.queued_msg_id` — a number, never a string) lands right after `QueueStatePayload`; `buildDequeueMessage` (`src/main/transport/dequeueMessageEnvelope.ts`, MAIN-only) is a byte-for-byte structural clone of `buildRequestSnapshot` (#299, security-sensitive, PASS, [codebase notes](codebase/299.md)). Ungated (no nonce, no answer token; SSOT pyrycode #720) — any paired client may drop a queued message it enqueued itself; the daemon's `msgqueue.Remove` no-ops an unknown id. The command path now ships too: a tenth `RendererCommand` member `dequeueMessage` (payload reused verbatim, no `Omit`-derivative — no token to strip) + `isDequeueMessagePayload` boundary guard (`typeof`-only) in `commands.ts`, and `daemonConnection.dequeueMessage` — the `createConversation` twin, fresh-literal-constructed, sharing the one `nextEnvelopeId` counter (#300, security-sensitive, PASS, [codebase notes](codebase/300.md)). The renderer UI now ships too: the drop affordance (#296, shipped, see [conversation shell](features/conversation-shell.md) above) is a required `onDrop` prop on `QueuedBacklog` bound to a pure `dropQueuedMessage` guarded-send helper (`cancelPrompt` minus the local dispatch — no optimistic removal), so the queue-drop path is now complete end to end (transport #292/#299 → store #293 → render #294 → command #300 → drop #296, [codebase notes](codebase/296.md)).
- [Interrupt envelope](features/interrupt-envelope.md) — the complete stop-a-running-turn chain, split from #146 into 3 slices, all merged. Wire+builder (#305, sec, PASS): `EnvelopeType` gains `'interrupt'`, no payload interface (AC5), `buildInterrupt` clones the module's **bare**-frame builders (not `buildDequeueMessage` as the ticket suggested); `payload:{}` present-and-empty load-bearing (`codec.ts:133`); fire-and-forget, no reply, daemon SSOT pyrycode #707. Command+IPC (#306, sec, PASS): eleventh `RendererCommand` member `interruptCommand()`, `daemonConnection.interrupt()` (the `requestConversations` clone), one `main/index.ts` dispatcher arm, no correlation state. Render (#307, not sec, superseded by #678): originally a standalone stop button, pure `InterruptButton` (null at idle) + in-file `InterruptControl` reading `phase`, gated by exported `isTurnRunning(phase)` — deliberately **broader** than `ThinkingIndicator`'s (`thinking` OR `responding`, not `thinking` only); `sendInterrupt.ts` a `dropQueuedMessage` simplification with no local dispatch (AC3); retracts on the daemon's `turn_state{idle}` alone. **[#678](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-desktop/issues/678)** deleted `InterruptButton`/`InterruptControl` and their CSS wholesale and merged the affordance into the composer's own send button as a second variant, `ComposerSendButton` (never returns null, so exactly one control renders in every state); `isTurnRunning`/`sendInterrupt.ts` unchanged, reused as-is. The Figma gap closed with node `114:3549`: the stop glyph is now `circle-stop-solid-full` exported verbatim at 28×28, replacing the improvised square — colour (`--color-primary`) and container (invisible-at-rest) remain a deliberate, still-open deviation pending a design ruling.
- [Conversation unarchive (transport)](features/conversation-unarchive.md) — the outbound half of the Archive screen split (#153: transport `#346` / scaffold+nav `#347` / counts+restore `#348`), a field-for-field clone of [conversation promote](features/conversation-promote.md) narrowed to one field: `unarchive_conversation{conversation_id}` (single required string, mirroring the daemon's shared `ArchiveConversationPayload` from pyrycode/pyrycode#881, which also serves the symmetric `archive_conversation` verb — now also wired, as of #363, see [conversation archive](features/conversation-archive.md) below). Five mirror sites (wire type, `buildUnarchiveConversation`, the `unarchiveConversation` connection method with its fresh-literal anti-smuggling net, the `unarchiveConversation`/`isUnarchiveConversationPayload` command boundary, the dispatch arm) — no new file beyond the builder, no inbound decode change (the existing `conversation_updated` decoder already tolerates #881's new `is_archived` key, and the eventual restore consumer reads it off the [conversation list store](features/conversation-list-store.md)'s next re-list rather than correlating the daemon's broadcast reply). Shipped dormant — fully tested, no consumer at the time. Security-sensitive (architect self-review PASS), code review PASS, no findings (#346). [#348](codebase/348.md) is now its first (and so far only) caller: the [Archive screen](features/archive-screen.md)'s restore row dispatches it via `requestUnarchiveConversation`, fire-and-forget, on click.
- [Conversation rename (transport)](features/conversation-rename.md) — the outbound half of the Rename dialog split from #154 (transport `#359` / dialog `#360`), a field-for-field clone of [conversation unarchive](features/conversation-unarchive.md) for plumbing and [conversation promote](features/conversation-promote.md) for the guard shape: `rename_conversation{conversation_id, name}` (two required strings, mirroring the daemon's own `RenameConversationPayload` from pyrycode/pyrycode#820 — a deliberate **non-reuse** of `PromoteConversationPayload`, which carries a third required `cwd` a rename neither has nor means). Five mirror sites (wire type, `buildRenameConversation`, the `renameConversation` connection method with its fresh-literal anti-smuggling net, the `renameConversation`/`isRenameConversationPayload` command boundary — cloned from `isPromoteConversationPayload` minus the `cwd` check, checking type not emptiness — the dispatch arm); no inbound decode change, the existing `conversation_updated` decoder (#273) already handles this verb's confirmation reply. Shipped dormant — fully tested, no consumer at the time. Security-sensitive (architect self-review PASS), code review PASS, no findings (#359). Its live caller is the [Rename dialog](features/rename-conversation-dialog.md) (#360, see below).
- [Conversation archive (transport)](features/conversation-archive.md) — the outbound half of the Channel Info sheet split (#155: archive transport `#363` / delete transport `#364` / sheet shell `#365` / archive action `#366` / delete action+confirm `#367` / rename action `#368`), the mirror-image twin of [conversation unarchive](features/conversation-unarchive.md), field-for-field, differing only in which way the durable flag flips: `archive_conversation{conversation_id}` (single required string, a new, **distinct** `ArchiveConversationPayload` type — deliberately not an alias of `UnarchiveConversationPayload` — mirroring the daemon's shared `ArchiveConversationPayload` struct from pyrycode/pyrycode#881, the one struct that serves both verbs). Five mirror sites (wire type, `buildArchiveConversation`, the `archiveConversation` connection method with its fresh-literal anti-smuggling net, the `archiveConversation`/`isArchiveConversationPayload` command boundary cloned from `isUnarchiveConversationPayload`, the dispatch arm) — no new inbound decode change (the existing `conversation_updated` decoder already tolerates `is_archived` flipping to `true`, and the eventual Archive-action consumer is expected to read it off the [conversation list store](features/conversation-list-store.md)'s next re-list rather than correlating the daemon's broadcast reply). Shipped dormant — fully tested, no consumer at the time. Security-sensitive (architect self-review PASS, 9-point findings list), code review PASS, no findings (#363). Its live caller is the Channel Info sheet's Archive action — a second `.channel-info__action` tonal pill reusing #368's just-landed Rename style verbatim (no new CSS), callback-gated on `onArchive?` exactly like Rename, dispatching via a new `requestArchiveConversation` helper (a structural clone of `requestUnarchiveConversation`) then closing the sheet; the archived row leaving the active list rides the existing #275 re-list path, no new reflection code (#366, see [conversation shell](features/conversation-shell-session-and-channel-info.md#channel-info-sheet-365)).
- [Conversation delete (transport)](features/conversation-delete.md) — the outbound half of the Channel Info sheet split's delete slice (#155, above), a field-for-field clone of [conversation archive](features/conversation-archive.md) / [conversation unarchive](features/conversation-unarchive.md) narrowed to the same single required `conversation_id`, mirroring the daemon's **permanent hard-delete** verb from pyrycode/pyrycode#822: `delete_conversation{conversation_id}`, a new, **distinct** `DeleteConversationPayload` type. Five mirror sites (wire type, `buildDeleteConversation`, the `deleteConversation` connection method with its fresh-literal anti-smuggling net, the `deleteConversation`/`isDeleteConversationPayload` command boundary cloned from `isUnarchiveConversationPayload`, the dispatch arm). The one deliberate divergence from the clone: unlike archive/unarchive, the daemon does **not** confirm via the existing `conversation_updated` broadcast — it replies with a brand-new record `conversation_deleted{id}` correlated to the requester by `in_reply_to`, with **no broadcast**, so there is no free re-list reflection; no inbound decode of that record is added here. Shipped dormant — fully tested, no consumer at the time. Security-sensitive (architect self-review PASS), code review PASS after one rework round (the initial PR's builder-only test coverage was missing dedicated blocks for the new boundary guard and connection method — fixed test-only, no production change) (#364). #367 (its intended first caller) has since split 3-way into #375 (this decode) / #376 (list-reflect) / #377 (UI + confirm). Gained the inbound decode: `ConversationDeletedPayload{id}` (single required field, mirroring the reply field-for-field — note the reply field is `id`, distinct from the request's `conversation_id`), a fail-closed `parseConversationDeletedPayload` narrow (isRecord guard + one `requireString`), the `conversation-deleted` `InboundDaemonMessage` variant, and the `conversationDeleted` `DaemonEvent` (flattened to a bare `id`, not the payload object) emitted unconditionally on decode from the same choke point as `conversationUpdated`. Still ships dormant — the three exhaustive renderer bridges no-op it; the list-reflect consumer is #376 (#375, security-sensitive, PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/375.md)). #376 shipped that consumer: [conversation list store](features/conversation-list-store.md)'s refresh-trigger predicate widened (`isConversationUpdated → shouldRefreshList`) to also fire on `conversationDeleted`, so the deleted row leaves every list reader on the next authoritative re-list, no per-reader wiring, no local remove-by-id path (#376, see [codebase notes](codebase/376.md)). #377 shipped the live caller, completing the #155/#367 split: the Channel Info sheet's Delete action — a third `.channel-info__action` pill, error-tinted via a new `--danger` modifier (`box-shadow: inset`-drawn outline, since desktop has no error-container fill token), gated behind a client-invented inline two-step confirm (no Figma node, the #226 posture) rather than dispatching directly; a new `requestDeleteConversation` helper (a `requestArchiveConversation` clone) fires on confirm, `window.pyry` dereferenced only there; the confirm's own open state is screen-local `useState`, the `renameOpen` twin. Code review PASS, one non-blocking NIT (focus drops to `document.body` on the pill→confirm swap) (#377, see [codebase notes](codebase/377.md)). [#652](codebase/652.md) (shipped) added a **second**, independent subscriber to `conversationDeleted`: [Paired shell](features/paired-shell.md)'s new `conversationDeletedBridge` reacts to the same event the list-reflect above already consumes (sends no command, so a delete still fires exactly one re-list) and, if the deleted id names the conversation currently open in the thread, exits it — clearing the timeline, the active-conversation record and the daemon session id, then returning to the Channel List — closing the wiring gap where the thread stayed open, and stayed active, after its own discussion was deleted. PASS, see [codebase notes](codebase/652.md).
- [Conversation workspace change (transport)](features/conversation-workspace-change.md) — the outbound half of the Workspace Picker split from #157 (transport #379 / picker UI #383), a field-for-field clone of [conversation rename](features/conversation-rename.md), the closest structural and guard twin (both carry `conversation_id` plus exactly one other required string): `change_workspace{conversation_id, cwd}`, mirroring the daemon's own `ChangeWorkspacePayload` from pyrycode/pyrycode#823 (field tag `cwd`, not `workspace` — the daemon's flagged reconcile point, matching `PromoteConversationPayload`/`ConversationCreatedPayload`). Five mirror sites (wire type, `buildChangeWorkspace`, the `changeWorkspace` connection method with its fresh-literal anti-smuggling net, the `changeWorkspace`/`isChangeWorkspacePayload` command boundary cloned from `isRenameConversationPayload` with `name` swapped for `cwd`, the dispatch arm); no inbound decode change, the existing `conversation_updated` decoder (#273) already handles this verb's confirmation reply, so list-reflect is free. Shipped dormant — fully tested, no consumer at the time. Security-sensitive (architect self-review PASS, no findings — the review's focus, confirmed clean, was that `cwd` is never resolved into a local filesystem path), code review PASS with no findings (#379). First (and so far only) caller is the [Workspace Picker sheet](features/conversation-shell-workspace-and-run-config.md#workspace-picker-sheet-383) (#383, see [codebase notes](codebase/383.md)).
- [Rename dialog + per-row affordance](features/rename-conversation-dialog.md) — the naming half of Figma 19:14, a token-identical twin of [Save-as-channel](features/save-as-channel-dialog.md) (#274): a per-row "Rename" affordance on saved (promoted) Channel List rows — the exact symmetric counterpart of Save-as-channel, which lives only on Recent rows, so no row carries two trailing buttons — opens a `role="dialog"` modal with a Name field **prefilled** with the row's current title (`titleFor(row.name)`) and a trailing Cancel/Save pair, Save disabled while the name is blank. Confirming dispatches the already-shipped [`renameConversation`](features/conversation-rename.md) command (`conversation_id: row.id, name: name.trim()` — **no `cwd`**, unlike promote); the dialog collects input and dispatches only, never mutating the list itself (the renamed row's new title lands via [#275](codebase/275.md)'s `conversation_updated` re-request — free, no new reflection code). New `RenameConversationDialog.tsx` (pure view + co-located `requestRenameConversation` dispatch helper, the `requestPromoteConversation` twin minus `cwd`, with an explicit `not.toHaveProperty('cwd')` regression test); `ChannelList.tsx` gained a second, fully independent `useState` pair (`renameRow`/`renameName`, not shared with the save-as pair — no mutual-exclusion logic needed since a `position: fixed` overlay covers the row behind it) and `Row` gained a second optional sibling affordance, `.channel-list__rename` (24px Material pencil glyph). `.rename-conversation*`/`.channel-list__rename` CSS cloned token-for-token from `.save-as-channel*`/`.channel-list__save`. Renderer-only, not security-sensitive. Code review PASS, no findings, visual fidelity against Figma 19:14 confirmed (#360).
- [Recent-workspaces store](features/recent-workspaces-store.md) — the renderer half of the `#380 transport → #382 renderer` split (the `#139 → #208` shape): a dedicated Zustand store holding the wire `RecentWorkspace{path,last_used_at}` rows verbatim, `null` = not-yet-loaded distinct from a delivered `[]`, and a **hybrid** headless bridge — the subscription half clones [conversation list store](features/conversation-list-store.md)'s `conversationListBridge` (translate filter, one-listener subscribe, off-handle cleanup, `refreshOnChange` dropped — no broadcast counterpart for this slice), the on-demand one-shot half clones [server-info store](server-info-store.md)'s `serverInfoLoader` lifecycle shape, using `ConversationListData`'s `useRef` dedup (not `serverInfoLoader`'s `active` promise-cancel flag — nothing awaited here). Consumes #380's already-shipped `recentWorkspacesReceived` event + `requestRecentWorkspaces` command; adds no wire/command/event/type plumbing. Shipped **dormant** — no consumer mounted the binding in this ticket; the [Workspace Picker sheet](features/conversation-shell-workspace-and-run-config.md#workspace-picker-sheet-383) (#157's remaining split-child) mounted it as its first real consumer in [#383](codebase/383.md). Renderer-only, not security-sensitive, no Figma (headless). Code review PASS, no findings (#382, see [codebase notes](codebase/382.md)).
- [Create-folder round-trip store](features/new-folder-store.md) — split-child B of #384 (Create-folder dialog), between #396 (rejection transport, merged) and the still-unbuilt #398 (dialog UI): a dedicated `newFolderStore` holding the round-trip status as a discriminated union **nested** under a `roundTrip` field (`idle`/`in-flight`/`created{path}`/`rejected` — nesting, not flat intersection, so a `created → idle` reset can't leak a stale `path` under zustand's shallow merge), plus an **inbound-only** dedicated bridge (`newFolderBridge.ts`, no request half — unlike `recentWorkspacesBridge`, the outbound `createWorkspaceFolder` send belongs to #398) folding [#381](codebase/381.md)'s `workspaceFolderCreated{path}` and [#396](codebase/396.md)'s bare `workspaceFolderRejected` into it. The in-flight gate — event-driven transitions honored only while `status === 'in-flight'`, a gated event returning the same state object so zustand skips the notify — lives in the reducer, not the bridge (which forwards both events unconditionally); because the rejection is bare (no correlation key), this gate is the renderer-side sole guard against a stale/unsolicited reply, companion to #396's main-side `pendingCreateFolders` correlation. Closest structural analog is [run-settings-write-store](features/run-settings-write-store.md), simplified from a `Map<changeId,…>` down to one value since only one create-folder round-trip is ever outstanding. Purely additive (zero edits to existing files), not security-sensitive, no UI (headless). Mount is dialog-scoped (decided) — #398 mounts `NewFolderData`, not `App.tsx`. Code review PASS (#397, see [codebase notes](codebase/397.md)).
- [Default-workspace store](features/default-workspace-store.md) — the data half of the Figma "Default workspace" setting (17:56): a client-owned, renderer-local `string | null` preference persisted via `localStorage` behind an injected `WorkspacePrefStorage` port (the `node`-env vitest runtime has no `window`/`localStorage`, so the store can't call it directly), key `pyry.defaultWorkspace` — the app's first `localStorage` key. Mechanism chosen specifically to stay within S: no new IPC command/event, no preload wiring, no `isRendererCommand` guard — the alternative, a main-process preference file, would have added all three. Clones the `serverInfoStore` DI-factory → singleton → hook → selector shape. Widens [`requestNewConversation`](features/new-discussion-fab.md) with a required `defaultCwd` parameter — `null` reproduces the pre-existing daemon-default behavior unchanged, a saved path rides through as `cwd` verbatim; no wire/`create_conversation` payload change. **Is** the read/write seam consumed by the Settings [Default-workspace row](features/settings-screen.md) (#404, see [codebase notes](codebase/404.md)), needing no further export. Not security-sensitive (a workspace path is not a secret), no UI ships here. Split from #352 (#403, see [codebase notes](codebase/403.md)).
- [Announced-model store](features/announced-model-store.md) — the renderer's held copy of the model claude announced for the running turn, consuming #587's `modelAnnounced` event: a dedicated `announcedModelStore` (`{model,truncated} | null`, `null` = no announcement yet, single unconditional `setAnnouncedModel` — most-recent-wins, **no dedup of a verbatim repeat**, empty `model` held verbatim not dropped) plus a reactive-only headless bridge (`translateModelAnnounced`/`subscribeAnnouncedModel`/`AnnouncedModelData`), a **fourth independent observer** of the daemon event stream — the three exhaustive bridges keep their `modelAnnounced` no-ops permanently. Deliberately **not** a `runConfigStore` facet: lifetime mismatch (App-level always-listening vs. sheet-scoped), and the `model` field name collides and means the *opposite* thing on `snapshotReceived`/`runConfigReceived` (the per-session override). `AnnouncedModelData` mounts as the eighth App-level headless leaf next to `BackgroundTaskRosterData`. Renderer-only, security-sensitive (architect self-review PASS), code review PASS with one NIT (a PR-body prose correction, not a code defect) (#588, see [codebase notes](codebase/588.md)). Rendered by [#560](codebase/560.md)'s `RunningModelSection`, the run-configuration sheet's sixth section — no longer dormant; see [Run configuration store](features/run-config-store.md) § Running model section. Pairing-scoped and, since [#593](codebase/593.md), a member of `clearPairingScopedState`'s shared set (`clearAnnouncedModel`, a second whole-value mutation returning `initialAnnouncedModelState` by reference) rather than the transport's `connected` edge — a reconnect to the same daemon leaves the held announcement accurate, unlike `backgroundTaskRosterStore`'s re-handshake case.
- [Assistant markdown renderer](features/assistant-markdown-renderer.md) — `AssistantMarkdown({text})`, a pure `react-markdown@10` wrapper that turns assistant-reply markdown into React elements while guaranteeing raw HTML is never interpreted (escaped to visible text, never deleted, never parsed) and that image syntax produces no `<img>` element at all, for any src. Link syntax shipped the same deny-everything treatment through [#609](codebase/609.md); [#610](codebase/610.md), described at the end of this entry, later narrowed it to an `http:`/`https:` allowlist. See [ADR 0010](decisions/0010-markdown-renderer-capability-absent-html.md) for the full rationale. Shipped dormant in #608, then wired into [`ConversationScreen`'s `assistantText` render arm](features/conversation-shell.md) by [#609](codebase/609.md): a settled bubble renders through it inside a new `.bubble__markdown` container (Figma `16:43`'s 8px block rhythm, fenced code wrapping via `pre { white-space: pre-wrap }`); the in-progress tail keeps the old plain-text render, and `.bubble--assistant-text`'s `white-space: pre-wrap` became conditional on that same fork rather than being neutralised inside the container. `React.memo` at the render site is declined (named seam, not built). Code review PASS with one non-gating SHOULD FIX (a stale module-header comment in `AssistantMarkdown.tsx`, corrected in documentation rather than by re-touching that file). [#623](codebase/623.md) then gave a fenced code block its chrome: a `pre` override wraps it in a bordered, 12px-radius `.code-block` (surface fill, distinct from the bubble's own container-high fill), with an optional `.code-block__header` naming the fence's language — read from the `language-*` class off the fence's `<code>` child via a new `fenceLanguage` helper, bounded to 20 characters and failing closed to no-header for a languageless fence, an indented (non-fenced) block, or any other unrecognized shape. `code` stays un-overridden (inline code is #624's). [#628](codebase/628.md) then sized a heading: four descendant-selector rules under `.bubble__markdown` give `h1`–`h6` an explicit type quartet (`h1` headline-small, `h2` title-large, `h3`/`h4` title-medium, `h5`/`h6` label-large) in place of the UA stylesheet's `2em..0.67em`/`bold`, chosen by a census of the renderer's own existing title-role usage of each scale step (and the exclusion of `body-large`, whose fourteen consumers are all row-body text). Descendant selectors reach a heading nested in a blockquote/list too, but such a heading isn't a direct child of the container and keeps its UA margin — left for #629, which also owns nested paragraphs/lists. No markup change (CSS-only, enforced by `AssistantMarkdown.test.tsx`/`ConversationScreen.test.tsx`'s attribute-free assertions), not security-sensitive. One new `e2e/assistant-whitespace.spec.ts` test compares a heading's computed type quartet against its own `--text-title-large-*` custom properties, read off the same element, keeping the spec literal-free. **#629 shipped next, closing the other half of #624's split** (#630, inline code, remains): four descendant/child rules under `.bubble__markdown` take over the last two UA-laid-out constructs — `ul`/`ol` trade the UA's 40px `padding-inline-start` for `--space-4`, and `blockquote` trades its 40px `margin-inline` for a `--space-3` indent plus a 4px `--color-outline` leading bar, the neutral role chosen over `--color-outline-variant` by contrast ratio (4.6:1 vs 1.5:1 against the daemon bubble's fill) and never the `--color-error`/`--color-primary` roles the four status chrome affordances own. A reset-then-owl margin pair *establishes* (not merely uncovers) the container's 8px rhythm one level inside either construct, since `.bubble__markdown > *`'s reset is direct-child only and flex `gap` never reaches a block-box child; `li + li` is a deliberate, named side effect that takes a tight list's items from 0px to 8px, restoring the same step a loose list's items would otherwise lose. The list-indent floor (`--space-4`) and the marker-overhang bound (a bullet/1–2-digit marker stays inside the list box or the bubble's fill; a 3-digit marker stays inside the thread's clipped padding box) are both settled by measured box arithmetic, with no size/colour/radius literal beyond the bar's 4px width (this file's existing border-width convention) and three pixel-scanned marker constants the new e2e tests use as a bound. Two new `assistant-whitespace.spec.ts` tests extend the shared `RHYTHM` fixture in place (a blockquote and a loose ordered list, no new turn) rather than adding a sixth reply. Not security-sensitive; CSS-only, same six-assertion mechanical guard as #628. Code review PASS with two non-blocking NITs (a stale fixture comment, a dense line-anchor count in the CSS comment). See [#629 codebase notes](codebase/629.md). **#630 shipped last, closing the #624 split.** One rule under `.bubble__markdown` — `code:not(.code-block__body code)` — gives inline code the `--font-mono` token, a `--color-surface` fill matching `.code-block`'s own, `--radius-xs` and `--space-1` inline padding, with no `font-size` declared so a span takes its container's own scale step (body-medium in prose, #628's heading quartets) in every context — the property that lets one rule cover a paragraph, a heading, a list item, a blockquote and `<em>`/`<strong>` with no per-context override. The exclusion uses the complex-`:not()` form over a child-combinator alternative specifically because it fails safe against a future syntax highlighter wrapping fence content in spans; the two selectors match disjoint element sets by construction, so specificity/source-order between them never matters. No margin (leaves #629's nested rhythm alone), no `padding-block`, no restated `white-space`/`overflow-wrap`/`word-break` (AC4's wrap rides on `.bubble`'s inherited `break-word`), no `box-decoration-break` change. `AssistantMarkdown.tsx` untouched, not security-sensitive. Two new `assistant-whitespace.spec.ts` tests, same shared-fixture-extension idiom as #628/#629; the required manual vacuity check (deleting the `:not()` exclusion and confirming the AC3 test goes red) is reported in the PR body. Code review PASS with two non-blocking NITs (a contrast-ratio precision note on the chip-fill-vs-bubble-fill pairing, an optional-chain consistency nit in the new spec). See [#630 codebase notes](codebase/630.md). **[#610](codebase/610.md) then reopened `AssistantMarkdown.tsx` itself for the first time since #608**, narrowing the deny-first link posture to an allowlist of exactly `http:`/`https:`: a module-local `allowedLinkHref` predicate parses the href with no base argument (rejecting relative/protocol-relative/empty/unparseable hrefs without resolving them, so a dev-server and packaged build always agree), requires an `http:`/`https:` protocol, and separately requires the raw string to carry an authority (closing `http:example.com`, a scheme with no authority that would otherwise base-resolve to a different host in the two builds) — returning the original href string, never the parser's normalised form. An allowed link renders `<a href target="_blank" rel="noreferrer">`; `target="_blank"` is the click mechanism, not decoration, routing the click to the unchanged `setWindowOpenHandler` (`src/main/index.ts:56`), which hands the URL to `shell.openExternal` and denies the in-app window on every path — a plain anchor would be a same-document navigation `will-navigate` cancels, so the link would look correct and silently do nothing. Everything else keeps the pre-#610 render (visible text, no element, no `href`); `mailto:` is denied because the allowlist narrows to match the main-process handler, not the reverse. One new `.bubble__markdown a` rule (the app's first anchor CSS) gives the anchor `--color-primary` plus a load-bearing underline — colour alone against the prose ink measures 1.31:1, under WCAG G183's 3:1 floor. Measured, not assumed: `react-markdown`'s built-in `urlTransform` *blanks* a denied href rather than removing the anchor, so a transform-only implementation would still emit `<a href="">` for several denied schemes — `allowedLinkHref` in the `a` override is the actual enforcement point, and this corrects ADR 0010's original prediction that #610 would "re-enable `urlTransform`." Security-sensitive (architect self-review PASS, one SHOULD-FIX deferred to its own ticket: a DNS-prefetch side channel, blocked on the CSP file being frozen by this ticket's AC5). Code review PASS with one non-blocking SHOULD FIX (the base-independence regex's case-insensitive flag shipped untested) and one NIT. New `e2e/assistant-link-opens-externally.spec.ts` covers the click itself — no server-render tier can — with a positive control before stubbing `shell.openExternal`; verified non-vacuous by mutation (`target="_blank"` removed → click hangs on the cancelled navigation). See [#610 codebase notes](codebase/610.md).
- [Conversation activity store](features/conversation-activity-store.md) — #747, split from #674: a `conversationActivityStore` holding turn-running/stalled/api-retrying/compacting per `conversationId` (not just the open conversation) on a `ReadonlyMap` with copy-on-write and four same-value-guarded named setters, so a future sidebar can draw a dot on a row the user has never opened; `ReadonlyMap` makes `'__proto__'`/`'constructor'`/`''` ordinary keys by construction. [#748](codebase/748.md) shipped the first writer: `conversationActivityBridge.ts`, a second independent daemon-event subscriber that translates the four owned arms (`turnState` fanning out to two writes via reused `isTurnRunning` plus an unconditional stall clear; `apiRetry`/`compacting` clearing only on their own wire falling edge) into per-`conversationId` writes, mounted as the eighth headless `App.tsx` leaf. A background (non-open) conversation's stall can only clear on its next `turnState`, coarser than the open conversation's finer-grained clears, because those arms drop `conversationId` at the emit — bounded not latched, closable only by #675's transport widening. **[#749](codebase/749.md) closed the store out:** `dropConversation` (clone-and-delete on the outer map, `Object.is`-survivor-safe, absent-key guard wakes no subscriber) wired to the bridge's `conversationDeleted` arm as a third, ungated listener alongside the existing gated `PairedShell` one; `clearAllActivity` (size-guarded, shaped after `resetRosters`) wired to the `connected` edge rather than `clearPairingScopedState`, since a same-daemon reconnect must also drop stale liveness — one App-level listener covers both the unpair and pair-another-server transitions. Bridge effects moved from a positional list to a named `ConversationActivityDeps` object (closing #748's forwarded SHOULD FIX), since the growing set of same-shaped setters had become cross-wireable and untestable under the repo's global `environment: 'node'`. #676 (sidebar reader) still unbuilt; the only remaining gap is unbounded growth *between* handshakes, left for #676.
- [Conversation last-read store](features/conversation-last-read-store.md) — #775, split from #677 (child A; siblings #776 persist, #777 stamp-on-open, #778 derive, #779 clear-on-unpair): `conversationLastReadStore` holding a `LastReadMark` (a client-originated count of [thread timeline](features/thread-timeline.md) items seen, not a timestamp — no timestamp exists anywhere the renderer can reach) per `conversationId`, the same `ReadonlyMap` + copy-on-write + selector-factory shape as [conversation activity store](features/conversation-activity-store.md) (#747) and [conversation timeline holder](features/conversation-timeline-holder.md) (#755). One write path, `recordLastRead`, replacing (never accumulating, never `Math.max`) with a `===`-against-raw-`get` create guard so a first record of `0` is not dropped; one read path, `selectLastReadFor`, using `??` (not `||`) so a real `0` mark stays distinct from "never read." The one deliberate divergence from its nearer precedent: no bound, no eviction, no LRU — an entry here is the cheapest in the family (one number, one bounded id string), and persisted size is left for #776 to size a ceiling against once it's measurable. Shipped dormant (#775, two new files, zero edits to existing ones) — no writer, no reader, no clear. Code review PASS, 0 MUST / 1 SHOULD (the header's `(?? -1)` falsy-guard example doesn't reproduce the failure it's named for — the actual hazard shape is `(?? 0)`; merged unfixed, flagged for #776-#778's specs) / 2 NIT (see [#775 codebase notes](codebase/775.md)). **[#776](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-desktop/pull/791) then made the marks survive a restart:** an injected `ConversationLastReadStorage` port (the [default-workspace store](features/default-workspace-store.md) (#403) / [push-notification preference store](features/push-notification-preference-store.md) (#408) shape), defaulting to a real `localStorage`-backed port under a new fixed key, `pyry.conversationLastRead` — the app's third persisted key, ruled to *not* trigger the shared key-namespacing helper both those stores' comments had deferred to "a genuine third case," recorded beside the new key const rather than acted on (no edit to either older file). The map encodes as a JSON array of `[id, mark]` entries, never an object, keeping the untrusted `conversationId` out of any object key space in either direction — `JSON.stringify(map)` alone would silently lose everything, since a `Map`'s entries are not own enumerable properties. `decodeLastReadMarks` rejects an absent, malformed, or non-conforming blob whole and silently (no per-entry salvage), `Number.isInteger` catching the `NaN`/`Infinity`-via-`JSON.stringify` case reachable even through this app's own encoder. The write-through call sits *inside* `recordLastRead`'s updater, *behind* the existing same-value guard rather than ahead of it as the two scalar precedents persist — load-bearing because `appendDelta` grows a streamed reply's tail item in place, so `items.length` doesn't move across most deltas, while #777 stamps the open conversation on every one. Ruled: no retention bound, no prune-on-load, no LRU, even now that the map survives a restart — an entry is cheap (one bounded id, one small integer) and the keyspace is bounded by the operator's own opening of conversations, not by anything the daemon can mint. The `init` state parameter is replaced by the port entirely (two hydration sources would be a smell); the port defaults to the real one so 17 of the 19 existing zero-argument test call sites needed no edit. Security-sensitive (architect self-review PASS). Code review PASS, 0 MUST / 0 SHOULD / 3 NIT (a comment line-wrap, a post-restart-shaped write-guard test left for #777 to add, and a forward note that `recordLastRead` accepts any `number` while the decoder only accepts a non-negative integer — an asymmetry with no writer to reach it yet). **[#777](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-desktop/pull/792) landed the writer** (`conversationLastReadBridge.ts`), stamping the open conversation's mark from its own held `items.length` on activation and on every timeline emission while it stays open. **[#778](features/conversation-unread.md) landed the reader** — see that page. **[#779](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-desktop/pull/798) landed the clear**: a nullary `clearAllLastRead`, built as `recordLastRead` is (guard, persist and return in one expression inside the updater, `size === 0` rather than a reference check since a clean install's `marks` is a fresh `Map` post-#776), wired as `clearPairingScopedState`'s seventh effect and placed **last** — after `clearAllTimelines`, because that clear synchronously re-enters #777's listener and re-mints a persisted `0` for the ended pairing's open conversation, and last overall because it is the one effect with an external side effect (`localStorage`) and therefore the only one that can throw. Persists through `storage.write(empty)`, not a port `clear()` (#776's declined method, honoured not reopened). Security-sensitive (architect self-review PASS after one MUST-FIX round on the ordering), code review PASS, 2 SHOULD (stale line-pin cites in two unrelated files, cite-sweep territory) / 2 NIT. See [paired shell](features/paired-shell.md#the-pairserver-route-152) for the ordering argument and its regression test.
- [Conversation unread predicate](features/conversation-unread.md) — #778, split from #677, unblocked by #777: `isConversationUnread(timeline, lastRead)`, a single framework-free pure function reading [conversation timeline holder](features/conversation-timeline-holder.md)'s `selectTimelineFor` output against [conversation last-read store](features/conversation-last-read-store.md)'s `selectLastReadFor` output — a conversation is unread when its held timeline has strictly more items than its last-read mark. Three branches in a load-bearing order (no timeline held → read; timeline held, no mark → unread; otherwise strict `>`), resolving the collision AC4/AC5 left open in favour of "no timeline held" so the sidebar doesn't light up at launch, and deciding two corners other than by accident: a present-empty slice with no mark reads unread, and a timeline slice recreated after eviction below a stale mark reads read (a missed mark beats a stuck one). Both of its imports are `import type` only — zero runtime dependencies, checkable by grep, so the conversation id never enters the file and the predicate tests with no store, port or singleton in scope. No new store, bridge, IPC arm or render; no existing production file touched. Ten tests, three mutation-checked against deliberately-wrong implementations (swapped branch order, `?? 0` collapse, `>=` for `>`) to confirm each fails at least one test rather than merely relying on "green." Security-sensitive (architect self-review PASS; one accepted SHOULD-FIX, a forged large `localStorage` mark suppressing one conversation's dot, self-healing since `stampLastReadFor` assigns rather than `Math.max`s). [#801](https://github.com/pyrycode/pyrycode-desktop/issues/801) landed the consumer — the #676 split's final ticket — composing the two bound selectors, per row, keyed by the row's own conversation id, in a new `ConversationStatusDotControl` inside [Channel List](features/channel-list.md).
- [Conversation timeline holder](features/conversation-timeline-holder.md) — #755, split from #675: `conversationTimelineStore` holding a whole `TimelineState` (items + phase + all four chrome scalars) per `conversationId`, the [conversation activity store](features/conversation-activity-store.md) shape applied to the timeline payload, so leaving a chat and coming back no longer throws its thread away. Not to be confused with [conversation timeline store](features/conversation-timeline-store.md) (`timelineStore.ts`), the existing flat single-conversation store this one runs alongside unchanged. Two write paths rather than a reducer: `dispatchFor` folds via the existing `reduceTimeline`; `markViewed` stamps activation, distinct from a write, because a background conversation is written constantly and viewed never. Bounded at `MAX_RETAINED_TIMELINES = 10` — the one deliberate divergence from both `backgroundTaskRosterStore` and `conversationActivityStore`, which each refuse a cap on purpose — with least-recently-**viewed** eviction: the map's own iteration order is the eviction order, a fold that creates a key inserts at the head, and only `markViewed` promotes to the tail, so an unbounded burst of unknown ids can displace at most one viewed slice rather than becoming the bound's own attack mechanism. `withNewSliceAtHead`/`withSliceAtTail` rebuild the map (`Map` has no insert-at-head primitive) via `Map` → entries → `Map`, which is in-bounds under the twin's `Record`-materialization ban rather than a violation of it. Shipped dormant (#755, two new files, zero edits to existing ones) — no writer, no clears, no reader. Code review PASS, 0 MUST / 0 SHOULD / 2 NIT (see [#755 codebase notes](codebase/755.md)).
